# Ralph Progress Journal - MOSS live meeting transcription MVP

Append-only. Agents add one entry per iteration; the launcher adds one-line
"[loop <run-id>] ..." markers at run boundaries. Never rewrite or delete
entries — context.md is the place for current state, this file is history.

Entry format (concise, a few lines each):

  ## Iteration <n> - <short focus>
  - Target: <candidate from context.md, or off-list + justification>
  - Files: <paths>
  - Validation: <command> -> <result>
  - Evidence: <artifact/log paths>
  - Learned: <what changed in understanding>
  - Next: <recommended next step>

Started: 2026-07-27 - Branch `ralph/live-meeting-mvp` cut from `main af3ac36` (clean tree). The
loop executes revision 3 of
`/Users/gao/Desktop/AI_Projects/0.AISIGHT_LOOP/moss-transcribe-diarize/docs/live-capture-gap-and-execution-plan-20260727.md`.
Feasibility is settled by measurement, not assumption: MOSS decodes a 7.5 s span in 0.233 s
(RTF 0.031) on the already-resident 4070Ti vLLM engine, so live transcription needs no extra VRAM
and latency is governed by the endpoint span cap alone. Seven defects are confirmed with
file:line anchors (pairing contract, no pinning, destructive frame drain, 900 s viewer expiry,
sequential blocking POSTs, mismatched keychain access group, no client-side 16 kHz conversion);
two of them are already fixed on `acl/IDEA-044--A-034@67a27b8` and are to be grafted rather than
rewritten. The server runs `163e969` with live disabled and 84 unpushed commits behind local
`main`; the MacBook has no installed app and its checkout points at OpenMOSS upstream. Mac code
signing is solved by a scripted self-signed identity in a dedicated keychain (proven over SSH,
designated requirement stable across rebuilds); the login keychain is locked to SSH sessions, so
neither the existing `LiveTranscribe Local Dev` identity nor the Keychain secret store can be used
non-interactively. The only irreducible human step is two TCC clicks on m4mbp — park and report
there. This loop is the single writer on this repo; the governed aisight-coding-loop stays halted.

## Iteration 1 - graft A-034 pairing/pinning/persistence/tracer
- Target: Phase A candidate A1 (graft `acl/IDEA-044--A-034@67a27b8`, do not rewrite)

  plus `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: `git merge --no-ff acl/IDEA-044--A-034` — A-034 branches from the same `af3ac36` and
  touches paths disjoint from the Ralph scripts, so the graft is conflict-free and preserves the
  original ten commits verbatim (no cherry-pick rewrite, no reimplementation)
- Validation:
  - `swift build --product mtd-capture` -> OK; `--product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **81 passed, 0 failures** (was 67)
  - `python3 -m pytest -q --ignore=tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py` -> **454 passed, 2 skipped,
    346 subtests** — identical to the pre-graft baseline, so no regression
  - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q` -> **1 passed, 1 failed**:
    `TimeoutExpired` on `mtd-capture start --label tracer` after 8 s. Everything the tracer
    asserts before that point passes on the real built products: private-address TLS server,
    real same-user UDS, two-step `/pairings` -> `/sessions` pairing, full-certificate pin
    persisted and re-pinned, device-ID stability across an app restart, and zero secret leakage
    into CLI output
- Evidence: `sample` of the live `MOSSCaptureApp` product during the hang gives the exact stack —
  `ControlCommandDispatcher.dispatch` -> `CaptureController.start` -> `NativeDualCaptureSource`
  -> `MicrophoneCapture.start` (`MicrophoneCapture.swift:65`) ->
  `AVAudioEngineMicrophoneDriver.currentInputDeviceID` (`:239`) -> `-[AVAudioEngine inputNode]`
  -> `AVAudioEngineImpl::UpdateInputNode` -> `_dispatch_sync_f_slow` -> `kevent_id`, blocked
  indefinitely. Reproduced outside pytest with a hand-built store/socket, then cleaned up.
- Learned: (1) the graft is a clean no-op for every existing test and adds 14 Swift tests plus the
  real-process tracer. (2) New defect 9, recorded in context.md: `MicrophoneCapture.start` throws
  only on `.denied`; `.undetermined` falls through to `engine.inputNode`, which blocks forever in
  a non-GUI process because nothing calls `AVCaptureDevice.requestAccess` and no TCC prompt can be
  answered. SwiftPM ad-hoc signing gives every rebuild a new cdhash, so `.undetermined` is the
  *normal* state on this host, not an edge case — and because the app's UDS loop is single
  threaded, the hang also makes `status` and `stop` unanswerable. This is precisely the gap A3
  exists to close, and it is now the only failing test on the branch.
- Blocker: none. The A4 checkpoint cannot be attempted until defect 9 is fixed (zero Darwin skips
  is not the issue; a hang is).
- Next: A3 — explicit per-lane permission coordinator (request access, non-blocking pending state,
  typed denied-lane failure), then A2 app-owned handoff, then the A4 eleven-command checkpoint.

## Iteration 2 - explicit per-lane permission coordinator (A3)
- Target: Phase A candidate A3 — close defect 9 with generation-fenced pending/granted/denied
  per-lane permission transitions and a typed denied-lane failure
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/{MicrophoneCapture,SystemAudioTap,
  NativeDualCaptureSource}.swift`; `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/MOSSCaptureCoreTests/
  CaptureControllerTests.swift`; `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: locality-constrained by design. `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py` fixes
  the registered thirteen paths, so the coordinator could not be a new file: it lives in
  `NativeDualCaptureSource.swift` as `NativeLanePermissionCoordinator`, the explicit microphone
  transition in `MicrophoneCapture.swift`, and the system-lane rule in `SystemAudioTap.swift` as
  `SystemAudioPermission`. Behaviour: `MicrophoneCapture.start` now requires `.granted` before it
  touches `AVAudioEngine.inputNode`; an `.undetermined` lane gets one
  `AVCaptureDevice.requestAccess(for: .audio)` and stays `pending`; the source runs when at least
  one lane is capturing and a pending lane joins later on a grant; when nothing is capturing,
  `start` retires the permission generation and throws the pending lane's typed
  `permissionDenied`; `stop` retires before teardown; a duplicate `start` adopts the running
  generation and never prompts twice.
- Validation:
  - `swift build --product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK; `--product mtd-capture` -> OK
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **92 passed, 0 failures** (was 81)
  - `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q
    -p no:cacheprovider` -> **2 passed in 9.22 s** (was 1 passed / 1 failed on an 8 s
    `TimeoutExpired`). The real built app now answers `start`, `stop` and `status` on an ungranted
    host; `start` takes the tracer's typed-failure branch, so no lane is ever falsely reported as
    recording
  - `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **456 passed,
    2 skipped, 346 subtests** (454+2, no regression)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **5/10** (was 1/10); all
    five permission checks green
  - `bash scripts/ralph-afk/validate-phase-a-locality.sh` -> PASS; `git diff --check` -> clean
- Evidence: discriminator output above; new Swift nodes
  `testDuplicateStartDoesNotRequestPermissionsTwice`,
  `testStopDuringPermissionPromptCancelsGeneration`,
  `testLatePermissionCompletionAfterStopIsNoop`,
  `testStartWithNoLaneRecordingReportsThePendingPermissionAndDisownsIt`,
  `testUndeterminedMicrophonePromptsOnceAndJoinsRunningCaptureOnGrant`,
  `testUndeterminedMicrophoneDenialFailsOnlyThatLane`,
  `test{System,Microphone}GrantedWith{Microphone,System}DeniedKeepsLanePermissionsIndependent`,
  `testMicrophoneCaptureUndeterminedPermissionNeverTouchesInputNode`,
  `testSystemAudioPermissionIsResolvedByTheUserInitiatedRecordingStart`,
  `testLanePermissionCoordinatorKeepsLaneStateAndFencesRetiredAnswers`
- Learned: (1) the attempt-2 red control is the real design spec, not just a gate — it fixes both
  the file locality and the type names (`PermissionCoordinator` in the dual source,
  `SystemAudioPermission`/`requestSystemAudio` in the tap, `requestAccess`+`.audio` in the
  microphone), so read it before designing any Phase-A change. (2) Retiring the *health*
  generation on a failed `start` destroys the per-lane failure evidence `status` must report;
  only the permission generation may be retired there. Caught by
  `testNativeDualCaptureSourceUnwindsBothLanesWhenEveryStartFails`. (3) `CaptureLane` is a public
  enum, so Swift does not infer `Sendable`; carrying a lane into an asynchronous permission answer
  needs the explicit conformance.
- Blocker: none. Known open risk recorded in context.md: `AudioHardwareCreateProcessTap` is still
  called on the control thread. It returns promptly on MacStudio (iteration-1 `sample` located the
  hang in the microphone path), but M36 is only fully answered once m4mbp shows it also returns
  while its own TCC prompt is on screen.
- Next: A2 — move `handoff` into the app dispatcher and strip view-token/pasteboard authority from
  the CLI (discriminator checks 1-4), then the tracer evidence for check 10, then A4.

## Iteration 3 - app-owned UDS handoff (A2)
- Target: Phase A candidate A2 — move view authority and the pasteboard write out of the CLI and
  into the app dispatcher (discriminator checks 1-4)
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/{CaptureSecurity,CaptureCommandLine}.swift`,
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/{MOSSCaptureApp,MTDCaptureCLI}/main.swift`,
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/{MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift,
  MTDCaptureCLITests/MTDCaptureCLITests.swift}`; `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: `CapturePortalHandoffConfirmation` / `CapturePortalHandoffAdapter` /
  `PasteboardCapturePortalHandoff` moved from `CaptureCommandLine.swift` to `CaptureSecurity.swift`
  (a registered path, and where the dispatcher that now owns them lives); the confirmation stopped
  being its own Codable wire type and became a plain value the dispatcher maps onto a new
  `ControlChannelResponse.viewAuthority` field, so the CLI relays one response type for every
  command. `ControlCommandDispatcher` gained an optional `portalHandoff` and `case "handoff"`;
  `MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift` is the only injector. The CLI's two-round-trip
  `status`-then-local-handoff path is gone: it now sends one
  `ControlChannelRequest(command: "handoff")`. `CaptureCLIError` lost
  `portalHandoffUnavailable`/`pasteboardUnavailable`; those are now `CaptureSecurityError` cases,
  so `sanitizedControlError` names them without leaking anything.
- Validation:
  - `swift build --product mtd-capture` -> OK; `--product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **95 passed, 0 failures** (was 92)
  - `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q
    -p no:cacheprovider` -> **2 passed in 8.48 s**. This is the real evidence: the tracer runs the
    *built* CLI's `handoff` against the *built* app over real same-user UDS, asserts the exact body
    `{"ok": true, "sessionID": ..., "portalURL": .../live, "viewAuthority": "copied-to-pasteboard"}`,
    asserts no secret appears in CLI output, and `_assert_named_pasteboard_matches_store` still
    matches — the named pasteboard is now written by the app process, not the CLI
  - `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **456 passed,
    2 skipped, 346 subtests** (unchanged, no regression)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **9/10** (was 5/10);
    checks 1-4 now PASS
  - `bash scripts/ralph-afk/validate-phase-a-locality.sh` -> PASS; `git diff --check` -> clean
- Evidence: new Swift nodes
  `testAppDispatcherOwnsHandoffAndAnswersWithNonSecretViewAuthorityStatus`,
  `testHandoffFailsTypedWhenAuthorityIsMissingPasteboardFailsOrNoAdapterIsInjected`,
  `testOnlyTheAppCompositionRootHoldsViewAuthorityAndPasteboardAccess`,
  `testCLIHandoffIsOneUDSRequestAndRelaysOnlyTheAppsNonSecretConfirmation` (real UDS server whose
  handler is a real `ControlCommandDispatcher`), plus inverted source assertions in
  `testShimCommandsStayControlOnlyAndAudioFrameworkFree`
- Learned: (1) the missing-authority case must fail *before* the pasteboard is touched, otherwise
  a half-paired app could clear the user's general pasteboard on every failed `handoff`; asserted
  by `copyAttempts == 0`. (2) The tracer's exact-dict handoff assertion is what forced the
  confirmation onto `ControlChannelResponse` rather than a second wire type — `JSONEncoder` omits
  nil optionals, so the added `viewAuthority` field is invisible to `pair`/`start`/`stop`/`status`
  and every existing response assertion still holds. (3) Check 10's only missing literal is
  `MOSSCapture.app`: the tracer already asserts `CFBundleIdentifier`, `sha256` and `codesign`, but
  against a throwaway per-test `MOSSCaptureTracer.app` under `tmp_path`
  (`tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py:408-427`). Check 10 is a real design demand — one immutable
  first-install lab bundle at a fixed path with reused evidence — not a token to sprinkle in.
- Blocker: none.
- Next: A4 step (a) — rebuild the tracer around the fixed lab bundle
  `macos/MOSSCapture/.build/idea044-lab/MOSSCapture.app` with first-install `sha256`/`codesign -dr -`
  evidence captured once and re-asserted unchanged, keeping the M38 JUnit node contract; then A4
  step (b), the eleven registered commands.

## Iteration 4 - immutable IDEA-044 lab bundle, A4 checkpoint green
- Target: Phase A candidate A4, both steps. (a) replace the throwaway per-test
  `MOSSCaptureTracer.app` with one immutable first-install bundle at the fixed lab path
  (attempt-2 discriminator check 10 / mutation row M37); (b) run the eleven registered IDEA-044
  attempt-2 commands as the compatibility checkpoint.
- Files: `tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py`; `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: the tracer's only fixed path is now
  `macos/MOSSCapture/.build/idea044-lab/MOSSCapture.app`, held under an exclusive `flock` on a
  sibling `install.lock` for the whole pytest process (acquired once, released on exit, bounded
  180 s wait then a named failure). `_install_or_reuse_lab_bundle` copies the built
  `MOSSCaptureApp`, writes the product `Info.plist` verbatim — identifier
  `com.alphasight.moss.capture` plus both `NSAudioCaptureUsageDescription` and
  `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`, asserted non-empty at install — ad-hoc signs the bundle, then
  records `first-install-evidence.json` (`schema: idea044-lab-bundle-evidence.v1`, 0600): the
  per-file inventory hashes, `executable_sha256`, `bundle_sha256`, the `codesign -d -r -`
  designated requirement, and provenance `built_product {macho_uuid, sha256}`. Reinstall happens
  only when the built product's Mach-O UUID or sha256 moves. Every later node calls
  `_assert_lab_bundle_unchanged`, which re-observes the bundle from disk and demands byte-equality
  with the recorded evidence plus `codesign --verify --strict`. The lab node additionally re-enters
  the install path and asserts the evidence file's own inode, mtime_ns and bytes are unmoved — the
  direct answer to "rebuilding at the same path and bundle ID is not continuity proof". Certificate,
  server, port, UDS, secret store and artifacts stay per-test temporary; `_make_temp_app_bundle` is
  deleted. `_swift_bin_dir()` is memoized so the three call sites do not re-shell `swift build`.
- Validation (all eleven registered attempt-2 commands, verbatim, plus the locality script):
  1. `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10 true** (was 9/10;
     check 10 "real tracer proves app-owned handoff and one immutable first-install lab build")
  2. `spikes/idea-044-real-uds-tracer/repro.py` -> **16/16 true**; check 13 reports
     `platform=darwin collected=3 passed=3 skipped=0 failures=0 errors=0`
  3. `swift build --product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK
  4. `swift build --product mtd-capture` -> OK
  5. `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **95 passed, 0 failures** (unchanged)
  6. `pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **3 passed in 6.55 s**
  7. `pytest tests/test_live_{auth,api,integration,portal,session_v2}.py` -> **59 passed,
     333 subtests**
  8. `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **457 passed, 2 skipped, 346 subtests** (+1 node)
  9. `git diff --exit-code af3ac366 -- <9 live server modules> ops README.md README_zh.md
     LOCAL_DEPLOYMENT.md pyproject.toml` -> exit 0, server fence clean
  10. `git diff --check` -> clean
  11. `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`
  plus `bash scripts/ralph-afk/validate-phase-a-locality.sh` -> `Phase A locality: PASS`
- Evidence:
  - New node `test_lab_app_bundle_is_one_immutable_first_install_reused_across_nodes`.
  - Cross-run reuse proven directly: `stat -f '%i %m %z'` on
    `macos/MOSSCapture/.build/idea044-lab/first-install-evidence.json` is
    `196780291 1785200680 1264` both before and after a second full tracer run — same inode, same
    mtime, same size, so the second run reused rather than reinstalled.
  - Recorded first-install evidence: `bundle_identifier com.alphasight.moss.capture`;
    `designated_requirement designated => cdhash H"dfd1e35058528d81bf8f265329fbf3becccf6f5c"`;
    `executable_sha256 eb236e4f...18698e25`; `bundle_sha256 e59218e1...1b2db05e`;
    `built_product.macho_uuid 2C2EEEB6-DFEA-33A7-A680-52F1F663481D`.
  - Mutation rehearsal for M37: `PlistBuddy -c 'Set :CFBundleVersion 0.1.1'` on the installed
    `Contents/Info.plist` -> **2 failed** (both lab-using nodes) at
    `_assert_lab_bundle_unchanged`; restoring the plist -> **3 passed**. A one-key bundle change
    is caught, so the immutability assertion is not vacuous.
- A4 CHECKPOINT SHA: **1ede498dde1ec16ae0b3f39922a3987883daeaaa** (`ralph: iteration 4 -
  immutable IDEA-044 lab bundle`). Deliberately **not merged and not pushed** — the one keeper
  merge stays at C4. This unblocks Phase B.
- Learned: (1) an ad-hoc signature has no explicit designated requirement, so `codesign -d -r -`
  emits it commented (`# designated => cdhash H"…"`) on stdout while `Executable=` goes to stderr;
  the parser must strip the `#`. The cdhash form is actually the stronger continuity evidence —
  it pins the exact immutable build. (2) The bundle's signed inventory is exactly
  `Contents/Info.plist`, `Contents/MacOS/MOSSCaptureApp`,
  `Contents/_CodeSignature/CodeResources`; asserting the exact set catches a stray file added to
  the fixed path. (3) Moving to the *production* bundle identifier is a strict improvement over
  the old per-run random one: a random id made every run a brand-new app to TCC, so no grant
  could ever persist. Fixed path + fixed identifier + ad-hoc signing gives a stable cdhash, which
  is the only way E3's real grant can survive into later tracer runs.
- Blocker: none for A4. Standing gap, unchanged: M38's *granted* dual-lane tracer node cannot run
  on MacStudio because no TCC grant exists for the lab cdhash; the tracer takes its typed
  `permissionDenied` branch and the granted branch is only reachable after the E3 human step
  (System Settings -> Privacy & Security -> Microphone, and -> System Audio Recording, toggled on
  for MOSSCapture on m4mbp). The registered A4 gate does not require it, so this is recorded
  evidence residue, not a gate failure.
- Next: Phase B is now open. Start with B1 — make `FileCaptureSecretStore` the default at
  `~/Library/Application Support/MOSSCapture/` (0700 dir, 0600 files, atomic replace, same path
  for app and CLI), remove the mismatched Keychain access group, and replace the lab-default
  source assertions with behavioral permission/secrecy tests. Do not edit the historical
  control-plane discriminator to keep it green; B1 deliberately supersedes its
  `KeychainCaptureSecretStore()`-is-default expectation (checks 09 and 15).

## Iteration 5 - production file secret store is the default (B1)
- Target: Phase B candidate B1. Make `FileCaptureSecretStore` the default for both products at
  `~/Library/Application Support/MOSSCapture/secrets.json` (0700 dir, 0600 file, atomic
  replacement, one path shared by app and CLI) and strip the mismatched Keychain access group.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/CaptureSecurity.swift`;
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/{MOSSCaptureApp,MTDCaptureCLI}/main.swift`;
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/{MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift,
  MTDCaptureCLITests/MTDCaptureCLITests.swift}`; `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: `CaptureSecretStoreSelection` gained `defaultPath(homeDirectory:)` and lost the
  `keychainDefault:` autoclosure; `makeDefault` now always returns a `FileCaptureSecretStore`,
  taking a non-empty `MOSS_CAPTURE_SECRET_STORE_PATH` as an override. Both composition roots call
  `CaptureSecretStoreSelection.makeDefault()` with no arguments, so the env-key literal and the
  path rule have exactly one owner and the two products cannot drift apart. `KeychainCaptureSecretStore`
  keeps its `SecItem*` implementation but lost `accessGroup` entirely (a shared group needs a Team
  ID a locally signed identity does not have) and is now documented as dormant. `FileCaptureSecretStore`
  gained a `path` accessor and a `prepareDirectory()` that creates the directory 0700, tightens a
  widened one via `chmod`, and rejects a non-directory or foreign-owned one. The write path is
  now `open(O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_CLOEXEC, 0600)` + `fchmod` + full write loop + `fsync` +
  `rename(2)` onto the live path, replacing `Data.write(.atomic)`-then-`chmod`, which left a
  window where the live file was not yet 0600. `validateFile` switched `stat` -> `lstat` and now
  demands `S_IFREG`, so a symlink planted at the secrets path is rejected instead of followed.

  the old design guaranteed it only by two matching string literals in two files, which is

## Iteration 6 - retained-until-ACK outbox (B2)
- Target: Phase B candidate B2 / plan D5. Kill defect 3 (frame loss on any transient send
  failure): retain 15 s of audio per lane keyed by `(lane, sequence)`, retry the identical frame on
  timeout/429/ambiguous answers, release only on an acknowledgement, and surface a typed degraded
  state on overflow.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/CaptureOutbox.swift` (new);
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/{CaptureController,CaptureSecurity}.swift`;
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift`;
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: `CaptureFrameOutbox` sits between `source.pendingFrames()` and `transport.publish` and
  owns **wire identity** — it stamps the `(lane, sequence)` a frame keeps for every attempt. That is
  what makes overflow survivable: the outbox **refuses** new audio when a lane is full instead of
  evicting a retained frame, and because a refused frame burns no sequence number the admitted
  stream stays gapless, so the lane keeps publishing instead of being rejected forever as out of
  order (`live_ingest.py:_check_epoch`/sequence check would 409 every later frame). Capacity is
  measured in audio seconds from each frame's own `sampleCount / sampleRate`, not in frames, so
  B3's frame-size/rate change needs no edit here. Loss is reported three ways: `refusedFrames`, a
  sticky `degradation` (`overflowedLaneRetention` / `undeliverableFrame`, never cleared by later
  success), and `discontinuity = true` on the next frame that lane admits. `reset()` runs on every
  `start` because a server session numbers each lane from zero. `publishPendingFrames` now admits
  everything first, then flushes in global admission order, stalling only the failing lane;
  `CaptureFrameRetryPolicy` is the one classifier (0/408 ambiguous, 429 backpressure, 5xx
  serverUnavailable, transient `URLError` ambiguous; 4xx / missing bearer / missing pin /
  `secureConnectionFailed` / `cancelled` not retryable). Depth and degradation reach the operator
  through `CaptureStatus.outbox` and `ControlChannelResponse.outboxRetainedFrames` /
  `.outboxDegradation`; the `moss-live-helper-health.v1` heartbeat wire was left alone.
  Two adjacent defects surfaced while writing the ambiguous-answer node and were closed with it:
  (a) a publish throw inside `start` failed the start while `state.beginStart` had already set
  `running = true` and no pump task existed — a zombie capture that could never publish or be
  restarted; a retryable failure is now a degraded start (pump scheduled, audio retained) and an
  unretryable one stops the source and rolls the state back; (b) a raw `URLError` — exactly what
  the pinned `URLSession` throws during a real outage — typed as `CapturePumpFailure.unexpected`
  instead of `.transportUnavailable`.
- Validation:
  - `swift build --product mtd-capture` -> OK; `--product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **106 passed, 0 failures** (was 98: +8 nodes)
  - `swift test --filter 'Outbox|Ambiguous|RetryPolicy|Stalled|NumbersItsLane|Unwinds'` -> 8 passed
  - `pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **3 passed in 6.73s**
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **457 passed, 2 skipped, 346 subtests** (unchanged;
    no server module touched)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
  - `validate-phase-a-locality.sh` on the tip -> **FAILS** on `CaptureController.swift` and
    `CaptureOutbox.swift`. Deliberate and recorded, not repaired: the script's own header says
    later production phases widen scope and its allowlist is the thirteen registered A4 paths.
    Equivalent read-only proof that it is still green at the frozen checkpoint:
    `git diff --name-only af3ac366 1ede498 --` is exactly that allowlist. The script was not edited.
- Evidence:
  - New nodes: `testOutboxRetainsEveryFrameAcrossAFiveSecondOutageAndDeliversEachExactlyOnce`,
    `testAmbiguousAnswerAndDuplicateRetryReuseTheOriginalLaneSequenceIdentity`,
    `testStartUnwindsOnlyWhenNoRetryCouldEverPublish`,
    `testTypedRetryPolicySeparatesTransientAnswersFromUnauthorizedOnesAndNeitherLosesAudio`,
    `testOutboxOverflowKeepsSequencesGaplessAndReportsATypedDegradedState`,
    `testOneStalledLaneNeitherBlocksTheOtherLaneNorReattemptsItsWholeBacklog`,
    `testOutboxIsTheWireSequenceAuthorityAndReleasesOnlyAcknowledgedAudio`,
    `testEachSessionNumbersItsLaneFramesFromZero`.
  - 5 s outage measured, not asserted abstractly: ten 0.5 s ticks with
    `URLError(.networkConnectionLost)` hold 20 frames = `[.system: 5, .microphone: 5]` seconds with
    `refusedFrames 0`, `degradation nil`, `publishedFrameCount 0`, nothing accepted; the recovery
    tick delivers all 20 in per-lane sequence order 0..9 with their original capture timestamps,
    20 distinct identities, `retainedFrames 0`, `pumpFailure nil`.
  - Overflow measured at the contract capacity, not an injected one: 32 x 0.5 s frames into one
    lane admits exactly 30 (15.0 s), refuses 2, and after recovery the accepted system sequences
    are `0...30` contiguous with `discontinuity` false x30 then true — the gap is in the audio,
    never in the sequence stream.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (retention): added `outbox.acknowledge(...)` to the publish-failure branch
    (i.e. the old destructive behavior) -> the ambiguous node fails 5 assertions and then crashes on
    `attempts[1]` because the retry never happens. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (gaplessness): made a refusal increment `nextWireSequence` -> the two
    sequence-continuity assertions fail (`Optional(3)` vs `Optional(2)`; `...29, 32` vs `...29, 30`).
    Reverted -> green, 106 passed.
- Learned: (1) the outbox has to own the wire sequence, otherwise refusing a frame on overflow
  burns a number the emitter already assigned and the server rejects every subsequent frame of that
  lane as out of order — a bounded queue would have converted a transient overload into a dead lane.
  (2) Measuring the window in audio seconds instead of frames is what keeps the 15 s contract true
  across B3's resampling and coalescing change. (3) A retained-until-ACK queue makes `start`'s
  publish attempt best-effort by construction, which exposed the pre-existing zombie-start path:
  once audio is never lost, failing the whole start on a network hiccup is strictly wrong.
  (4) Sticky degradation is the only honest reading of "never report clean" — an overflow that later
  drains still lost audio, so `degradation` survives recovery and resets only on a new session.
- Blocker: none.
- Next: B3 — one stateful `AVAudioConverter` per lane, exact 8000-sample coalescing, and D14
  `AudioConvertHostTimeToNanos` conversion (defects 7 and 8). Realtime callbacks must stay
  copy/enqueue only. The outbox needs no change for it: capacity is duration-based and it re-stamps
  sequence after coalescing, so B3 only has to preserve the converted first-sample timestamp
  through the converter and emit exact frames.

## Iteration 7 - canonical 16 kHz wire format and converted-nanosecond timestamps (B3)
- Target: Phase B candidate B3 / plan D13 + D14. Kill defects 7 and 8: both lanes must leave the
  Mac as 16 kHz mono in exact 8000-sample frames, and `capture_timestamp_ns` must be real
  nanoseconds in one Mac host-time domain rather than raw Mach ticks.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/NativeLaneWireFormat.swift` (new);
  `.../{NativeAudioBuffers,NativeDualCaptureSource,CaptureController,MicrophoneCapture,
  SystemAudioTap}.swift`; `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift`;
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: everything unsafe on a Core Audio callback thread moved into one new file and one caller.
  `NativeLaneWireStream` (per lane) converts host ticks to nanoseconds, downmixes to mono,
  resamples through one stateful `AVAudioConverter`, and coalesces exact 8000-sample frames;
  `NativeLaneFrameEmitter` sequences and encodes what comes out. The drivers still only copy and
  enqueue, and they now hand over zero ticks when `AVAudioTime.isHostTimeValid` /
  `AudioTimeStamp.mFlags.hostTimeValid` is absent, which the conversion stage refuses.
  Two design choices carried the iteration. (1) Frame timestamps are re-derived from every
  buffer's own converted capture instant (`pendingStartNS = capturedNS - duration(pending)`)
  instead of accumulated as `anchor + n x 500 ms`: cadence-accumulated stamps would drift away
  from the host clock by the audio-device clock error over a meeting-length run, and the Phase F
  latency number is measured against exactly that clock. Cost: <= 1 wire sample of cadence jitter
  plus a fixed converter group delay, both measured below. (2) The outbox's capacity is in audio
  seconds, so the frame-size and rate change needed no edit there - 15 s per lane is now 30 frames
  instead of a rate-dependent count.
  A frame is emitted only at full size; the trailing partial leaves through `flush()`, which
  `NativeDualCaptureSource.stop` calls before parking the tail for one final `pendingFrames()`,
  and `CaptureController.stop` now drains once after stopping the source so the tail actually
  reaches the wire instead of being dropped at the end of every meeting.
- Validation:
  - `swift build --product mtd-capture` -> OK; `--product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **116 passed, 0 failures** (was 106: +10 nodes)
  - `swift test --filter 'HostTicks|CanonicalFrames|ConserveDuration|HostTimeDomain|CaptureInstant|TerminalFlush|TimelineGap|RealtimeCallbacks|FlushedTail'`
    -> 11 passed (the 10 new nodes plus the pre-existing realtime-callback source gate)
  - `pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **3 passed in 9.14s**, 0 skips
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **457 passed, 2 skipped, 346 subtests** (unchanged;
    no server module touched)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
  - `ls -d ~/Library/Application\ Support/MOSSCapture` -> absent (no real-home side effect)
  - `validate-phase-a-locality.sh` on the tip -> still FAILS by design, now also on
    `NativeLaneWireFormat.swift`. Frozen green stays `1ede498`; script not edited.
- Evidence:
  - New nodes: `testHostTicksBecomeRealNanosecondsAndAZeroReadingIsRefused`,
    `testDeviceRateInputBecomesSteadyCanonicalFramesOnTheConvertedHostClock`,
    `testFortyEightAndFortyFourPointOneKilohertzInputConserveDuration`,
    `testBothLanesShareOneConvertedHostTimeDomain`,
    `testUnusableCaptureInstantIsRefusedRatherThanFabricated`,
    `testTerminalFlushReleasesTheTrailingPartialFrameExactlyOnce`,
    `testADeviceTimelineGapMarksTheSplicedFrameAndKeepsFollowingTheDeviceClock`,
    `testRealtimeCallbacksNeitherConvertHostTimeNorResample`,
    `testNativeDualCaptureSourceHandsOverItsFlushedTailAfterStop`,
    `testCaptureControllerPublishesTheSourcesFlushedTailOnStop`.
  - Measured, not asserted abstractly. 96 x 1024 samples at 48 kHz produce exactly 4 frames of
    8000 samples at 16000 Hz (callback-shaped output would have been 96 frames of 1024 at 48 kHz)
    plus a 768-sample terminal flush: 32768 output samples for 98304 input, exact 3:1.
    44.1 kHz: 129 x 1024 = 132096 input -> 47926 output vs 47925 ideal, one sample of remainder.
    Frame cadence measured at 500,041,666 / 500,000,000 / 499,979,209 ns - within one wire sample
    (62,500 ns) of 500 ms. First frame lands 666,625 ns after the first buffer's converted capture
    instant: the converter's fixed group delay, never earlier than the capture.
  - Gap node: six missing callbacks (128 ms) produce exactly one frame flagged discontinuous and a
    step of 500 ms + the missing 128 ms, proving the wire timeline follows the device clock across
    a splice rather than pretending the audio was continuous.

    of converting it -> 4 nodes fail and the suite crashes on an empty frame list; the steady-frame

    against the required >= 1,000,000,000. Reverted -> green.

    numerator` traps on an absurd tick count (signal 5). It now divides first, reports overflow,
    and returns nil rather than wrapping a huge reading into a small plausible timestamp.
- Learned: (1) A sample-rate converter's retained samples make "timestamp = anchor + n frames" and
  "timestamp = capture instant - pending duration" differ by a constant group delay and about one
  output sample of jitter. Choosing the second is what keeps a 16-minute soak's timestamps on the
  device clock; choosing the first would have looked exact in tests and drifted in the meeting.
  (2) The 15 s outbox window survived the rate change untouched only because iteration 6 measured
  it in audio seconds - a frame-count window would have silently become 7.5 s of retention here.
  (3) The existing lane tests are about admission, health and sequencing, not about audio, so they
  now run on an explicit one-sample lab wire format; making the format a stated parameter rather
  than a hidden constant is what let the production grid be tested for real.
- Blocker: none.
- Next: B4 - one persistent URLSession, at most one in-flight POST per lane with the lanes
  concurrent, and no overlapping pump re-entry. The thing to bound is the recovery burst, not the
  steady state: B3 fixed steady state at 2 POSTs/s/lane, but draining a 15 s backlog is still 30
  sequential semaphore-blocked round trips per lane inside one 0.5 s tick.

## Iteration 8 - bounded concurrent transport (B4)
- Target: Phase B candidate B4 / defect 5. Bound what the transport can have outstanding, in width
  (requests) and in time (overlapping passes), and stop building a TLS session per frame.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/CapturePublishPump.swift` (new);
  `.../{CaptureController,CaptureHTTPTransport,CaptureScheduling,FakeCaptureAdapters}.swift`;
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift`;
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift`;
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: four changes, one contract.
  (1) `CaptureFramePublishPump` is now the only caller of `transport.publish`. One pre-created
      serial `DispatchQueue` per lane: a lane has at most one request in flight because its stream
      must arrive in sequence order, and the lanes run at the same time because nothing couples
      them. In-flight work is `CaptureLane.allCases.count` and does not grow with the backlog, the
      meeting length or the tick count. B2's one-head-retry-per-lane-per-tick rule is unchanged;
      the reported failure is now picked in lane order so a two-lane outage is deterministic.
  (2) Passes never overlap. `Contention.skip` for the periodic tick (its audio is already retained;
      a second pass over the same frames would publish identities that are in flight and multiply
      in-flight work by the number of late ticks) and `.wait` for start and stop (the meeting's
      final frames reach the wire only through the stop's pass). A skipped tick still emits health
      — a long recovery drain must not read as a dead helper against the 30 s lease.
  (3) The app's pump interval was a `0.25` literal; it is now `CapturePumpContract.interval` = 0.5 s,
      the prd's domain value and exactly one canonical wire frame per lane per tick.
  (4) `PinnedURLSessionCaptureHTTPClientProvider` caches one client per pin and `invalidate()`s a
      superseded one. Before this, `publish` called the provider per frame and the provider built a
      fresh ephemeral `URLSession` every time: a TLS handshake per 0.5 s of audio per lane, and —
      because a session retains its delegate until invalidated — a leaked session plus pinning
      delegate per frame for the whole meeting (~14,400 in a 60-minute two-lane run). The app now
      builds one provider and hands it to frames, heartbeat and pairing.
- Validation:
  - `swift build --product mtd-capture` -> OK; `--product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK; zero warnings
    (checked against a clean scratch path, the tree was warning-free before and after)
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **121 passed, 0 failures** (was 116: +5 nodes)
  - the five new nodes re-run 12x consecutively -> 12/12 clean, no flake
  - `pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **3 passed in 8.28s**, 0 skips
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **457 passed, 2 skipped, 346 subtests** (unchanged)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
  - `ls -d ~/Library/Application\ Support/MOSSCapture` -> absent (no real-home side effect)
  - `validate-phase-a-locality.sh` on the tip -> still FAILS by design, now also on
    `CapturePublishPump.swift`. Frozen green stays `1ede498`; script not edited.
- Evidence:
  - New nodes: `testLanesPublishConcurrentlyWithOneRequestInFlightPerLane`,
    `testATickArrivingDuringADrainSkipsInsteadOfReSendingFramesAlreadyInFlight`,
    `testStopWaitsForTheRunningPassInsteadOfSkippingTheFinalDrain`,
    `testPinnedProviderKeepsOneSessionPerPinAndSupersedesARotatedOne`,
    `testProductionPumpTicksOncePerCanonicalWireFrameOnOneSharedPinnedProvider`.
  - Concurrency is proven by rendezvous, not by a sleep: every lane's first request is held until
    every lane has one outstanding, so a serial pump fails on the deadline instead of passing
    because it was fast. Measured max concurrency 2 overall and 1 per lane, with each lane's four
    sequences still 0..3 in order.
  - Two existing nodes asserted a cross-lane publish *order* B4 deliberately gives up
    (`[system, microphone]`, `[system, microphone, system, microphone]`). Cross-lane order was never
    contractual — the server aligns lanes by capture timestamp and rejects only out-of-order frames
    *within* a lane — so they now assert per-lane order plus total count, with the reason in a
    comment. `FakeCaptureTransportAdapter` and `ProgrammableCaptureTransport` grew locks: the first
    concurrent run segfaulted (signal 11) inside `ProgrammableCaptureTransport.publish`, which was
    an unsynchronized array in the test double, not a fault in the transport.
  - Bug found by the stop node, not by review: `publishPendingFrames` snapshotted
    `outbox.retainedFrames()` *before* asking for the turn, so a caller that waited worked from a
    list captured while the running pass still had those frames in flight and re-published them
    (`[1, 1, 2]` where `[1, 2]` was required). The pump now takes a `retainedFrames` closure and
    reads it after `beginPass`. Admission from the source stays outside the turn on purpose — a
    skipped tick must still drain the source's bounded ring into the outbox, which is where loss is
    accounted for.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (lane concurrency): `queue.async(group:)` -> `queue.sync` -> the rendezvous
    times out and max concurrency reads 1 instead of 2. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (skip becomes wait): dropped the `.skip` early return -> the late tick
    never returns within 5 s and the heartbeat count does not advance. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 3 (no gate at all): removed `beginPass`/`endPass` -> attempts go 2 -> 4, the
    same two identities published twice concurrently. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 4 (stop skips): `stop`'s contention `.wait` -> `.skip` -> the flushed tail is
    never published (`[1]` not `[1, 2]`), `publishedFrameCount` 0 and `outbox.retainedFrames` 2 —
    the meeting's last audio is stranded. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 5 (no session cache): removed the per-pin hit -> 180 sessions built for 180
    requests instead of 1. Reverted -> green, 121 passed.
- Learned: (1) The re-entry guard and the frame snapshot are one decision, not two. Winning the turn
  is worthless if the caller already decided what to send — a waiting stop with a stale list is
  exactly the duplicate publisher the guard was added to prevent. (2) A per-request `URLSession` is
  invisible in tests and unbounded in production: nothing fails, the process just accumulates one
  session and one strongly-retained pinning delegate per frame. Making the provider's construction
  count observable is what turned it into a node. (3) Concurrency can be proven without sleeping by
  making the fake refuse to proceed until the concurrency actually exists; the serial implementation
  then fails on a deadline, which is a real signal rather than a slow-machine artifact. (4) Giving
  up cross-lane publish order is the price of lane concurrency, and it was cheap only because the
  wire contract is per-lane; the tests that asserted global order were asserting an implementation
  detail.
- Blocker: none.
- Next: B5 - tracked Mac packaging/install tools (`bootstrap-signing-identity.sh`, `build-app.sh`,
  `install-app.sh`), idempotent and scratch-path testable with no real keychain/app/install mutation
  during tests. `build-app.sh` must drop the `keychain-access-groups` entitlement key: it still
  carries the unresolvable `$(AppIdentifierPrefix)` literal (defect 6 residue) and B1 made the
  Keychain store dormant, so signing that entitlement would assert an identity the certificate
  cannot satisfy.

## Iteration 9 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 1) - tracked Mac packaging/install tools (B5)
- Target: Phase B candidate B5 / defect 6 residue. Give Phase E a reviewed, idempotent path from
  "no identity, no app" to "signed app installed", and stop shipping an entitlement the signing
  identity cannot satisfy. Prior run's iterations 9-11 exited 1 in 1-7 s each (agent launch
  failures, `telemetry/run-20260728-002458-97091/run.jsonl`); no work was lost and B5 was untouched.
- Files: `macos/scripts/{moss-tool-lib,bootstrap-signing-identity,build-app,install-app}.sh` (new);
  `tests/test_macos_packaging_tools.py` (new, 9 nodes); `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`
- Method: one output discipline in `moss-tool-lib.sh` - `--dry-run` prints the ordered plan and
  mutates nothing, `rollback:` is printed *before* the first mutation, a re-run prints `unchanged:`,
  and `evidence: key=value` lines carry observed facts (never a secret). On top of it:
  (1) `bootstrap-signing-identity.sh`: dedicated `moss-signing.keychain-db` with a random password in
      a 0600 file, self-signed `extendedKeyUsage=critical,codeSigning` cert, import with
      `-T /usr/bin/codesign`, key partition list, append to the user keychain search list keeping
      every existing entry, accept only if a scratch `codesign` run succeeds. Refuses the
      login/System/default keychain. Reuse and repair paths for an interrupted earlier run.
  (2) `build-app.sh`: composes and signs the bundle + CLI into build output only (refuses an install
      location), derives the signing entitlements from the tracked file with `keychain-access-groups`
      dropped, then reads identifier/entitlements/DR back out of the *signature* with `codesign -d`.
  (3) `install-app.sh`: verifies the source first; does nothing when the installed bundle is already
      byte-identical (inode preserved -> TCC grants survive); moves a replacement aside to
      `<installed>.backup-<utc>` instead of deleting it; says so loudly when the DR changes.
- Validation:
  - `pytest tests/test_macos_packaging_tools.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **9 passed in 2.28s**
  - `pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **466 passed, 2 skipped, 346 subtests** in 39.4s (was 457/2)
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **121 tests, 0 failures** (no Swift source changed)
  - `swift build --product mtd-capture` / `--product MOSSCaptureApp` -> OK
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
  - after everything: `~/Library/Keychains/moss-signing.keychain-db`, `~/.config/moss-capture`,
    `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app`, `~/.local/bin/mtd-capture` all absent, and
    `security list-keychains -d user` is back to the login keychain alone.
- Evidence:
  - **The plan's D7 incantation does not work as written, and now does.** `codesign --keychain <kc>
    --sign "<name>"` is accepted and ignored: with the identity only in `<kc>` it fails
    `no identity found`, while `security find-identity <kc>` lists it as `CSSMERR_TP_NOT_TRUSTED`.
    Adding the keychain to the **user keychain search list** makes the identical command succeed -
    no `add-trusted-cert`, no trust change, and `find-identity -v -p codesigning` still says
    `0 valid identities found`. So search-list membership, not the flag, is what makes an identity
    reachable. Rollback is complete: `security delete-keychain` removes the search-list entry too.
  - Proven for real once, in a temp keychain (`/tmp/moss-b5/kc/`), rollback recorded before the run
    and executed after: create -> import -> partition list -> search list -> probe. Two different
    binaries (`/usr/bin/true`, `/usr/bin/false`) both got
    `designated => identifier "..." and certificate leaf = H"421b362cb77ae6bb662cd30c1c9dd0910f67fae6"`,
    reproducing D7's "DR identical across rebuilds" claim on this host. `build-app.sh` signed with
    that identity produced
    `designated => identifier "com.alphasight.moss.capture" and certificate leaf = H"421b..."`.
    Teardown verified: no keychain file, no search-list entry, login keychain untouched.
  - Ad-hoc build reproducibility: two independent output directories from the same sources gave the
    same `bundle_digest` (`fb1b9408...`) and the same DR; a third run over an existing output
    printed `unchanged:` and re-signed nothing.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (entitlement drop removed): the signature really does carry
    `keychain-access-groups = ['$(AppIdentifierPrefix)com.alphasight.moss.capture']`. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (idempotence check -> `if false`): the installed executable's inode and
    mtime change on the second install - the case where a human's TCC grants are silently lost.
    Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 3 (`mv` aside -> `rm -rf`): the backup test fails; the previous install is
    unrecoverable. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 4 (source verification removed): a bundle with one byte appended to its
    executable installs cleanly. Reverted -> green, 9 passed.
  - The install rollback line is executed by the test, not just matched: it restores the replaced
    bundle's `CFBundleShortVersionString` byte for byte.
- Learned: (1) A "proven" incantation in a plan document is still a hypothesis on another host -
  the D7 probe evidently ran with the keychain in the search list, and the flag it recorded does
  nothing. Running the tool for real once, in a temp path with the rollback recorded first, is what
  converted a plausible E1 step into a working one; discovering this at E1 would have cost a human
  round trip. (2) The safest install is the one that does nothing: comparing a whole-bundle digest
  and skipping the copy is what preserves the inode, and the inode is what preserves the two grants
  that only a human can give. (3) `--dry-run` plus rollback-before-mutation is worth the lines - it
  makes each tool reviewable *before* it is pointed at m4mbp, which is the only machine where these
  mutations are expensive.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks remain the one irreducible human step (E3), untouched by this iteration.
- Next: B6 - the Phase B client gate. Both Swift products build, Swift suite, full Python suite,
  Darwin tracer with zero skips, and the leak scan, all recorded with the exact SHA. Everything in
  the gate has been run green this iteration except recording it as one checkpoint; after that,
  Phase C opens with C1 (session-lifecycle view authority, defect 4).
