## Iteration 11 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 3) - session-lifecycle view authority (C1)
- Target: Phase C candidate C1, confirmed defect 4. `VIEW_TTL_SECONDS = 900` fixed at
  `bind_session` fails a >15-minute meeting outright, which is the PRD's whole point.
- Files: `moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_auth.py`, `moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_transport.py`,
  `tests/test_live_auth.py`, `tests/test_live_api.py`, `tests/test_live_portal.py` (route surface),
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.
- Method: make view authority **derived** rather than mirrored. `attach_live_routes` wires
  `LiveAccessRegistry.bind_session_lifecycle(_session_status)`; `_view_for_digest` admits a token
  only if the ownership entry exists, is not operator-revoked, is under the 12 h absolute cap
  (`VIEW_ABSOLUTE_CAP_SECONDS`), and the lifecycle reports a status in
  `VIEWABLE_SESSION_STATUSES = {active, closing}`. `VIEW_TTL_SECONDS` deleted, not raised.
  Added `revoke_view` + loopback-only `DELETE /api/live/sessions/{id}/view` for operator revoke.
- Validation:
  - `pytest tests/test_live_auth.py tests/test_live_api.py tests/test_live_portal.py -q` ->
    **52 passed** (auth 7 -> 13, api 26 -> 29)
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **475 passed, 2 skipped, 368 subtests** in 36.1s
    (was 466/2/346; +9 nodes, no regressions)
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **121 tests, 0 failures** (no Swift change)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
- Evidence:
  - **The status a viewer must follow is not `session.status`.** Written as a status-only check the
    failed-stop node returned `'active' != 'failed'`: a runtime terminal failure (stop that fails
    accounting, helper lease expiry) refuses every later frame while the mono session still reads
    `active`. `_session_status` now reports `failed` whenever `snapshot.terminal_failure is not
    None`. Found by the test, not by review, and it is exactly the case a mirror cannot cover -
    the failed-stop path releases v2/mixer/presence but deliberately **not** the access entry,
    because the capture client still has to be able to abort and clean up (existing
    `test_stop_timeout_and_abort_return_terminal_failure_semantics` depends on that abort working).
  - Fail-closed is structural: an unwired registry grants **no** view authority, so authority that
    is not bound to a lifecycle cannot exist by omission. The allowlist (not a terminal denylist)
    means a status added later revokes the view until it is admitted deliberately.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (`_session_is_viewable` -> `return True`, i.e. mirror-only): 5 lifecycle
    subtests + the fail-closed node + the failed-stop route node fail. Clean stop still passes -
    that is the path a mirror does cover, which is why it alone was not enough evidence.
    Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (`VIEW_ABSOLUTE_CAP_SECONDS = 900`): the virtual-meeting node fails from
    minute 20 on, plus the scope and route expiry assertions. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 3 (drop the `terminal_failure` branch): the failed-stop route node fails.
    Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 4 (drop the loopback check in `revoke_view`): both operator-revoke nodes
    fail. Reverted -> green.
  - Secret hygiene held: the persisted state file still carries devices only - no view digest, no
    session id - which is also what makes a restart revoke every view (checked by the restart node,
    not assumed).
  - `tests/test_live_portal.py`'s `EXPECTED_LIVE_API` caught the new route immediately; the live
    API surface is guarded by an exact set, so the route was added there deliberately.
- Learned: (1) "Bound to the session lifecycle" is only real if the authority *asks* the lifecycle
  owner; a copy maintained by four call sites is a mirror, and the hole is always the error path
  that forgot to update it. (2) Two terminal representations coexist here - `session.status` and
  `terminal_failure` - and the ownership entry has a third lifetime again, because capture needs to
  outlive the session's death long enough to abort. Keeping those three separate is what let the
  view die immediately without stranding the capture client. (3) The one thing to re-check at F3:
  the 16-minute soak now depends on `closing` being viewable, so a viewer polling through a drain
  keeps working and only the terminal transition cuts it off.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.
- Next: C2 - bounds retune (`hard_cap_samples=40000`, `max_retained_samples=960000`,
  `frame_samples=8000`) with generated bundle hashes, never hand-edited, plus descriptor/config
  admission and capacity-headroom tests. B3 already made the client send 8000-sample 16 kHz frames,
  so the host manifest bounds are the last thing standing between the two ends of the wire.

## Iteration 12 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 4) - generated manifest bounds (C2)
- Target: Phase C candidate C2. B3 made the Mac client send exact 8000-sample 16 kHz frames, but
  the bounds the server admits them against live in the *host* manifest, which nothing in the repo
  generated or checked. The deployed provisional still carried the 7.5 s span cap and 30 s
  retention, so a >15-minute meeting would have been admitted against pre-retune bounds.
- Files: `moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_manifest_finalizer.py` (new),
  `ops/finalize-live-provider-manifest.py` (new, tracked wrapper),
  `tests/test_live_manifest_finalizer.py` (new), `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.
  No existing product source changed.
- Method: make the bounds **generated, with every relation checked**, rather than typed into a
  JSON file. The three tuned bounds are required flags (the plan's latency remedy tunes the span
  cap 40000 -> 32000 -> 24000, so they cannot be constants), and everything else is a derived
  relation the tool refuses to violate. Hashes are regenerated from the retuned sections; admission
  is then proven with the runtime's own readers (`_endpoint_config`, `_bounds`,
  `LiveServiceDescriptor`) before anything is written. Output discipline copied from B5's
  `moss-tool-lib.sh`: dry-run plan, rollback line before the first mutation, `unchanged:` re-run,
  backup instead of delete, `evidence: key=value`.
- Validation:
  - `pytest tests/test_live_manifest_finalizer.py -q` -> **17 passed**
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **492 passed, 2 skipped, 368 subtests** in 40.0s
    (was 475/2/368; +17 nodes, no regressions)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`
  - Swift untouched this iteration (no Swift source changed); last measured 121 passed at
    iteration 11.
- Evidence:
  - **The real host input was measured before trusting the tool** (read-only ssh probe, nothing
    mutated): the staged provisional carries `frame_samples 4000`, `hard_cap_samples 120000` in
    *both* `endpoint_config` and `bounds_config`, `max_retained_samples 480000`,
    `max_frame_samples 16000`, `decoder max_samples 120000`, `min_silence_samples 8000`, paddings
    1600, `source_revision "PROVISIONAL-UPDATE-AFTER-KEEPER"`. So every field the finalizer does
    not set already satisfies the contract and D2 will not be refused - and the test fixture now
    mirrors those exact values instead of invented ones.
  - The two hard caps are separate fields with separate readers: `EndpointPolicy` closes the span
    and `LiveSession` freezes it. The host file happens to hold them equal; nothing enforced it.
    The tool now sets both from one flag and refuses a divergence, because a smaller freeze cap
    would split spans the endpoint had not closed.
  - Capacity headroom is measured, not asserted in prose: a `LiveLaneIngress` at the generated
    960000 accepts one 2.5 s span plus a full 15 s reconnect burst on **both** lanes
    (35 frames/lane = 280000 samples), the oldest burst frame still replays to the identical ack
    (70 acks used of the shared 256-entry window), the lane fills to exactly 120 frames = 60.0 s,
    and frame 121 raises `LiveV2LaneCapacityError`.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (drop the `max_retained_samples` assignment): 5 nodes fail, including the
    capacity-headroom node and the ops-tool end-to-end node. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (inherit `config_hashes` from the input instead of regenerating): **7**
    nodes fail - the tool's own admission step refuses to write, so a stale-hash manifest cannot
    reach the host even if a test had missed it. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 3 (write over an existing differing manifest without the backup): the
    backup/rollback node fails; the printed `rollback: mv ...` is executed by that test and really
    restores the previous bytes. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 4 (drop `hard_cap_samples <= decoder_config.max_samples`): the
    160000-cap refusal node fails. Reverted -> green.
  - Refusals cover: the `PROVISIONAL-...` placeholder, an uppercase SHA, a short SHA, a 4000-sample
    frame, a cap that is not a whole wire frame, a cap below `min_silence_samples`, a cap beyond the
    decoder bound, retention below burst+cap, a manifest whose *untuned* `min_silence_samples`
    drifts, output == input, unreadable and non-JSON input. Every refusal leaves no output file.
- Learned: (1) The plan's ordered latency remedy (2.0 s span cap) is why the bounds cannot be
  constants in the server - but the *relations* can be, and the relations are what a wrong flag
  violates. Required flags plus checked relations gave both. (2) Verifying admission inside the
  generator - with the runtime's readers, before writing - turned "the tests check the tool" into
  "the tool cannot emit a manifest the runtime would reject", which is what rehearsal 2 exposed.
  (3) Probing the real host input first was worth more than any local fixture: it proved the two
  fields the tool deliberately does not set (`min_silence_samples`, `max_frame_samples`) already
  hold contract-compatible values, so D2 has no hidden blocker.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.
- Next: C3 - the tracked deployment and certification bundle: TLS generator with four SANs, live
  env/systemd templates, start-web port/runs-dir overrides, two-port Windows networking, loopback
  `ops/live-pair.sh`, `LOCAL_DEPLOYMENT.md` (which must document the C2 finalizer), and the
  app-owned latency probe that emits only redacted aggregate timings. C3 is the largest remaining
  item before the C4 gate; consider splitting it (transport/TLS artifacts first, latency probe
  second) rather than one oversized iteration.

## Iteration 13 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 5) - live TLS material + loopback pairing (C3a)
- Target: Phase C candidate C3, split as iteration 12 recommended. This iteration is C3a - the two
  *credential* tools D2 and D4 run on the host: `ops/generate-live-tls.sh` and `ops/live-pair.sh`.
  They are one logical change because they are coupled by one value: SHA-256 over the leaf
  certificate's DER bytes. The generator mints it, the server derives it from the same file
  (`web_cli._certificate_sha256`) and stamps it into every pairing payload, and the Mac compares it
  to the leaf a handshake offers (`FullCertificatePinValidator`, 64 lowercase hex, nothing else).
  The staged host material has no tailnet SAN (plan D9), so nothing in the repo could generate the
  certificate the browser has to match, and nothing could mint a payload on the loopback-only route.
- Files: `ops/moss-ops-lib.sh` (new), `ops/generate-live-tls.sh` (new), `ops/live-pair.sh` (new),
  `tests/test_live_deployment_credentials.py` (new), `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.
  **No product source changed** (`git diff --name-only` touches only ops/, tests/ and the loop dir).
- Method: names are flags with checked relations, exactly C2's shape - a deployment states its own
  DNS names and addresses, and an address is refused unless it is inside the networks `live_auth`
  admits, so the tool cannot mint material for a peer the server itself would reject. What was
  passed in is an input; what the generated file says is evidence (SAN set, `serverAuth`,
  key/certificate pairing, and the pin computed two independent ways which must agree) and the
  installed file is re-read again after the move. Output discipline is B5's, via a Linux-side
  `moss-ops-lib.sh`; the two libraries stay separate because the primitives differ per host family
  and a parity node compares their emitted lines byte for byte.
- Validation:
  - `pytest tests/test_live_deployment_credentials.py -q` -> **14 passed** in 10.7s
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **506 passed, 2 skipped, 368 subtests** in 49.5s
    (was 492/2/368; +14 nodes, no regressions)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
  - `bash -n` on all three new scripts -> clean; every script is bash-3.2 compatible (MacStudio's
    /bin/bash is 3.2.57, the server's is 5.x, so 3.2 is the binding constraint - no associative
    arrays, no `${var,,}`, `[[ =~ ]]` and `$'\n'` verified by hand under 3.2 before use)
  - Swift untouched this iteration (no Swift source changed); last measured 121 passed at
    iteration 11.
- Evidence:
  - **The pin is proven identical across four readers**: the tool's `evidence: pin=`, the server's
    own `web_cli._certificate_sha256`, `hashlib.sha256(ssl.PEM_cert_to_DER_cert(...))` computed in
    the test without either implementation, and the leaf a *real handshake* offers - the node loads
    the generated pair into an `SSLContext` (the same `load_cert_chain` call uvicorn makes) and
    reads the certificate back with `ssl.get_server_certificate`. A key that did not belong to the
    certificate would fail at `load_cert_chain`, so that node covers the pairing too.
  - **A re-run must not rotate.** This started as tidiness and turned out to be the safety property
    of the tool: the pin is what every paired Mac stores, so an accidental second run would break a
    live client with a pin mismatch and invalidate every outstanding payload. A re-run with the same
    names in a different order and a different `--days` prints `unchanged:`, emits no `change:` line
    and leaves inode, mtime and bytes untouched. Rotation is `--rotate` only, and a name change, CN
    change, near-expiry or half-installed pair is *refused* with the exact re-run command rather
    than silently rotated.
  - **`live-pair.sh` catches the rotated-but-not-restarted service.** The payload carries the digest
    of the certificate the *running process* loaded; the tool compares it with the digest of the
    file on disk and refuses to print anything when they differ, naming both digests. Without this
    the failure mode surfaces on the Mac as an unexplained pinning failure with a valid-looking
    pairing code. Proven with a real uvicorn TLS server on cert A and `--cert` pointing at cert B.
  - Secret hygiene is measured, not asserted in prose: two real payloads are minted from the real
    `POST /api/live/pairing-codes` route over real TLS, each printed exactly once on its own
    `payload:` line, and the node then walks the whole tree under the tool's HOME, TMPDIR and
    working directory and asserts neither secret reached any file - plus not stderr, not an
    `evidence:` value, and not the server's persisted auth state. The two mints differ, so nothing
    is cached or replayed.
  - Address admission is a **parity table against the server**, asked through `LiveAccessRegistry`'s
    public surface rather than by copying its network tuple: 14 IPv4 cases including the exact
    boundaries 100.63.255.255/100.64.0.8/100.128.0.1 and 172.15/172.16/172.32, plus 169.254.1.1,
    8.8.8.8 and two malformed values. The tool's accept/refuse agrees with the server on all 14.
    IPv6 is refused although the server admits ULA and link-local: narrower than the server is safe,
    wider would not be, and that direction is asserted.
  - A dry run validates *before* it plans: 15 malformed-input cases produce zero `plan:` lines, so
    `--dry-run` refuses exactly what a real run would. A failed generation (openssl stubbed to fail
    at `req`) leaves no certificate, no key and an empty scratch TMPDIR.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (drop the payload-vs-disk digest cross-check): the mismatch node fails.
    Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (also emit the payload as an `evidence:` line): the secrecy node fails.
    Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 3 (make the `unchanged:` branch unreachable, i.e. rotate on every run): the
    no-rotate node fails. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 4 (print `rollback:` after the moves instead of before): the reversible-
    rotation node fails on ordering. Reverted -> green.
  - Mutation rehearsal 5 (drop the loopback check in `live-pair.sh`): the pre-send node fails - and
    it fails *by reaching the stub curl*, which is what the marker file in that node is for.
  - Found while writing it: `10.0.0.5` is a label-legal DNS name, so an address typed into `--dns`
    was accepted and would have become a DNS-type name that matches nothing. Fixed generally - a
    final label that is all digits is not a hostname - so `8.8.8.8` is caught the same way, and the
    error says which flag to use.
  - Not covered here on purpose: exchanging a minted payload needs a non-loopback TLS peer, which
    `tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py` already does end to end with a payload from the same route.
    This test's server binds loopback only on an ephemeral port; D4 exchanges for real.
- Learned: (1) The `unchanged:`/rollback discipline B5 introduced for TCC grants transfers exactly
  to TLS material, and for the same reason: the thing being protected is a human-visible binding
  (there, the grant; here, the pin every paired client stores) that a silent rewrite destroys.
  (2) Asking the server whether it would admit an address - through its public API, in the test -
  is a better rule than copying its network list into the tool: the tool's own check can be simple
  and narrow as long as a node proves it never becomes wider. (3) A credential tool's most useful
  check is not on its own output but on the *disagreement between two places the value lives*: the
  file on disk and the process that loaded it.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.
- Next: C3b - the live service templates and networking: `ops/moss-live.env.example`,
  `ops/systemd/moss-live-web.service`, `ops/start-web.sh` port/runs-dir overrides that keep the
  7860 batch invocation byte-identical when live is off, two-port Windows forwarding/firewall,
  `ops/install-services.sh` for the second unit, and `LOCAL_DEPLOYMENT.md` documenting the C2
  finalizer plus both C3a tools (including that the `payload:` line is never redirected to a file).
  Then C3c, the app-owned latency probe, which is the last item before the C4 gate.

## Iteration 14 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 6) - two-service deployment bundle (C3b)
- Target: Phase C candidate C3b - the tracked templates and networking that make the live service
  a *deployable* second service: `ops/moss-live.env.example`, `ops/systemd/moss-live-web.service`,
  port/runs-dir overrides in `ops/start-web.sh`, `--with-live` in `ops/install-services.sh`,
  two-port forwarding in `ops/configure-windows-network.ps1`, and the `LOCAL_DEPLOYMENT.md` section
  that documents them together with the C2 finalizer and the C3a credential tools.
- Files: `ops/moss-live.env.example` (new), `ops/systemd/moss-live-web.service` (new),
  `ops/start-web.sh`, `ops/install-services.sh`, `ops/configure-windows-network.ps1`,
  `LOCAL_DEPLOYMENT.md`, `.gitignore`, `tests/test_live_service_deployment.py` (new),
  `tests/test_speaker_identity_provider.py`, `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.
  **No product source changed.**
- Method: the shape of the change is forced by one fact in the existing code - `web_cli.main`
  passes the certificate to `uvicorn.run`, so `--live` applies TLS to the *whole* listener
  (`web_cli.py:197-202`). The live service therefore cannot be a mode of the batch process; it has
  to be a second unit on its own port, and the property worth protecting is that adding it leaves
  the first one byte-for-byte alone. One adapter serves both units and only the environment
  differs, so the two invocations cannot drift. `MOSS_WEB_PORT`/`MOSS_RUNS_DIR` are required flags
  under live mode with checked relations (not the batch port, not the batch runs dir), which is the
  same required-flag/checked-relation shape C2 and C3a use.
- Validation:
  - `pytest tests/test_live_service_deployment.py -q` -> **30 passed** in 4.5s
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **536 passed, 2 skipped, 368 subtests** in 52.5s
    (was 506/2/368; +30 new nodes, no regressions)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
  - `bash -n` on every `ops/*.sh` and `macos/scripts/*.sh` -> clean; the new script runs under
    MacStudio's bash 3.2.57 in the tests, so it is 3.2-compatible (`"${arr[@]}"` on an empty array
    is still guarded by the existing `((${#arr[@]}))` test - 3.2 errors on the expansion, not the
    count)
  - Swift untouched this iteration; last measured 121 passed at iteration 11.
- Evidence:
  - **The batch invocation is recorded, not asserted loosely.** `BATCH_ARGV` in the new test file
    is the exact argv the adapter produced before the live service existed, and the first node
    replays `ops/moss.env` verbatim through the real script - `getent` stubbed to a scratch home,
    the venv entrypoint replaced by an argv recorder - and compares element for element. The live
    node then proves the live argv is that same list with exactly two elements changed (port,
    runs dir) plus the six live flags appended. That is what the PRD's "batch service unharmed"
    clause rests on, checked mechanically rather than by reading the diff.
  - **The template is executable evidence.** The live node drives `ops/moss-live.env.example`
    verbatim rather than a fixture, so a template that is missing a variable, names one the adapter
    does not read, or spells one wrong fails the suite. A second node walks the template's keys and
    drops each one in turn: all seven are required and each refusal names the missing key on stderr
    with nothing on stdout.
  - **Fail-closed both ways.** `MOSS_LIVE_ENABLED=0` with the whole live profile still exported
    emits no `--live*` flag and none of the four live paths anywhere in the argv; and the live
    unit's `EnvironmentFile` for `ops/moss-live.env` carries no `-`, so a host that installs the
    unit without the profile gets a unit that fails to start rather than a second *batch* server
    on the batch port.
  - Found while writing the adapter: `${MOSS_WEB_PORT:-7860}` silently turns a profile line that
    sets nothing (`MOSS_WEB_PORT=`) into the batch port - i.e. a typo in the live profile would
    have produced a TLS listener on 7860 and taken the batch service down. Fixed generally by using
    `${VAR-default}` for both overrides, so an explicitly empty value reaches the validator and is
    refused; the empty-string case is in the malformed-port table.
  - `install-services.sh` derives its source from its own location, so it installs the checkout it
    lives in - which is exactly the D1-then-D3 order. It installs the tracked unit bytes verbatim
    at 0644 (asserted against the repository files), prints `unchanged:` per unit on a re-run
    without moving an inode, and the re-run also reports the *activation* as unchanged, because the
    test's `systemctl` stub remembers `enable`/`start` and answers `is-enabled`/`is-active` from
    that state.
  - The tool never restarts a running service. A changed unit file surfaces as
    `evidence: restart_required=<units>` instead, because bouncing the batch service is a
    deployment decision; that is recorded here so D3 does not mistake the absence of a restart for
    the absence of a change.
  - Ordering is checked positionally, not by prose: the replaced-unit node asserts the last
    `rollback:` line appears before the first `change:` line in stdout, then *executes* the printed
    rollback and gets the previous bytes back.
  - The PowerShell script cannot run on this host, so it is checked structurally by brace-matching
    the `if ($IncludeLive)` blocks: every line naming 7861 or `moss-live-web.service` must be
    inside one, every line naming 7860 or the batch rule name must be outside all of them, no
    `netsh` line may carry a port literal (both ports go through one loop), and the scheduled-task
    argument must append `-IncludeLive` so a sign-in refresh forwards the same ports as the run
    that registered it.
  - Documentation is covered by a node, not by intent: every operator-run tool in `ops/` must be
    named in `LOCAL_DEPLOYMENT.md`, with `*-lib.sh` (sourced) and anything a unit's `ExecStart`
    starts excluded. That is what pulled `finalize-live-provider-manifest.py`, `generate-live-tls.sh`
    and `live-pair.sh` into the document with their real invocations, including the rule that the
    `payload:` line is never redirected to a file.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (hard-code `--port 7860` again): the live-argv node fails. Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (drop the batch-port refusal): that refusal node fails. Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 3 (make the live `EnvironmentFile` optional with `-`): the unit-layout node
    fails. Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 4 (move the live forwarding row out of the `-IncludeLive` guard): the
    Windows node fails on "live forwarding is unconditional". Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 5 (drop the `ops/moss-live.env` precondition from `--with-live`): the
    refusal node fails. Reverted -> 30 passed.
  - `tests/test_speaker_identity_provider.py::test_start_web_is_the_single_environment_adapter`
    encoded the previous live contract (five required variables). It was updated to the widened one
    and *gained* two refusal checks; it was not relaxed. This is the deliberate supersession the
    PRD authorizes for post-checkpoint production work, and the discriminator stayed 10/10.
- Learned: (1) The two-service split was not a design preference - it falls out of
  `uvicorn.run(ssl_certfile=...)` covering the whole listener. Reading that one call before
  designing the templates is what turned "add live flags to the env file" (which would have put TLS
  on 7860 and broken the batch service the PRD requires intact) into "add a second unit".
  (2) `${VAR:-default}` versus `${VAR-default}` is a safety distinction whenever the variable comes
  from a file a human edits: colon-default absorbs the typo, plain-default surfaces it.
  (3) A stub that only records calls cannot show idempotence; giving the `systemctl` stub memory
  turned the re-run assertion from "it printed unchanged" into "it observed the state the first run
  created and then did nothing".
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.
- Next: C3c - the app-owned latency probe: the app (never the CLI) uses view authority, maps
  `committed_samples` to converted client capture timestamps, and emits only redacted p50/p95/max
  plus snapshot/events fetch timings. Test default-off, secrecy, and deterministic latency math
  with no host mutation. That is the last item before the C4 gate and keeper merge.

## Iteration 15 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 7) - app-owned latency probe (C3c)
- Target: Phase C candidate C3c - the last item before the C4 gate. The app (never the CLI) uses
  view authority, maps `committed_samples` to converted client capture timestamps, and emits only
  redacted p50/p95/max plus snapshot/events fetch timings, so the PRD's user-visible p95 gate stops
  being unfalsifiable.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/CaptureLatencyProbe.swift` (new),
  `CaptureController.swift` (one optional observer hook), `CaptureSecurity.swift` (`latency`
  command, `ControlChannelResponse.latency`, `latencyProbeUnavailable`), `CaptureCommandLine.swift`
  (allowlist + usage), `CaptureHTTPTransport.swift` (`liveURL` made internal so the probe reuses the
  route shape instead of copying it), `MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift` (the only composition root that
  builds the probe), `Tests/MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift`,
  `Tests/MTDCaptureCLITests/MTDCaptureCLITests.swift`, `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.
  **No server source changed.**
- Method: the shape came from two facts read before writing anything. (1) The plan's Phase F
  procedure is a subtraction between a capture instant and a later instant, so both readings must
  come from one clock - which only exists inside the app, where the converted `capture_timestamp_ns`
  and the view token are co-located. (2) `live_mixer.py:139-169` defines the origin as the maximum
  first capture instant over lanes that have registered and are not failed, and freezes it into
  `_cursor_ns` at the first mix. So the probe derives the origin by the same rule rather than
  assuming the earliest frame or the start of capture. Splitting the file into a pure sampler (no
  clock, no network) and an IO probe is what makes the arithmetic testable to the nanosecond.
- Validation:
  - `swift test --filter 'Latency|AcknowledgedFrameObserver'` -> **10 passed** (9 core + 1 CLI)
  - `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **131 passed, 0 failures** (was 121; +10)
  - `swift build --scratch-path <empty> --product MOSSCaptureApp --product mtd-capture` ->
    both products from an empty scratch path in **8.0 s with zero warnings**
  - `pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **536 passed, 2 skipped, 368 subtests** in 53.0s
    (unchanged - this iteration touched no Python)
  - `spikes/idea-044-attempt2-red-control/repro.py --target "$PWD"` -> **10/10**, unchanged
  - `leak-scan.sh` -> `leak-scan: clean`; `git diff --check` -> clean
- Evidence:
  - **The origin is derived, not assumed.** A node feeds mic-first-at-1.0 s and system-first-at-1.5 s
    and proves the mapping uses 1.5 s; another proves that with only one lane's frame in hand the
    report says `mixerOriginResolved: false` and produces *no* figures at all, then resolves when the
    second lane arrives and does not move when a lane later leaves. 16000 divides 1e9 exactly
    (asserted), so `origin + committed x 62500` is exact integer arithmetic and the mapping
    contributes no rounding to a gate measured in milliseconds.
  - **The first reading is a baseline, not a measurement.** Four advances after it yield exactly the
    four injected latencies; nearest-rank p50/p95/max are asserted as literals (300/400/500/900 ->
    p50 400, p95 900), so a percentile change is a test failure rather than a quieter number.
  - **The plan's four disqualifiers are counted, not averaged.** One node drives a negative reading,
    a backwards clock, and a post-discontinuity reading through the same sampler and asserts one
    surviving measurement plus `rejectedNegative=1`, `rejectedClockRegression=1`,
    `rejectedAfterTimelineBreak=1`, `timelineIntact=false`.
  - Found while writing the short-frame rule: "reject non-8000 frames" would have tainted every
    clean stop, because `CaptureController.stop` deliberately drains the trailing partial (B3). The
    general rule that actually distinguishes the two cases is *succession*: a short frame the lane
    continues past is a hole; a short frame with no successor ended the stream. A node asserts both
    directions.
  - **Secrecy is structural, not reviewed.** The report type has one string field (the schema name);
    everything else is a count, a flag or a duration - so there is no field a token could occupy. A
    node filters *all* headers of every issued request for the token and asserts the carrying set is
    exactly `["Authorization"]`, and asserts the encoded control response contains neither the token,
    the server host, nor the session id. The events body is decoded into `{seq}` only, so the
    transcript never enters the app's memory.
  - **Default-off is proven three ways**: constructing the probe schedules nothing and sends nothing;
    `measure()` with no running session schedules nothing and answers `polling: false`; a dispatcher
    built without a probe answers the typed `latencyProbeUnavailable` (this is what the CLI-side
    composition would get). Asking twice still schedules one poll.
  - **The measurement anchors to accepted audio.** The observer hook fires after
    `outbox.acknowledge`, and a node with an always-failing transport proves a retained frame is
    never observed while the same frame through a working transport is observed exactly once with
    its wire facts intact. The hook takes lane/timestamp/rate/count/discontinuity rather than a
    `CaptureFrame`, so nothing on this path can reach the PCM; a source gate asserts the probe file
    contains no `pcm16`/`pcm_base64`/`loadCaptureBearerToken`.
  - Mutation rehearsal 1 (`origins.max()` -> `.min()`): 4 assertions fail. Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 2 (copy the view token into a second request header): 2 assertions fail -
    *after* the node was strengthened. The first attempt at this rehearsal passed, because the node
    only checked the URL; the all-headers check exists because the mutation found the gap.
  - Mutation rehearsal 3 (observe the frame before the publish/ack): 3 assertions fail. Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 4 (drop the `current.running` guard so `measure()` always polls): 3 fail.
  - Mutation rehearsal 5 (count readings after a timeline break): 1 fails. Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 6 (drop the 1 s portal cycle from the render bound): 2 fail. Reverted.
  - Mutation rehearsal 7 (wait for a failed lane the mixer already excluded): 6 fail. Reverted.
- Learned: (1) Reading the *server's* origin rule before writing the client's was the whole
  iteration - "first frame seen" and "start of capture" are both wrong, and either would have
  produced a plausible latency figure that was silently offset by the lanes' start skew.
  (2) A mutation rehearsal is worth running even when the node looks obviously sufficient: the
  token-secrecy node passed a real leak (a second header) until the assertion was changed from "not
  in the URL" to "in exactly one header".
  (3) Handing an observer facts instead of the frame is cheaper than auditing what the observer does
  with the frame - the PCM is simply not reachable from that call.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.
- Next: C4 - the final local gate and the single keeper merge. Run the Swift, full Python, tracer and
  Phase C reliability gates on the feature tip, record the clause-to-node map the C1 residue asks for
  (5 s outage / ambiguous retry / duplicate retry / 429 / outbox overflow), then
  `RALPH_MERGE_DRY_RUN=1 bash scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh` followed by the real run, and record
  both the feature tip and merge SHAs.

  The checkout is deliberately **detached** at the reviewed SHA, so the server's local `main` ref
  never moves and this one command restores the exact pre-mutation worktree. No service is
  restarted by D1, so no service rollback is needed: `moss-web.service` keeps serving the process
  it already has.
- Rollback for the **push**: there is none, by design. `git push origin main` is a fast-forward
  163e969..f9285d6 (118 commits) and the PRD forbids force-push, so publishing is one-way. It is
  authorized only because all three gates are green and the single keeper merge is complete:
  Phase B client gate at 3fb5567, server meeting-reliability gate and final local gate at
  f400d426, merge f9285d6 with `git diff f400d42 f9285d6` empty.

### Applied
- Files: `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}` only. **No tracked file changed** - D1 is a
  publish, and the merge freeze means the feature branch may carry only Ralph evidence.
- Validation, in order:

  - **The reviewed ops tooling landed on the host with the checkout**: `ops/` now lists

    pre-D3 state: the live unit cannot start by accident because its `EnvironmentFile` is
    mandatory.
  - **"Batch unharmed" needed a second, sharper proof and got one.** The four 200s are weaker
    evidence than they look: `INDEX_HTML` and `FAVICON_SVG` are module-level constants
    (`moss_transcribe_diarize/app/server.py:123-129`), so the running uvicorn is still the
    `163e969` process image and a checkout *cannot* change what it serves. What actually carries
    risk is the next restart, which will use the new `ops/start-web.sh`. Proven without restarting
    or starting anything: a /tmp copy of the deployed script with only its final line rewritten
    (`sed '$ s|.*|printf "argv:%s\n" "${web_args[@]}"|'`, after asserting the original last line
    really is the `exec`, and confirming the copy contains zero `^exec ` lines), sourced with the
    host's real `ops/moss.env`, emits exactly the recorded contract argv from
    `tests/test_live_service_deployment.py:52-62` - `--backend vllm`, `--model <state>/model`,
    `--vllm-base-url http://127.0.0.1:8000/v1`, `--vllm-model OpenMOSS-Team/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize`,
    `--vllm-timeout 1800`, `--runs-dir /mnt/d/Coding/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize/runs`,
    `--host 0.0.0.0`, `--port 7860`, `--max-len 16384`, `--max-new-tokens 12000` - and **no live
    flag**. The C3b claim "the batch argv is unchanged" is now measured on the deployed host, not
    only in a test fixture.
  - **Detached checkout was chosen over fast-forwarding the host's `main`.** Both pin the same
    exact SHA, but detaching leaves the host's `main` ref at `163e969`, so the recorded rollback is
    a single `git checkout 163e969` that moves `HEAD` and nothing else - no ref rewriting, and no
    `reset --hard` anywhere near a deployment tree. It also makes "deploy only a reviewed SHA"
    literal rather than a property of a branch that could later be advanced.
- Learned: (1) A green HTTP probe is not evidence a deployment left a service alone when the
  service loads its assets at import time - it is evidence the *old process* is still running. The
  question worth answering was "what will the next restart do", and that was answerable read-only.
  (2) Deriving the argv by rewriting one line of a copy is safer than any flag I could have added:
  the tool needed no change, nothing was installed, and asserting the replaced line first means a
  drifted script fails the probe instead of silently launching a server.
  (3) The push is the one genuinely irreversible act in this whole plan (force-push is forbidden),
  so the ordering that mattered was recording the rollback and re-verifying `diff f400d42 f9285d6`
  *before* it, not after.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.

  is a public fingerprint by construction - it is what the Mac must store and what the server
  embeds in pairing payloads. No pairing payload was minted this iteration (that is D4), no bearer,
  device or view token was read, and no private key material was printed: the key was only ever
  passed as a **path** to `openssl pkey -pubout` and `ssl.load_cert_chain`.
- Defect found (recorded, not fixed - the merge freeze forbids tracked-source edits here):
  `ops/finalize-live-provider-manifest.py` imports the finalizer *through* the package, so
  `moss_transcribe_diarize/__init__.py` -> `configuration_moss_transcribe_diarize.py` ->
  `from transformers import PretrainedConfig` runs first. The host's system `python3` has no
  `transformers`, so the exact line `LOCAL_DEPLOYMENT.md:666` tells the operator to paste dies with
  `ModuleNotFoundError` before printing a single `plan:` line. The finalizer module itself needs
  nothing from `transformers`. D2 ran it with the deployment venv python
  (`~/.local/share/moss-transcribe-diarize/venv/bin/python3`, 3.12.13, transformers 5.14.1), which
  resolves `moss_transcribe_diarize` from **this same checkout** - so the reviewed revision is still
  what generated the file, and it is the interpreter the live service itself will use. The durable
  fix is `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` so the tool is stdlib-only and matches its own
  docstring; that needs a new branch and a decision, not a second keeper merge. Nothing downstream
  is blocked - only the doc's copy-paste line is wrong.
- Learned: (1) A dry-run is not a rehearsal of a **refusal**. `--dry-run` without `--rotate` printed
  the plan and exited 0; the refusal only exists on the mutation path. Running the real command
  without `--rotate` was the cheap way to prove the guard, and it is safe precisely because the tool
  refuses before it mutates - "run it for real and let it refuse" is better evidence than any dry
  run.
  (2) Verifying a manifest with the generator that wrote it proves nothing about admission. Loading
  it with the server's own `_endpoint_config`/`_bounds`/`_preflight_payload` is what shows D3 will
  start; those two take their per-section sub-mappings, not the whole payload, and passing the whole
  payload raises a misleading `min_speech_samples must be a non-negative integer`.
  (3) The interpreter is part of a deployment tool's contract. A tracked tool that works on the dev
  host and dies on the deployment host is a real defect even though every one of its 17 tests
  passes - the tests import the module directly and never exercise the wrapper's import path.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.

    trusting only the operator's own `live.crt`: `/live` **200**, `/api/live/descriptor` **200**
    (`/healthz` 404 - no such route, expected). Descriptor body carries
    `source_revision f9285d69…`, `provider_manifest_hash 61d97ffe…`, `hard_cap_samples 40000`,
    `max_retained_samples 960000`, `max_frame_samples 16000`, `sample_rate 16000`,
    `frame_samples 8000`, `feature_enabled true` - i.e. the D2 manifest is what the running service
    actually loaded, not just what is on disk.
  - **Windows forwarding.** The ssh session is elevated (`elevated=True`), so
    `configure-windows-network.ps1 -IncludeLive` ran directly. After: portproxy gained
    `0.0.0.0:7861 -> 172.30.115.123:7861` with the 7860 and both 5100 rows intact; firewall rule
    `MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize-Live` (Inbound / Allow / **Private** profile only) beside the existing
    `…-Web`; scheduled-task arguments now `… -RefreshOnly -IncludeLive`. Output:
    `LAN forwarding ready: 0.0.0.0:7860 …` and `… 0.0.0.0:7861 …`.
  - **PRD "Live service answering" clause, measured from m4mbp** (not only from MacStudio):
    the leaf offered on `100.64.0.8:7861` hashes to
    `a35ca9fc4a0f5b32bf7da6dc2e03c1fa5b4ac60992f0ee49b6d5677d22b680ff` - the D2 pin - and with that
    exact leaf as the only trust anchor `/live` -> **200** and `/api/live/descriptor` -> **200**.
    The same two routes are 200 in the PRD's literal `https://100.64.0.8:7861/...` form. MacStudio
    reproduces all of it independently.
  - **PRD "Batch service unharmed" clause**: `http://192.168.68.38:7860/` -> **200** from both
    m4mbp and MacStudio, `/api/runtime` -> 200, served by the same unrestarted pid 301112.
    Plaintext on 7861 -> **000** (TLS covers the whole listener, as designed).
  - **Idempotence, proven for real**: an immediate `ops/install-services.sh --with-live` re-run
    printed `unchanged:` for all three units plus `unchanged: moss-live-web.service is already
    enabled and running`, and `evidence: restart_required=none`. No `change:` line, no restart.
  - **Secret hygiene**: the live unit's whole journal (17 lines) contains **zero** case-insensitive
    matches for bearer/token/pairing/secret/authorization; `ops/moss-live.env` contains zero matches
    for bearer/token/secret/password (it is eight path/scalar keys); no pairing payload was minted
    this iteration (that is D4) and `live/live-auth.json` does not exist yet.
- Defect/finding recorded (not fixed - merge freeze): **file modes are unenforceable inside the
  host checkout.** `/mnt/d` is a 9p `drvfs` mount without the `metadata` option, so `chmod 0600
  ops/moss-live.env` succeeds and the file still reads `777` - as does the tracked
  `ops/moss-live.env.example`, so this predates the loop and D3 did not introduce it. It is
  harmless here because the profile carries only paths and scalars; it would be serious if live
  secret state were ever moved onto that drive. Everything secret - `live.key`, the manifest, and
  the `live-auth.json` D4 will create - lives on **ext4** under
  `~/.local/share/moss-transcribe-diarize/live` at mode 0700, where modes are real. Do not write a
  doc line promising a mode inside the checkout.
- Learned: (1) The cheapest proof that a two-profile deployment is really *one* adapter is to
  derive both argvs from the same script in the same shell: the batch line came back byte-identical
  to the recorded contract while the live line came back complete, which is stronger evidence than
  reading either unit file. (2) A descriptor fetched from the running service is the only proof the
  manifest was *loaded*; D2 proved the file admits, D3 proved the process serves those exact bounds.
  (3) Pin-then-trust is the right shape for verifying a pinned service from a shell: fetch the leaf,
  assert its DER sha256 equals the recorded pin, and only then use it as the sole `--cacert`. That
  reproduces `FullCertificatePinValidator`'s semantics instead of `curl -k`, which proves nothing.
- Blocker: none. TCC clicks (E3) remain the one irreducible human step.
- Next: D4 - mint exactly one pairing payload on the host with the reviewed
  `ops/live-pair.sh --url https://127.0.0.1:7861 --cert
  "$HOME/.local/share/moss-transcribe-diarize/live/live.crt"`, never redirecting the `payload:`
  line to a file and never running it from MacStudio (the mint route is loopback-only by design).
  Then verify the payload left no artifact (shell history, journal, argv, temp files) and that
  `live-auth.json` appeared at 0600 on ext4. The payload is only useful once a Mac can consume it,
  so if E1/E2 are not yet done, D4 may instead be run as the *refusal* checks plus the
  served-vs-on-disk digest agreement, deferring the single real mint to just before E2's install.

## Iteration 20 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 12) - E2a: align the m4mbp checkout with the published SHA
- Target: the checkout half of Phase E candidate E2, split out and taken before E1 because of what
  the pre-probe showed: **none of the reviewed tools existed on m4mbp**. `macos/scripts/` and
  `ops/systemd/` were absent (the checkout sat at OpenMOSS upstream `40cf854`, 14 commits, 37
  tracked files), so "run the reviewed signing tool on m4mbp" (E1) and "run the reviewed
  build/install tools" (E2b) were both unreachable steps, not just unstarted ones. E2 itself
  authorizes this action ("add AlphaSight remote on m4mbp; ... exact SHA"). It also closes a PRD
  acceptance clause on its own, which no later Phase-E step does.
- Deliberate deviation from E2's wording, recorded: `40cf854` **is** an ancestor of `f9285d6`
  (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` = yes), so a literal fast-forward of m4mbp's `main` was possible -
  and rejected. That branch tracks `origin` = **OpenMOSS upstream**; moving it would repoint a
  branch belonging to a different remote and its rollback would need a ref rewrite
  (`reset --hard`/`branch -f`), which prompt.md forbids. A **detached checkout** reaches the same
  `git rev-parse HEAD` with a rollback that moves only HEAD - exactly the pattern D1 already
  recorded for the server, where local `main` was likewise left behind at `163e969`.

  make a fast-forward the right move: whose remote a branch tracks decides whether the rollback is a
  HEAD move or a forbidden ref rewrite. (3) A cross-host git copy should be verified with digests as
  well as `git status`; SHA equality plus a clean porcelain is git's own word about its own store,

## Iteration 21 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 13) - E1: run the reviewed signing tool on m4mbp
- Target: Phase E candidate E1 - create the dedicated-keychain self-signed code-signing identity on
  m4mbp by running the reviewed `macos/scripts/bootstrap-signing-identity.sh`, which E2a put on that
  host. It is the highest-leverage open candidate: E2b (build + install the signed app) cannot start
  without an identity, and E3's TCC grants are only durable if the designated requirement is stable
  across rebuilds, which is exactly what a certificate-backed identity buys.

### Rollback recorded BEFORE any mutation (PRD "Reversibility")
- Pre-mutation state on m4mbp, measured read-only this iteration:
  - checkout HEAD `f9285d69ed7bcc592bb41b3dcdf29e3221968f44`, tree `815f23b0d7cd0c3fd7322640…`,
    `dirty_lines=0`; `macos/scripts/bootstrap-signing-identity.sh` present, `-rwxr-xr-x`,
    sha256 `729b597c4dfe6a01…` (== MacStudio), `moss-tool-lib.sh` sha256 `087793c68214d75d…`,
    non-executable by design
  - `~/Library/Keychains/moss-signing.keychain-db` **absent**;
    `~/.config/moss-capture/` **absent** (so the password file is absent too); `~/.config` is 0700
  - default user keychain = `login.keychain-db` (**not** the target)
  - user keychain search list, three pre-existing entries in this order:
      "/Users/ga0/Library/Keychains/projectclerk-signing.keychain-db"
      "/Users/ga0/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db"
      "/Users/ga0/Library/Keychains/openvpn.keychain-db"
    Two of them belong to other projects; the tool must preserve all three.
  - `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` = 1 valid identity, `LiveTranscribe Local Dev`
    (the login-keychain one that fails `errSecInternalComponent` over SSH)
  - `/usr/bin/openssl` = LibreSSL 3.3.6 (the PBES2 gotcha in the tool's header does not apply)
  - `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app` absent, `~/Library/Application Support/MOSSCapture` absent,
    macOS 26.5.2, 81 GiB free
- Rollback, printed by the tool itself before its first mutation:
    security delete-keychain '/Users/ga0/Library/Keychains/moss-signing.keychain-db' \
      && rm -f '/Users/ga0/.config/moss-capture/signing-keychain.password'
  `delete-keychain` also removes the search-list entry (measured on MacStudio in iteration 9), so
  that one command restores both the keychain set and the search list.
- Second rollback, printed only if the search list is actually touched, restoring the exact
  pre-existing three entries:
    security list-keychains -d user -s \
      '/Users/ga0/Library/Keychains/projectclerk-signing.keychain-db' \
      '/Users/ga0/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db' \
      '/Users/ga0/Library/Keychains/openvpn.keychain-db'
- Nothing outside `~/Library/Keychains/moss-signing.keychain-db`, `~/.config/moss-capture/` and the
  user search list is touched. No trust store change, no SIP/Gatekeeper change, no login keychain.

### Applied
- Files: `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}` only. **No tracked file changed** — the merge
  freeze means the feature branch may carry only Ralph evidence, and E1 is a host-state change.
- Validation, in order:
  - **`--dry-run` mutates nothing**: ten `plan:` lines plus the `rollback:` line, exit 0, and
    afterwards the keychain, the `~/.config/moss-capture` directory and the search list were all
    still exactly as measured above.
  - **Both keychain refusals fire before any mutation** (exit 1, nothing created):
    `--keychain ~/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db` → `error: refusing to manage the session
    keychain`; `--keychain /Library/Keychains/other.keychain-db` → `error: refusing to manage a
    system keychain directory: /Library/Keychains`. The first proves the basename guard, the second
    the directory guard (a system *name* would have been caught by the basename guard first, so the
    directory branch needed its own non-system-name case to be exercised at all).
  - **Real run**: `change:` ×4 (password file 0600, keychain created, identity imported, search-list
    entry added) then `change: verified codesign signs with 'MOSS Capture Local Signing'`, exit 0.
    The search-list `rollback:` line was printed **before** `security list-keychains -s` ran.
  - **Search list preserved, not replaced**: the three pre-existing entries survive in their original
    order and the MOSS keychain is appended fourth; `moss_entries=1` after the idempotent re-run, so
    the append cannot duplicate. Default keychain still `login.keychain-db`.
  - **Certificate is what was planned** (read back out of the keychain, not assumed from argv):
    self-signed `CN=MOSS Capture Local Signing`, RSA 2048 / sha256WithRSAEncryption,
    `X509v3 Basic Constraints: critical CA:FALSE`, `Key Usage: critical Digital Signature`,
    `Extended Key Usage: critical Code Signing`, notBefore 2026-07-28 05:05:08 GMT →
    notAfter 2036-07-25 05:05:08 GMT.
  - **The PRD's "DR unchanged across a rebuild" property, measured at the identity level.** Two
    *different* binaries (`/usr/bin/true`, `/bin/echo`) signed with the same `--identifier` both
    emit `designated => identifier "com.alphasight.moss.capture" and certificate leaf =
    H"e118d874377746c4bd25beb8252bb84302b73e72"`, byte-identical, while their `CDHash` values differ
    (`acf92317…` vs `4f4391fc…`) — so the DR is demonstrably not cdhash-derived. Re-signing changed
    bytes at the *same path* (a stand-in for a rebuild) leaves the DR byte-identical. The `H"…"`
    value equals `shasum -a 1` of the certificate's DER, computed independently from
    `security find-certificate | openssl x509 -outform DER` → `e118d874377746c4bd25beb8252bb84302b73e72`.
    Ad-hoc contrast measured in the same session: `codesign --sign -` on the same two binaries gives
    `designated => cdhash H"…" or cdhash H"…"` with different hashes per binary — which is exactly
    the TCC-grant-invalidating behavior the tool's header says a certificate identity avoids.
  - **Never gate on `find-identity`, reproduced on m4mbp**: `security find-identity -v -p codesigning`
    reports `1 valid identities found` and lists only the pre-existing `LiveTranscribe Local Dev` —
    the MOSS identity is absent from that list while `codesign` signs with it successfully. The
    iteration-9 MacStudio finding holds on this host too.
  - **No trust change**: the MOSS certificate is in neither the user nor the admin trust-settings
    domain (`security dump-trust-settings` / `-d` → `moss_in_*_trust_settings=0`). The three certs
    that do carry user trust settings (`LiveTranscribe Local Dev`, `Ga0-Alienware-RTX4070Ti`,
    `Ga0-RTX4090`) are pre-existing and unrelated. No SIP, Gatekeeper or global trust change.
  - **Idempotence**: a second run printed `unchanged: codesign already signs with 'MOSS Capture Local
    Signing' …` plus the same eight `evidence:` lines, no `change:` line, and left the password
    file's inode/size/mtime and the keychain's inode untouched.
  - **Rollback rehearsed for real, then re-applied.** `security delete-keychain … && rm -f <password>`
    removed both files and restored the search list to exactly the three pre-existing entries
    (`moss_entries=0`, default keychain untouched); re-running the tool from that clean state
    rebuilt everything with `change:` ×4 and exit 0.
  - **Secret hygiene**: the keychain password appears in no shell history (`.zsh_history`,
    `.bash_history` → 0), in no file under `~/Library/Logs`, and in no process argv
    (`ps_hits_excluding_self=0`; the first measurement's `ps_hits=1` was my own `grep -F "$P"`
    matching its own command line, not a leak). Its only occurrence on disk is the 0600 file inside
    a 0700 directory. No token, payload or pin is involved in E1.
  - **Checkout undisturbed**: m4mbp still `f9285d69ed7bcc592bb41b3dcdf29e3221968f44`, `dirty=0`.
- Learned:
  1. **The signing identity is not reproducible, and that changes when the rollback is free.** The
     rehearsal minted a new RSA key, so the leaf moved `8c99754e…` → `ef8fa542…` and the DR with it.
     The lab bundle's identity is a function of its inputs; this one is a function of a stored
     private key. So E1's rollback is free **only until E2b signs the installed bundle** — after E3
     it would destroy the human's TCC grants. Recorded on the E1/E2b candidates, not just here.
  2. **A fresh SSH session finds the keychain locked**, even though `set-keychain-settings` disabled
     the auto-lock timeout: `security show-keychain-info` answers `User interaction is not allowed`
     and `codesign --sign` fails `errSecInternalComponent`. The unlock state is per security session,
     not a keychain setting. My first DR probe failed this way and looked like a broken identity;
     the tool's own probe and `build-app.sh:186-191` both unlock first, so nothing in the pipeline is
     affected — but any hand-run `codesign` from a new session must unlock, and that is now in the
     Validation section.
  3. **A guard that shadows another guard is not exercised by the obvious input.** `System.keychain`
     tests the basename branch, not the system-directory branch, because the basename check runs
     first; only a non-system *name* in `/Library/Keychains` reaches the directory branch. Worth
     remembering for the other tools' refusal rehearsals.
  4. **Exit codes measured through a pipe are the pipe's.** The first refusal rehearsal reported
     `rc=0` because `$?` followed `tail`, not the script; re-measured without a pipe both refusals
     are exit 1. Never record a refusal's exit code from a pipeline.
- Blocker: none new. E3's TCC clicks remain the one irreducible human step.
- Next: E2b — run the reviewed `macos/scripts/build-app.sh` then `install-app.sh` on m4mbp to build,
  sign and install `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app` plus `mtd-capture`. Record the rollback before
  mutating (`install-app.sh` moves a replaced bundle to `<installed>.backup-<utc>`; on a first
  install the rollback is `rm -rf /Applications/MOSSCapture.app` plus the CLI path). Expect the
  bundle DR to be `identifier "com.alphasight.moss.capture" and certificate leaf = H"e118d874…"`,
  identifier `com.alphasight.moss.capture`, and `keychain-access-groups` **absent** from the embedded
  entitlements. Check whether `~/.local/bin` is on m4mbp's PATH first and pass `--bin-dir` if not,
  rather than editing a shell profile. Build both Swift products with **separate** `--product`
  invocations. State C4 review note (a) when `build-app.sh` unlocks the keychain with the password
  on argv. After this step the E1 rollback stops being free.

## Iteration 22 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 14) - E2b: build, sign and install the app on m4mbp
- Target: Phase E candidate E2b - run the reviewed `macos/scripts/build-app.sh` then
  `macos/scripts/install-app.sh` on m4mbp so `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app` and `mtd-capture` exist,
  signed by the E1 identity. Highest-leverage open candidate: it closes the PRD's "Signed app
  installed" acceptance clause outright, and E3 (the human TCC clicks) has nothing to click on until
  an installed bundle exists.

### Rollback recorded BEFORE any mutation (PRD "Reversibility")
- Pre-mutation state on m4mbp, measured read-only this iteration:
  - checkout HEAD `f9285d69ed7bcc592bb41b3dcdf29e3221968f44`, tree `815f23b0d7cd0c3f...`, `dirty_lines=0`
