    `~/.local/bin/mtd-capture` **absent**; `macos/MOSSCapture/.build` **absent** (no build output at all)
  - `/Applications` is `drwxrwxr-x root:admin` and `ga0` is in `admin`, so the install needs no sudo
  - reviewed tools present and executable: `build-app.sh` sha256 `74b26414ef0b2902...`,
    `install-app.sh` sha256 `0d59c6c78bbfd516...`, `moss-tool-lib.sh` sha256 `087793c68214d75d...`
    (non-executable by design)
  - E1 identity intact: `moss-signing.keychain-db` present, password file 0600, keychain fourth in the
    user search list after the three pre-existing entries, default keychain still `login.keychain-db`
  - Swift 6.3.3 / swift-driver 1.148.6, macOS 26.5.2, 81 GiB free, no MOSSCapture process running
- Rollback, printed by the tools themselves before their first mutation:
    rm -rf '/Users/ga0/Desktop/AI_Projects/Github_Projects/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize/macos/MOSSCapture/.build/product'
    rm -rf '/Applications/MOSSCapture.app' && rm -f '/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture'
  This is a **first** install, so no backup path is created and the second command is complete on its
  own. The full build-output rollback is `rm -rf .../macos/MOSSCapture/.build` (the whole scratch
  directory is absent right now and is gitignored).
- **After this step the E1 rollback stops being free.** `security delete-keychain` would mint a new
  leaf on the next bootstrap, changing the designated requirement of any rebuilt bundle - and from E3
  on that costs the human's TCC grants. E1 rollback is therefore retired as an option here.
- Nothing outside those three paths is touched: no keychain change, no search-list change, no shell
  profile edit, no trust/SIP/Gatekeeper change.

### Applied
- Files: `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}` only. **No tracked file changed** — the merge
  freeze means the feature branch may carry only Ralph evidence, and E2b is a host-state change.
- Validation, in order:
  - **Both dry runs mutate nothing**: `build-app.sh --dry-run` printed 7 `plan:` lines + `rollback:`,
    `install-app.sh --dry-run` printed 5 `plan:` lines + `rollback:`, both exit 0, and afterwards
    `.build`, `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app` and `~/.local/bin/mtd-capture` were all still absent.
  - **Build (release) green**: 11.0 s including signing, **0 `warning:` lines** in the whole swift
    build stderr, exit 0, `change:` ×3 (`composed`, `dropped keychain-access-groups`, `signed`).
  - **The signature was read back independently of the tool's own evidence**: `codesign -dvvv` →
    `Identifier=com.alphasight.moss.capture`, `TeamIdentifier=not set`, `flags=0x10000(runtime)`;
    `codesign -d -r-` → `designated => identifier "com.alphasight.moss.capture" and certificate
    leaf = H"e118d874377746c4bd25beb8252bb84302b73e72"`; the CLI is a separate identifier
    `com.alphasight.moss.capture.cli` under the same leaf. That `H"…"` was recomputed from scratch as
    `security find-certificate -c 'MOSS Capture Local Signing' | openssl x509 -outform DER |
    shasum -a 1` → `e118d874377746c4bd25beb8252bb84302b73e72`, i.e. the DR really does name the E1
    leaf and not something the tool asserted.
  - **B5's entitlement drop fires on this host**: the embedded entitlements of the installed bundle
    are exactly `{com.apple.security.device.audio-input: true}`; `keychain-access-groups` is absent
    from the signature while the tracked `Resources/MOSSCapture.entitlements` still declares it.
    Info.plist carries both usage strings and `CFBundleExecutable=MOSSCaptureApp`.
  - **PRD clause "the designated requirement … is unchanged across a rebuild" — proven against a
    genuinely different binary.** `rm -rf .build` + full rebuild (8.6 s, 0 warnings) produced
    `built_app_sha256 5bf01255… → ad83c0f8…` and `bundle_digest 3391655e… → e20d36f3…`, while the DR
    came out **byte-identical**. A third from-scratch build changed the bytes again
    (`bundle_digest 385a1930…`, CDHash `02683678…`) with the same DR.
  - **Idempotence of both tools**: a second `build-app.sh` printed `unchanged:` and left the app
    executable's, the CLI's and the evidence file's inode/mtime/size untouched; a second
    `install-app.sh` printed two `unchanged:` lines and **preserved the installed inode**
    (`211501958 → 211501958`) — the property the TCC grants depend on.
  - **Install green**: `change: installed /Applications/MOSSCapture.app` + `change: installed
    /Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture`, `backup_bundle=none` / `backup_cli=none` (first install),
    `codesign --verify --strict --deep` → `valid on disk` and `satisfies its Designated Requirement`
    for the bundle, strict-verify green for the CLI. Installed digest == source digest.
  - **First-install rollback rehearsed for real, then re-applied.** The recorded command verbatim
    (`rm -rf '/Applications/MOSSCapture.app' && rm -f '…/mtd-capture'`) returned both paths to
    absent with the other 58 `/Applications` entries untouched; re-installing from the same build
    output reproduced the **same CDHash** `c7c6a6aa…` and the same DR (a new inode, as expected).
  - **Replacement/backup path rehearsed for real** — the path every post-E3 rebuild will take.
    Installing build #3 over build #2 moved the old bundle to
    `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app.backup-20260728T051255Z` (still `codesign --verify --strict`
    clean, still carrying build #2's CDHash `c7c6a6aa…`), printed its `rollback:` line **before** the
    move, and did **not** print the "designated requirement changes on install" warning — correctly,
    since only the bytes changed. Applying that printed rollback verbatim restored build #2's exact
    CDHash. Settled by re-installing build #3 so the install matches the build output.
  - **Installed CLI behaves**: `--help` prints the one-line usage (rc=64), `status` answers
    `{"ok":false}` (rc=70) with no helper running, and neither call created
    `~/Library/Application Support/MOSSCapture` — B1's side-effect-free construction holds for the
    installed binary, not just the test one. `zsh -lic 'command -v mtd-capture'` →
    `/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture`.
  - **Secret hygiene, C4 review note (a) discharged with a measurement.** `build-app.sh` really does
    unlock with `-p "$(cat …)"`, so the random keychain password is briefly on argv. After the run:
    `ps` hits excluding self **0**, `.zsh_history` **0**, `.bash_history` **0**, files under
    `~/Library/Logs` **0**, build output **0**, installed bundle + CLI **0**; the password file is
    still `-rw-------`. No token, pin or pairing payload is involved in E2b at all.
  - **Nothing else moved**: m4mbp checkout still `f9285d69…` with `dirty_lines=0`; the keychain
    search list still has its four entries in order and the default keychain is still
    `login.keychain-db`; zero `*.backup-*` / `*.incoming-*` paths left behind (the CLI backup the
    replacement rehearsal created was removed — it is an artifact of this iteration).
- Learned:
  1. **SwiftPM release builds are not byte-reproducible on this host, and that is exactly why the
     certificate identity matters.** Three from-scratch builds of an unchanged checkout gave three
     different binaries and three different bundle digests, but one DR. So `install-app.sh`'s
     byte-identical shortcut — the one that preserves the inode — will **never** fire after a real
     rebuild; every rebuild-install is a replacement. The grants survive anyway because macOS keys
     them on the DR, which is the property that was measured. Recorded on the installed-app block so
     a later iteration does not read a `change: installed` line as a regression.
  2. **The replacement path leaves a CLI backup nobody cleans up.** `install-app.sh` backs both
     artifacts up, but only the bundle backup is conspicuous; `~/.local/bin/mtd-capture.backup-<utc>`
     sat there until I removed it. Worth remembering for F4's rollback rehearsal, which will produce
     one of each.
  3. **A tool's own evidence line can be true and still misleading.** `bin_dir_on_path=false` is
     correct for the non-interactive SSH `PATH` the tool ran under, and wrong about the human, whose
     `.zshrc` puts `~/.local/bin` on `PATH`. The B5 residue said to pass `--bin-dir` if the default
     was not on PATH; measuring the *interactive* shell instead showed the default was right and no
     profile edit was needed. Measure the environment the claim is about.
  4. **A "did the warning fire?" check has to be run as its own observation.** I grepped the DR-change
     warning out of a *second* invocation, which by then took the `unchanged:` path — so a 0 there
     would have been vacuous either way. It is only meaningful because the first invocation's full
     output was captured and shows the `change:` lines without it. (Same class of error as the
     iteration-21 pipe/exit-code slip: measure the run you mean.) A CDHash comparison earlier in the
     iteration was vacuous for the same reason — `codesign -dv` does not print `CDHash=`, only
     `-dvvv` does — so that comparison was discarded and re-measured, not reported.
- Blocker: none new. E3's TCC clicks remain the one irreducible human step, and it is now the only
  thing standing between the loop and Phase F.
- Next: **E3 — the human TCC step**, or **D4 immediately before it**. The two are coupled by a hard
  300 s window: `live_auth.PAIRING_TTL_SECONDS = 300`, so the payload minted on the host by
  `ops/live-pair.sh --url https://127.0.0.1:7861 --cert <live.crt>` must be consumed by
  `/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture pair --server https://100.64.0.8:7861` on m4mbp inside five
  minutes, and the `payload:` line is never redirected to a file. Only after pairing does the app
  have anything to start. The exact click sequence for the operator (Microphone, then System Audio
  Recording, both under System Settings → Privacy & Security) must be recorded as a blocker rather
  than retried — TCC cannot be scripted. Watch M36 on this host: the system-tap prompt is triggered
  on the control thread and has never been observed on a machine that actually shows the prompt.

## Iteration 23 (run 20260728-023004-98501, its iteration 15) - E3: record the human TCC blocker with a verified runbook, and establish read-only grant verification
- Target: Phase E candidate E3, the human TCC step. The PRD's own constraint decides the shape of
  this iteration: "TCC cannot be scripted... the loop must record the exact click sequence in
  progress.txt as a blocker and move to another candidate - never retry, never attempt to write the
  TCC database." Every other open candidate (F1-F3) is downstream of the grants, and F4's rollback
  rehearsal is explicitly last, so the highest-leverage work available is to make the human step
  succeed on the first attempt: derive its exact commands and click order from the reviewed source,
  and find a way to VERIFY the result without writing anything.
- Nothing was mutated. Every command this iteration was read-only: three SSH probes of m4mbp, three
  HTTP probes of the two services, and local reads of the reviewed source at f9285d6. No process was
  started, no app launched, no payload minted, no service restarted, no file written on either host.
  The one remote artifact created (a mktemp dir holding a csreq blob) was removed in the same
  command and its removal was asserted (`cleaned=yes`).
- Files: `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}` only. No tracked file changed - the merge
  freeze allows only Ralph evidence on this branch.

### THE BLOCKER: E3 requires physical GUI clicks on m4mbp
Two system alerts must be answered by a human at the machine. They cannot be scripted, and the loop
will not retry, launch the app, or touch the TCC database. Everything below is the operator's
sequence, in order, with the commands verified against the reviewed source and the installed binary.

**Preconditions, all re-asserted read-only this iteration:**
- `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app` installed (`drwxr-xr-x ga0:staff`, Jul 28 01:12), CLI at
  `/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture` (0755), no leftover `*.backup-*` of either.
- No `MOSSCaptureApp` process, no `/tmp/moss-capture-501/control.sock`, no
  `~/Library/Application Support/MOSSCapture` - nothing has been launched or paired.
- m4mbp checkout `f9285d69ed7bcc592bb41b3dcdf29e3221968f44`, `dirty_lines=0`.
- `stat -f "%Su" /dev/console` -> `ga0`: a GUI session is logged in, so the prompts have somewhere
  to appear. (Over SSH alone they would have nowhere.)
- Services up: batch `http://192.168.68.38:7860/` **200**, live `https://100.64.0.8:7861/live`
  **200**, `https://100.64.0.8:7861/api/live/descriptor` **200**.
- TCC baseline: **zero** rows for `%moss%` in `kTCCServiceMicrophone` or `kTCCServiceAudioCapture` -
  both lanes undetermined.

**Step 1 - launch the app from the GUI session.** Finder -> `/Applications` -> double-click
`MOSSCapture`; or in a GUI Terminal `open -a /Applications/MOSSCapture.app`.
*Nothing will appear on screen and that is correct*: `Info.plist` sets `LSUIElement=true`, so there
is no window, no Dock icon and no menu-bar item. Confirm with `pgrep -x MOSSCaptureApp` and
`ls -l /tmp/moss-capture-$(id -u)/control.sock`.
**Do not** run `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app/Contents/MacOS/MOSSCaptureApp` from a shell: TCC would
attribute the grant to the terminal instead of the bundle, and a DR-keyed grant on the terminal does
nothing for the app. **Do not** expect the CLI to launch it - see the finding below.

**Step 2 - trigger the prompts with one `start`.** In a GUI Terminal:
    /Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture start
The command will appear to hang. That is expected, not a fault (see finding 4).

**Alert 1 - System Audio Recording. This one appears FIRST.** It names *MOSSCapture* and shows the
`NSAudioCaptureUsageDescription` string "MOSS captures system audio only after the user starts a
local live session." -> click **Allow** (the other button is Don't Allow; exact macOS 26 wording of
this particular alert is not something this iteration could observe, so match on the app name and the
usage string, not on a button caption).
**Alert 2 - Microphone.** It shows "MOSS captures microphone audio only after the user starts a
local live session." -> click **Allow**.
Answer Alert 1 before Alert 2 - the source cannot present them in the other order, and until Alert 1
is answered nothing else on the control channel will respond.

**If no alert appears, or a lane was previously denied:** System Settings -> Privacy & Security ->
**Microphone** -> enable **MOSSCapture**; then System Settings -> Privacy & Security ->
**System Audio Recording** -> enable **MOSSCapture**. macOS will offer to quit and reopen the app;
after that, relaunch it (step 1) and re-run step 2.

**Step 3 - the loop verifies, read-only.** The two commands are in context.md's Validation section.
The clause is green when
`select service,client,client_type,auth_value from access where client="com.alphasight.moss.capture"`
prints exactly:
    kTCCServiceAudioCapture|com.alphasight.moss.capture|0|2
    kTCCServiceMicrophone|com.alphasight.moss.capture|0|2
and the csreq decodes to
`identifier "com.alphasight.moss.capture" and certificate leaf = H"e118d874377746c4bd25beb8252bb84302b73e72"`.
The PRD additionally requires `mtd-capture status` to report both lanes active, which needs a paired
session, i.e. step 4.

**Step 4 - pair (D4), now decoupled from the clicks.** On the host, inside one 300 s window:
    ops/live-pair.sh --url https://127.0.0.1:7861 --cert "$HOME/.local/share/moss-transcribe-diarize/live/live.crt"
then on m4mbp, in a GUI Terminal:
    /Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture pair --server https://100.64.0.8:7861
    <paste the payload>  then press Ctrl-D
Payload on **stdin**, never on argv and never redirected to a file - that keeps it out of the shell
history as well as out of `ps`. Then `mtd-capture start` again for the real session.

### Findings that changed the runbook (all derived from the reviewed source at f9285d6)
1. **The TCC grants do NOT have to happen inside D4's five-minute pairing window.**
   `CaptureController.start` (`CaptureController.swift:230-246`) requires only
   `loadControlSecret() != nil` - which the app writes for itself at launch - and calls
   `source.start` at `:242`, *before* the first publish at `:250`. Pairing is needed only by that
   publish. So an **unpaired** `start` still raises both prompts; the publish then fails
   `missingCaptureBearer`, which `CaptureFrameRetryPolicy` classes as not retryable, so the
   controller unwinds the source and throws - but the user's two decisions are already durable,
   because TCC records the click, not the capture outcome. Both iteration 22's "Next" note and the
   old E3 candidate asserted the opposite coupling. Splitting them removes the worst failure mode of
   the human step: a mis-timed prompt answer can no longer burn a minted payload.
   **Derived, not observed** - E3 itself is the confirmation; if the unpaired `start` behaves
   differently, fall back to the coupled sequence (mint, pair, then start once).
2. **Prompt order is fixed by the source, not by chance.** `NativeDualCaptureSource.start` admits
   system audio first (`:191`), microphone second (`:192`). System audio has no request API -
   `AudioHardwareCreateProcessTap` inside `SystemAudioTap.start` *is* the request, called inline on
   the control thread - while the microphone is the asynchronous
   `AVCaptureDevice.requestAccess(for: .audio)` that leaves the lane `pending`. So System Audio
   Recording is always the first alert, and it is the only one that can block.
3. **The CLI cannot launch the app on this host, and nothing says so anywhere.**
   `NSWorkspaceLaunchServicesCaptureAppLauncher.launch()` (`CaptureCommandLine.swift:69`) reads
   `MOSS_CAPTURE_APP_URL` and throws `launchServicesUnavailable` when it is unset. Nothing in the
   product, in `install-app.sh` or in a shell profile sets it - the only occurrences in the tree are
   two test fixtures and one `env.pop` in `tests/live_integration_replay.py`. That is exactly why
   the installed CLI answered `{"ok":false}` `rc=70` in iteration 22: not "no helper running" as
   recorded, but "no helper running **and** no way to start one". Had the operator been handed
   `mtd-capture pair` as the first command, it would have failed with an opaque `{"ok":false}` and
   burned the 300 s payload. The runbook therefore starts with an explicit GUI launch.
4. **A hung `start` is the expected appearance of an unanswered prompt.**
   `UnixDomainControlServer.serve()` is a serial accept loop (`CaptureSecurity.swift:897-902`) and
   `UnixDomainControlClient` sets no `SO_RCVTIMEO` (no timeout symbol exists in that file). So while
   the tap call waits on the alert, `start` blocks with no timeout and every other `mtd-capture`
   command - `status` included - queues behind it. This is M36's consequence made concrete: a hung
   `status` is not evidence the app died. Diagnose with `pgrep`/`sample`, never by killing the app.
5. **The pairing payload is a stdin argument.** `CaptureCommandLine.swift:114-130`: `pair` takes
   `--server` from argv and the payload from `input.readAll()`, with empty stdin -> `rc=65`. Pasting
   it into an interactive `mtd-capture pair` and pressing Ctrl-D satisfies the PRD's secret-hygiene
   clause for free - no argv, no file, no history entry.

### The verification finding: TCC state is readable read-only, and the grant carries the DR
- `~/Library/Application Support/com.apple.TCC/TCC.db` on m4mbp is `-rw-r--r-- ga0:staff`, 163840
  bytes, and opens over plain SSH **without** Full Disk Access. Proven non-vacuously before any
  conclusion was drawn from it: `select count(*) from access` -> **320**, and the service histogram
  lists 20 services. (A zero-row answer and a failed open look identical; iteration 22's learning 4
  is exactly this class of error, so the reader was proven first.)
- The service names are read off that live table, not guessed: Microphone is
  `kTCCServiceMicrophone` (21 rows) and **System Audio Recording is `kTCCServiceAudioCapture`**
  (3 rows). `auth_value` 2 = allowed, 0 = denied, both present on this host
  (`com.openai.chat|2` vs `com.google.antigravity|0`, `com.microsoft.rdc.macos|0`). `client_type`
  0 = bundle identifier, 1 = absolute path.
- **The csreq column stores the designated requirement.** Each row carries a `FADE0C00` code
  requirement blob; `sqlite3 … writefile()` + `csreq -r <file> -t` decodes it. Decoded on the
  `com.livetranscribe.app` precedent - a locally-signed app already holding **both** grants on this
  same machine - it reads
  `identifier "com.livetranscribe.app" and certificate leaf = H"51e289ba70a36bcdd063f1efa660d56aebd83da7"`,
  80 bytes. That is byte-for-byte the *form* of the E1 DR. So after E3 the moss rows must decode to
  `identifier "com.alphasight.moss.capture" and certificate leaf = H"e118d874377746c4bd25beb8252bb84302b73e72"`,
  and that decode is the **direct observation** - in TCC's own storage - that the grants key on the
  DR rather than the cdhash. Until now iterations 21/22 could only argue that link from the DR's
  stability across three byte-different builds; this makes it checkable.
  It also means the precedent app proves the *whole scheme* works on this host: a self-signed
  local identity can hold System Audio Recording here.
- Consequence for the loop: the PRD's "Permissions granted" clause becomes a two-command read-only
  check the loop can run itself the moment the operator reports back, with no TCC write, no
  `tccutil`, and no reliance on the operator's description of what they clicked.
- Validation of this iteration's own claims: the decode command was run for real (output above), the
  reader was proven with a row count, the temp directory was removed and its absence asserted, and
  the three service probes returned 200/200/200. `git status --porcelain` on m4mbp still 0 lines.
- Learned:
  1. **"Record the blocker" is not the same as "stop".** The PRD says record the click sequence and
     move on. The useful work was not writing the sentence "a human must click Allow" - it was
     reading the source hard enough to find that the click sequence is System-Audio-then-Microphone,
     that the CLI cannot launch the app, and that the clicks are not actually bound to the 300 s
     window. A blocker is worth as much as the runbook attached to it.
  2. **A read-only path to the evidence often exists where a write path is forbidden.** The PRD bans
     touching the TCC database; it does not ban reading it, and the user-domain file turned out to be
     world-readable. That converts the one acceptance clause the loop cannot cause into one it can
     still verify. Worth asking of every "unscriptable" gate.
  3. **An empty query result is not evidence until the query is proven to work.** Same shape as
     iteration 22's vacuous CDHash comparison and iteration 21's exit-code-through-a-pipe slip: the
     `%moss%` baseline of zero rows means "undetermined" only because `count(*)` returned 320 first.
  4. **A tool's error string can hide a missing precondition.** `{"ok":false}` `rc=70` was recorded
     in iteration 22 as "no helper running", which is true but incomplete - the CLI also had no way
     to start one, because `MOSS_CAPTURE_APP_URL` is unset everywhere. The generic error made the
     two cases indistinguishable from the outside.
- Blocker: **E3's two GUI clicks on m4mbp** - the exact sequence is above. Nothing else in the loop
  is blocked on input the loop cannot obtain; F1-F4 are blocked only on E3's outcome, which is a
  human action rather than missing information.
- Next: after the operator confirms both grants, run the two read-only TCC verifications to close the
  PRD's "Permissions granted" clause, then D4 (mint on the host, pair on m4mbp inside 300 s, confirm
  `live/live-auth.json` appears 0600 on ext4 and no payload reached history/logs/argv/journal), then
  F1's 60 s canary. If the operator is unavailable for a long stretch, the one candidate that needs
  no human and no TCC is F4's rollback rehearsal - but it takes the live service down and must not be
  started while a pairing or canary window is open, so prefer waiting.

[loop 20260728-023004-98501] blocked at iteration 15

## Supervisor diagnosis - ATS blocks the signed app bundle (2026-07-28, operator-attended)
- Context: not a loop iteration. The operator ran the E3 runbook, pairing failed, and this entry
  records the diagnosis performed with them. No tracked product source was changed.
- Trigger: the runbook's step order was wrong. `captureConfiguration(from:)`
  (`CaptureSecurity.swift:847-862`) throws `missingCaptureConfiguration` before
  `CaptureController.start` runs, so an unpaired `start` can never raise the TCC prompts. Iteration
  23's finding 1 predicted the opposite and flagged itself "Derived, not observed" - the fallback it
  named (pair first, then start) is correct and is now the order in Phase G's runbook.
- Defect A, fixed procedurally, still a product bug (candidate G2): pasting the payload and pressing
  Return before Ctrl-D yields `invalidPinnedHash`. `CapturePairingPayload.init` never trims, so the
  pin field is 65 bytes against a strict `count == 64` check. Measured: 64 without the newline, 65
  with. No test covers whitespace. Operator workaround: `IFS= read -r payload` then
  `printf '%s' "$payload" | mtd-capture pair --server ...` - payload stays off argv, out of `ps`
  and out of shell history.
- Defect B (candidate G1), the real blocker. After the trim, pairing failed `control_failed`, and
  the server never logged `POST /api/live/pairings` at all. Isolated by A/B on m4mbp, each step
  measured rather than argued:
  - bare binary, pinned GET to `https://100.64.0.8:7861/api/live/descriptor` -> **200**
  - real `URLSessionCapturePairingExchangeAdapter` (throwaway package depending on the repo by
    path, so the repo was untouched) -> **paired**, session + bearer + view token returned
  - unsigned `.build/debug/MOSSCaptureApp` + `mtd-capture pair` -> **{"ok":true}**
  - installed `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app` -> **control_failed**
  - minimal ad-hoc `.app` bundle wrapping the same probe binary -> **-1200 / -9802**, after the
    delegate had printed `pin MATCH -> accepting`
  - that same bundle + `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` -> **200**
  Root cause: ATS applies to `.app` bundles and not to bare binaries, and overrides the pinning
  delegate's acceptance because the leaf is not CA-chained. Ruled out first, each by direct test:
  wrong pin (m4mbp, served leaf and payload all `a35ca9fc...b680ff`), network path (`/live` and
  descriptor both 200 from m4mbp), TCC/Local Network (fails identically under the terminal's
  identity; not listed in Local Network settings), hardened runtime (ad-hoc + runtime -> 200),
  entitlements (ad-hoc + runtime + `audio-input` -> 200), sandboxing (no `app-sandbox` embedded),
  application firewall (disabled) and network filters (only a Logitech HID extension).
- Defect C (candidate G3): `sanitizedControlError` reduces every unrecognised error to
  `control_failed` and `MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift` logs nothing at all. The `-1200`/`-9802` was
  available from the first failed attempt and was discarded at the boundary. This is why B took
  hours instead of minutes.
- Why no gate caught B: `_private_non_loopback_ipv4()` (`tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py:690`)
  returns MacStudio's 192.168.x address, which ATS exempts as local networking. Production dials
  100.64.0.8 (Tailscale CGNAT), which ATS does not exempt. The tracer lists `100.64.0.0/10` among

  never takes.

## Iteration 1 (run 20260728-072601-15601) - G1: the ATS declaration, and the measurement that
## showed the planned regression test could never have caught it
- Target: candidate G1 from the prd.md amendment of 2026-07-28 - the App Transport Security
  declaration that lets the app's pinned live transport work from an `.app` bundle - together with
  G4's "tracer case bound to a 100.64.0.0/10 address", because a fix wants its gate in the same
  change.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Resources/Info.plist` (+16),
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/MTDCaptureCLITests/MTDCaptureCLITests.swift` (+30),
  `tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py` (+219/-6), `tests/test_macos_packaging_tools.py` (+7),
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`. First tracked product change since the keeper
  merge, authorized by the amendment and inside its four items.

### What was measured, before anything was fixed
The planned regression test was written first and it **passed red** - it could not see the bug. So
the next step was to find out why, with one disposable ad-hoc `.app`-bundled Swift probe in /tmp
whose URLSessionDelegate accepts any leaf (built, measured, deleted; nothing entered the repo).
Matrix on MacStudio (macOS 26.5.1; m4mbp is 26.5.2 and answered identically on the one row it
shares):
  | destination                        | bare binary | inside an `.app` |
  | remote 100.64.0.8   (tailnet)      | 200         | **-1200 / -9802** |
  | remote 192.168.68.38 (LAN)         | 200         | 200               |
  | this host's own 100.64.0.1         | 200         | 200               |
  | this host's own 192.168.68.36      | 200         | 200               |
Then the fix, on the same bundle, changing nothing else: `NSAppTransportSecurity =
{NSAllowsArbitraryLoads: true}` -> remote 100.64.0.8 answers **200**. That is the red/green pair
for G1, measured directly rather than argued.
Two things follow, and the second one contradicts the plan:
1. ATS applies to a bundle and not to a bare executable, and it overrides a delegate that has
   already accepted the leaf. Confirmed independently of m4mbp.
2. **The exemption is loopback + RFC 1918, not "the address range the tracer picks".** A server the
   tracer starts is on the tracer's host, so however its peer address is written the connection is
   routed over loopback and exempted - `100.64.0.1` (this host's own tailnet address) proves that
   directly. G4's premise, that binding `100.64.0.0/10` would reproduce the failure, is wrong. And
   the supervisor's diagnosis was only half right: remote RFC 1918 is exempt too, so even a second
   LAN host would not have caught this. **No single-host test can gate ATS.**

### What was built instead
- The fix: `Resources/Info.plist` declares `NSAppTransportSecurity = {NSAllowsArbitraryLoads: true}`
  and nothing else, with a comment carrying the failure signature and the reason a scoped
  `NSExceptionDomains` entry was rejected (it would bake one deployment's hostname into the shipped
  product; the exact-leaf pin is the security control by prd.md design).
  The sibling trap is called out in every gate: declaring `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` or either
  `NSAllowsArbitraryLoadsIn*` key makes the OS ignore `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads`.
- Three shape gates, since a behavioral one is impossible here:
  1. `MTDCaptureCLITests.testBundleDeclaresTheTransportExceptionThePinnedClientCannotWorkWithout` -
     **parses** the plist rather than substring-matching it, so the explanatory comment inside the
     plist cannot satisfy the assertion, and requires the key set to be exactly
     `["NSAllowsArbitraryLoads"]`.
  2. `test_macos_packaging_tools.py` - asserts it on the bundle `build-app.sh` *composes*, which is
     the artifact E2b installs, not on the tracked source.
  3. `_first_install_lab_bundle` - refuses to install a lab bundle whose product plist lacks it.
- The CGNAT tracer node was kept but renamed to what it can honestly prove:
  `test_bundled_app_on_the_production_address_range_still_refuses_an_unpinned_leaf`. Its docstring
  states that it does **not** observe ATS and why. What it does prove is the other half of the
  declaration's safety case: with ATS off the entire trust decision belongs to
  `PinnedCertificateURLSessionDelegate`, so the node pairs once honestly over the production
  address range and then serves a certificate the pairing payload's pin does not describe and
  requires refusal.
- One adjacent defect found and closed on the way: the lab bundle's reuse predicate keyed only on
  the built executable's Mach-O identity, so a product-Info.plist edit changed the installed bundle
  without triggering a reinstall - the tracer would have failed its own continuity assertions
  instead, and a passing run could have been driving a bundle the product no longer describes. The
  predicate now also keys on `product_info_plist_sha256`, recorded in the evidence file.

### Validation
- `python3 -m pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q` -> **4 passed** (was 3), 0 skips, 15 s.
- `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **132 passed / 0 failures** (was 131).
- `python3 -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **537 passed / 2 skipped / 368 subtests** in
  60.5 s (was 536; the 2 skips are the pre-existing Python-3.10 contract in test_large_upload.py).
- Both Swift products build from the tip, separate invocations, zero warnings.
- Phase A attempt-2 discriminator **10/10**; `leak-scan: clean`.
- Mutation rehearsals, each applied and reverted:
  1. `NSAppTransportSecurity` removed -> **4 nodes fail** (three tracer nodes through the lab-bundle
     install assert, plus the packaging node).
  2. `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` added beside it -> the **2 exact-shape nodes fail**; the tracer's
     behavioral nodes correctly stay green, since the OS-level consequence is invisible on one host.
  3. Phase two of the new node made to serve the very leaf its payload pins -> the refusal assertion
     fails with `exit=0 … "ok":true`, so the refusal in the real node is caused by the leaf
     mismatch and by nothing incidental.
- Nothing outside the repo was mutated: no service restarted, no host touched, no payload minted,
  no app installed. The /tmp probe and its certificates were deleted and their absence asserted.
  The m4mbp app is still the pre-fix build - this iteration did not rebuild or reinstall it.

### Learned
1. **A regression test that passes on the first run is a measurement, not a success.** Writing the
   planned test first is what surfaced that the plan's premise was wrong; had it been written after
   the fix it would have been recorded as proof and shipped a gate that gates nothing.
2. **Ask what the environment exempts, not only what the code does.** Two independent exemptions
   were in play (bundle-vs-bare and loopback/RFC1918-vs-everything else) and the second one is
   invisible to any test that starts its own server. The blind spot was not the tracer's address
   picker; it was that the tracer is its own peer.
3. **When a behavioral gate is impossible, say so in the test.** The honest move was to rename the
   node to what it proves and write the impossibility into its docstring and into context.md, so
   the next reader does not re-derive it or, worse, trust it.
- Blocker: unchanged and still the only one - E3's two GUI clicks on m4mbp. G1 does not need them;
  the corrected E3 order (pair first, then start, then the clicks) is in the supervisor entry above.
- Next: **G2** - trim the pairing payload in `CapturePairingPayload.init` with a regression test for
  the trailing-newline case that produced `invalidPinnedHash` (65 bytes against a strict 64 check);
  then **G3** - classify `URLError`/`NSError` into a typed non-secret shape and log unclassified
  control-channel failures, which is what would have shown `-1200/-9802` on the first attempt; then
  G4's gate and the single authorized merge. After the merge, E2b must be re-run on m4mbp so the
  installed app carries the fix - the shape gates prove the declaration ships, but only that host
  can prove the product path, because only it has a remote non-exempt peer.

## Iteration 2 (run 20260728-072601-15601) - G2: the pairing payload is trimmed, and the trimmed
## payload is the one that goes on the wire
- Target: candidate G2 from the prd.md amendment of 2026-07-28 - the pairing-payload whitespace
  defect that turned an operator's Return into `invalidPinnedHash`, an error blaming the server's
  certificate for a keystroke.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/CaptureSecurity.swift` (+31/-3),
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift` (+85),
  `tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py` (+6/-1), `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.

### The second half of the defect, found while fixing the first
Trimming in `CapturePairingPayload.init` alone would have been a half-fix.
`URLSessionCapturePairingExchangeAdapter.pair` parsed the payload for the pin and then put the
**raw stdin bytes** in the HTTP body (`String(decoding: pairingPayload, as: UTF8.self)`) - the
client validated one string and transmitted another. Read against the server: `live_auth.
_normalize_cert_sha256` does `.strip().lower()` on the pin field but nothing strips the prefix, so
a trailing newline would have been forgiven on the wire while a leading space would have come back
as 401, which the app surfaces only as `nonSuccessStatus(401)` - a second whitespace fault wearing
a second wrong error. The adapter now sends `parsedPayload.wireRepresentation`.
Three changes, all in one parse:
1. trim `.whitespacesAndNewlines` before splitting; whitespace-only input is `missingPairingPayload`;
2. whitespace that survives the trim is *inside* a field, so refuse it as `invalidPairingPayload`
   rather than letting it reach the strict 64-hex check and blame the certificate again;
3. `wireRepresentation` = `"mtd1.<secret>.<lowercased pin>"` is what is transmitted.
Safety of the trim was checked against the generator, not assumed: the secret is
`secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, no dot, no whitespace) and both sides lowercase the
pin, so canonicalization cannot eat a legitimate character.

### Validation
- `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **134 passed / 0 failures** (was 132).
- `python3 -m pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q` -> **4 passed**, 0 skips, 14.3 s.
- `python3 -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **537 passed / 2 skipped / 368 subtests** in
  60.9 s (unchanged count: the tracer node was strengthened in place, not added to).
- Both Swift products build, separate invocations, zero warnings.
- Phase A attempt-2 discriminator **10/10**; `leak-scan: clean`.
- Mutation rehearsals, each applied and reverted:
  1. wire form reverted to the raw bytes -> the body assertion fails on all four typed forms,
     showing `mtd1.secret.aaaa…\n` (and `\r\n`, and the indented form) actually on the wire.
  2. trim removed -> the trailing-newline node fails; through the **real process path** the tracer's
     `mtd-capture pair` answers `exit=70 {"ok":false,"error":"invalidPairingPayload"}`.
  3. trim *and* the internal-whitespace guard removed -> the same input reproduces the shipped
     signature exactly: `invalidPinnedHash`. The same mutation also crashed the whitespace node's
     stub client on `removeFirst` from an empty response queue - i.e. without the guard a payload
     with a space inside the secret really does reach the network.
- Nothing outside the repo was mutated: no host touched, no service restarted, no payload minted,
  no app rebuilt or reinstalled on m4mbp.

### Learned
1. **A canonicalization that does not reach the wire is not a fix.** The parse and the transmission
   have to be the same string; anywhere they diverge, the validation applies to a string the peer
   never sees. Grepping for the *other* use of the raw input was what turned a one-line trim into
   the actual fix.
2. **Check the tolerant peer before deciding what the fix must cover.** The server strips the pin
   and not the prefix, so trailing and leading whitespace fail in two different places with two
   different error shapes. Only reading `_normalize_cert_sha256` made that asymmetry visible.
3. **Put the operator's keystrokes into the existing real-process node instead of adding one.**
   Feeding the tracer's second pairing `payload + b"\n"` covers stdin -> UDS -> app -> HTTPS at zero
   extra runtime, and it is the node that proved mutation 2 for real rather than in a stub.
- Blocker: unchanged and still the only one - E3's two GUI clicks on m4mbp. G2 does not need them.
- Next: **G3** - classify `URLError`/`NSError` into a typed non-secret shape (domain + code, never a
  token or payload) and log unclassified control-channel failures in `MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift`,
  which is what would have shown `-1200/-9802` on the first attempt; then G4's full client gate and
  the single authorized merge through `merge-keeper.sh`. After the merge, re-run E2b on m4mbp so the
  installed app carries G1+G2+G3, then the corrected E3 order (pair first, then start, then clicks).

## Iteration 3 (run 20260728-072601-15601) - G3: a failure the control channel cannot name now
## says which failure it is, and the app records it
- Target: candidate G3 from the prd.md amendment of 2026-07-28 - `sanitizedControlError` mapped
  every unrecognised error to the bare string `control_failed` and `MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift`
  logged nothing at all, so the ATS block reached the operator as four words and survived a merge.
- Files: `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/CaptureSecurity.swift` (+148/-9),
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureCore/CaptureCommandLine.swift` (+1/-1),
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Sources/MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift` (+3),
  `macos/MOSSCapture/Tests/MOSSCaptureCoreTests/CaptureControllerTests.swift` (+307),
  `tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py` (+19), `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.

### What was built
`classifyControlError` replaces `sanitizedControlError` and returns `(name, detail)`. The four
typed families keep their exact `String(describing:)` names and get **no** detail - their cases
carry only what this process put in them, so the name already is the evidence, and a detail beside
one would be noise. Everything else keeps the bare `control_failed` it always had **plus** a
`ControlChannelErrorDetail{domain, code, underlyingCode?}` built from `error as NSError`. Building
it from the bridge rather than from a type list is the point: `URLError`, `POSIXError` and a bare
Swift struct all answer, so this is a backstop instead of another list the next unfamiliar error
falls off the end of. `underlyingCode` is `_kCFStreamErrorCodeKey` when non-zero, else
`NSUnderlyingError.code`, else absent - never zeroed, because zero is a code an error can have.
`UnixDomainControlServer` gained an optional `ControlChannelFailureLogging` and calls it **only**
for a detail-bearing failure; the app wires `OSLogControlChannelFailureLog()`, the CLI has no
control server. Unified logging is the only surface an `LSUIElement` app has - no window, and
Launch Services gives it no usable stderr:
  log show --predicate 'subsystem == "com.alphasight.moss.capture"' --last 30m
Everything that line marks `.public` is provably non-secret. The detail is numbers and a constant;
the command word is reduced to `ControlChannelCommands.all` or the literal `other`. That constant
also replaced the CLI's duplicate command literal, so the log's public vocabulary and the CLI's
accepted set are now one list rather than two that can drift.

### The distinction this buys, measured through the real process
The tracer's pin refusal answers `control_failed` + `{domain: NSURLErrorDomain, code: -999}` with
no `underlyingCode`. -999 is `NSURLErrorCancelled`: the pinning delegate cancelled the challenge,
so *this client* refused the leaf. The ATS block G1 fixed is -1200 with underlying -9802: the *OS*
refused the connection. Both were `control_failed` before this iteration, and telling them apart
is exactly the diagnosis nobody could make in E3. The tracer node was strengthened in place to
assert that signature rather than only `returncode != 0`.

### Validation
- `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **139 passed / 0 failures** (was 134).
- `python3 -m pytest tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py -q` -> **4 passed**, 0 skips, 14.9 s.
- `python3 -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **537 passed / 2 skipped / 368 subtests** in
  63.1 s (unchanged count: the tracer node was strengthened in place, not added to).
- Both Swift products from an **empty** scratch path, separate invocations: mtd-capture 8.2 s,
  MOSSCaptureApp 0.7 s, **zero warnings**.
- Phase A attempt-2 discriminator **10/10**; `leak-scan: clean`.
- Mutation rehearsals, each applied and reverted:
  1. default branch returns a nil detail -> **2 nodes / 7 assertions** fail across all three
     NSError shapes and the log node.
  2. the `failureLog?.recordUnclassifiedFailure` call removed -> the log node fails with 0 records
     against 2; the response node still passes, so the two halves are gated independently.
  3. the log emits the raw command instead of the bounded one -> the fixed-vocabulary node fails
     showing `control command status; token=sk-live-do-not-log …` in the emitted line.
  4. `domain = nsError.description` instead of `nsError.domain` -> the absence assertions fire with
     `https://100.64.0.8:7861/api/live/pairings` and the whole SSL message on the wire. That is the
     leak this shape exists to prevent, reproduced rather than argued.
- Nothing outside the repo was mutated: no host touched, no service restarted, no payload minted,
  no app rebuilt or reinstalled on m4mbp. Nothing was written to the unified log by the suite - the
  log node drives the injected `emit`, not `Logger`.

### Learned
1. **The classifier's default branch is where the diagnosis dies, and it is reachable by
   construction.** Four typed families were listed; everything the OS raises fell through. Building
   the fallback from the `NSError` bridge instead of from a fifth `case` is what stops the same
   hole reopening for the next unfamiliar error type.
2. **Deciding what is safe to log is easier than sanitising what was logged.** The protocol is
   handed a command word and a `ControlChannelErrorDetail` and nothing else, so an implementation
   cannot write a payload even by accident. Mutation 4 shows the alternative: one field widened
   from `domain` to `description` and the deployment's URL is on the wire.
3. **Bound the public vocabulary even where the input cannot currently be hostile.** An unknown
   command throws a named error and never reaches the log, so the `other` guard is unreachable
   today - and it is the guard that keeps the claim "everything public here is non-secret" true if
   that ever changes.
- Blocker: unchanged and still the only one - E3's two GUI clicks on m4mbp. G3 does not need them.
- Next: **G4** - the amendment's last item. Re-run the full client gate (both products from an
  empty scratch path, `swift test`, `pytest tests`, tracer, discriminator, leak-scan, clean tree)
  and perform the single authorized merge through `merge-keeper.sh`. Read that script first: it
  fences on a pre-merge `main` SHA and `main` is now `f9285d6`, so record what the fence requires
  and satisfy it honestly rather than raising `RALPH_MERGE_MAIN_BEFORE` to get past it. After the
  merge, re-run E2b on m4mbp so the installed app carries G1+G2+G3, then the corrected E3 order
  (pair first, then start, then the clicks), reading `log show --predicate 'subsystem ==
  "com.alphasight.moss.capture"'` if the rebuilt app still fails.

## run 20260728-072601 / iteration 4 - G4 client gate green; the merge fence measured
================================================================================

- Target: candidate 29 (**G4**), first half - re-run the full client gate for the amendment's fix
  cycle, review the merge payload against the amendment's scope, and establish exactly what
  `merge-keeper.sh` requires for the one authorized merge. **The merge itself was deliberately not
  performed**: a merge into `main` cannot be undone without the history rewriting the rules forbid,
  so the gate is recorded first, exactly as B6 preceded C4 for the first merge.
- Files: `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}` only. **No tracked product source changed**,
  no host touched, no service restarted, no app rebuilt or reinstalled on m4mbp.

### The gate, measured on MacStudio 2026-07-28 at product tree 23dc163 (tree clean before and after)
- Both Swift products from an **empty** scratch path (`mktemp -d`, verified 0 entries), separate
  invocations because `--product A --product B` silently builds only B:
  `mtd-capture` 8.48 s wall / 7.69 s build, `MOSSCaptureApp` 0.92 s / 0.61 s, **zero warnings**
  (a third invocation grepped for `warning` and counted 0).
- `swift test --package-path macos/MOSSCapture` -> **139 passed / 0 failures** in 0.96 s.
- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` ->
  **537 passed / 2 skipped / 368 subtests** in 59.2 s.
- The two skips were **named, not assumed**: `-rs` reports `tests/test_large_upload.py:155` and
  `:175`, both "Python 3.10 compatibility contract". Same pre-existing pair as every prior gate;
  **zero Darwin skips**.
- `tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py` alone -> **4 passed / 0 skips** in 14.7 s, including the
  `100.64.0.0/10` node that fails rather than skips without a CGNAT address on this host.
- Phase A attempt-2 discriminator -> **10/10 true**. `leak-scan: clean`.

### Pre-merge review of what this merge would carry
`git diff --stat main HEAD -- ':!scripts/ralph-afk'` is **eight files, +864/-19**:
`Resources/Info.plist` (G1), `CaptureSecurity.swift` (G2 trim + wire form, G3 classifier),
`MOSSCaptureApp/main.swift` + `CaptureCommandLine.swift` (G3), and four test files
(`CaptureControllerTests.swift`, `MTDCaptureCLITests.swift`, `tests/test_macos_packaging_tools.py`,
`tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py`). No `ops/`, no server source, no deployment template, no
packaging tool. That is strictly inside the amendment's four allowed items. The only other paths in
the diff are `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,prd.md,progress.txt}`.

### The fence: TWO blocking conditions, and the second is silent
The previous iteration's next-step note flagged one. A dry run found two.
1. `merge-keeper.sh:21` - `RALPH_MERGE_DRY_RUN=1 bash scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh` prints
   `ERROR: main moved from expected pre-merge SHA af3ac3667393a0411616f52f76339eff01dc13e2`, rc=1.
   That default is the *first* merge's pre-merge SHA; `main` is now `f9285d6`.
2. `merge-keeper.sh:27` - re-running the same dry run with
   `RALPH_MERGE_MAIN_BEFORE=f9285d69ed7bcc592bb41b3dcdf29e3221968f44` exits **rc=1 with no output
   whatsoever**. The cause: `git merge-base --is-ancestor main HEAD` is false, under `set -e`, with
   no message. `main` has never been an ancestor of the feature branch since the first merge -
   `f9285d6` *is* that merge commit and lives only on `main`, while its second parent `f400d426` is
   the feature-branch commit.
Had iteration 5 gone straight at the merge with only the SHA override, it would have hit a bare
rc=1 with nothing on stdout or stderr to explain it. Measuring the fence was the point of doing
this as its own iteration.

### Why fence 2 can be satisfied by history instead of by editing the check
`git rev-parse f400d426^{tree}` and `git rev-parse f9285d6^{tree}` are the **same object**,
`815f23b0d7cd0c3fd73226404036fdce49802da2`, and `git diff --stat f400d426 f9285d6` is empty. So
`main` carries **zero content** the feature branch lacks; only the merge commit object itself is
exclusive to it. Merging `main` into the feature branch is therefore a pure history join with a
provably empty diff, after which the ancestry fence is honestly true and the gate recorded here at
product tree `23dc163` still measures the merged tree. `git merge-tree --write-tree main HEAD`
already returns rc=0, so the merge is conflict-free.
Fence 1 gets the amendment's expected pre-merge SHA written **into the script**, not passed as a
command-line override: the guard then still fails on a *third* merge, and the reason this one was
allowed is reviewable in the diff rather than invisible in a shell invocation.

### Learned
1. **A guard that fails silently is a guard you have to run to understand.** Fence 2 is a bare
   `git` command under `set -e`; it is the only check in the script with no error message, and it
   is the one that was missed by reading. The merge iteration should give it one while it is
   editing the file anyway.
2. **"main is an ancestor" stops being true the moment main's tip is the merge commit.** The check
   was written for a first merge and silently assumes the feature branch is never merged. The
   property it actually protects - that the merge cannot drop content only `main` has - is provable
   here by tree identity, which is why the join is a no-op rather than a risk.
3. **Splitting gate from merge cost one iteration and bought the fence diagnosis.** The merge is
   the one irreversible action left on this branch; the suite is re-run inside the merge worktree
   anyway, so nothing was measured twice for free.
- Blocker: unchanged and still the only one - E3's two GUI clicks on m4mbp. G4 does not need them.
- Next: **finish G4 - the merge.** In one iteration, in this order: (1)
  `git merge --no-ff main` on the feature branch and assert `git diff --stat 23dc163 HEAD -- .` is
  empty; (2) edit `merge-keeper.sh:10` so `expected_main` defaults to
  `f9285d69ed7bcc592bb41b3dcdf29e3221968f44` with a comment citing the 2026-07-28 amendment, and
  give the ancestry fence an error message; (3) `RALPH_MERGE_DRY_RUN=1` dry run, then
  `bash scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh`; (4) record the feature tip and merge SHA and confirm
  `HEAD^2` equals the feature tip. Do not push in the same iteration. After the merge, re-run E2b
  on m4mbp so the installed app carries G1+G2+G3, then the corrected E3 order, reading
  `log show --predicate 'subsystem == "com.alphasight.moss.capture"'` if the rebuilt app still fails.

## run 20260728-072601 / iteration 5 - the amendment's one authorized merge, made
================================================================================

- Target: candidate 29 (**G4**), second half - perform the single follow-up keeper merge the
  2026-07-28 prd.md amendment authorizes. Gate was already green at product tree `23dc163`
  (iteration 4); this iteration only satisfied the two fences and ran `merge-keeper.sh`.
- Files: `scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh` (the fence edit), plus `context.md`/`progress.txt`.
  No tracked product source changed. No host touched, no service restarted, nothing pushed, no app
  rebuilt or reinstalled on m4mbp.

### Result
- Join commit `502a49ab464116dcded711235e075e7c858c1378` (`git merge --no-ff main`).
- Fence commit `23aabe640cc39d16a996e7d48e4fdf297bf4f51e`.
- **Merge `317df4d728b6765dbe365a3166158ba581299557`** on `main`; `main^1 = f9285d6…` (the first
  keeper merge), `main^2 = 23aabe6…` = the feature tip, asserted by the script and re-asserted by
  hand. `git diff 23aabe6 317df4d` is empty; both trees are `3b37815fd1ba68d83e8f2441f8d4d9a2778446a3`.
- `origin/main` deliberately left at `f9285d6` - publishing is its own iteration (candidate G5).

### The join was proven content-free BEFORE it was made
`git merge-tree --write-tree main HEAD` returned `5963a2b0afd362eaacf0e664cec0fe8f0e6e52ac`, which
is `git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}` - i.e. the merge's own result tree is the feature tree, so the join
could not carry content even in principle. Confirmed after: `git diff --stat 0b5383f HEAD -- .`
empty, `git diff --name-only 23dc163 HEAD -- ':!scripts/ralph-afk'` empty, and
`git merge-base --is-ancestor main HEAD` then true. main's tree and the merge base's tree were the
same object `815f23b0…`, as iteration 4 measured.

### The fence edit, and both halves rehearsed non-vacuously
- `expected_main` now defaults to `f9285d69…` **in the script**, with a comment citing the
  amendment. Not an env override: the guard stays live and its reason stays in the diff.
  *Rehearsed after the merge*: `RALPH_MERGE_DRY_RUN=1 bash scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh` now
  prints `ERROR: main moved from expected pre-merge SHA f9285d69…`, rc=1. **A third merge is
  refused**, which is the whole point of writing it down rather than overriding it.
- The ancestry fence was a bare `git merge-base --is-ancestor` under `set -e` that exited 1 printing
  nothing. It now names both SHAs and the fix. *Rehearsed* against a genuinely non-ancestor ref - a
  dangling `git commit-tree` object, so no ref was created or moved - via a sed'd copy of the script
  run with `RALPH_MERGE_DRY_RUN=1 RALPH_MERGE_ALLOW_DIRTY=1`: both lines printed, rc=1. The copy was
  deleted in the same command.

### Validation
- `RALPH_MERGE_DRY_RUN=1 bash scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh` -> `Keeper merge dry-run: PASS
  feature=23aabe64… main_before=f9285d69…`, rc=0.
- `bash scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh` -> `Keeper merge: PASS feature=23aabe64… main=317df4d7…`,
  rc=0, ~95 s. Inside its temp worktree it built both Swift products in separate invocations, then:
  `swift test` **139 passed / 0 failures**; `pytest tests` **537 passed / 2 skipped** in 67.4 s.
- Merged-tree measurements taken by hand in the first (killed) merge worktree, same staged merge:
  `swift test` 139 passed / 0 failures; `pytest tests` 537 passed / 2 skipped in 67.35 s;
  `tests/test_macos_uds_tracer.py` alone **4 passed / 0 skips** in 16.47 s.
  Logs: `/tmp/ralph-i5-swifttest.log`, `/tmp/ralph-i5-pytest.log`, `/tmp/ralph-i5-tracer.log`,
  `/tmp/ralph-i5-keeper.log`.
- Merge payload re-reviewed immediately before the merge: `git diff --stat main HEAD --
  ':!scripts/ralph-afk'` = the same eight files / +864/-19 as iteration 4. Against published
  `f9285d6` the entire delta is 12 files, and
  `git diff --name-only f9285d6 317df4d -- ':!macos' ':!scripts/ralph-afk' ':!tests/test_macos_*'`
  is **empty**: the server tree is byte-identical, so G5 can republish without a restart.
- Primary worktree left on `ralph/live-meeting-mvp`, clean, `git worktree list` back to one entry.

### The first attempt was killed at 10 minutes - what that cost and what it taught
The first `merge-keeper.sh` invocation ran in a time-capped foreground shell and was killed at 10
min. Facts, not inference: the last artifact written in its worktree was at 04:20:29, 17 s after the
worktree was born (04:20:12), and nothing was written for the remaining ~10 min. Re-running each
stage by hand in that same worktree afterwards passed - `swift test` 1.15 s, tracer 16.5 s, full
pytest 67.4 s - and a fresh full run of the script then finished in ~95 s. So the stall is
**unexplained**; the honest suspicion is a first-run macOS security assessment of freshly built
binaries at a new path, and it was not proven because the stuck process was killed by the cap before
anyone sampled it.
The concrete damage was recoverable but not obvious: SIGTERM skips the script's EXIT trap, so the
kill left a linked worktree with `main` checked out and the merge staged. A branch can be checked
out in only one worktree, so the retry would have failed at `git worktree add … main`. Recovery:
`git -C <wt> merge --abort && git worktree remove --force <wt>` - both run, both confirmed, `main`
still at `f9285d6` at that point (the killed run never committed).

### Learned
1. **Run the irreversible step where a timeout cannot kill it.** The merge is not slow, but it is
   the one command in this loop whose interruption leaves shared state (a worktree owning `main`)
   behind. Background it and poll; the cap is an orchestration artifact, not a property of the work.
2. **A fence you advance is stronger than a fence you override.** Moving `expected_main` inside the
   script cost one line and bought a rehearsable refusal of a third merge; `RALPH_MERGE_MAIN_BEFORE`
   on the command line would have left nothing behind to test.
3. **Prove the no-op before performing it.** `git merge-tree --write-tree` answered "this join
   produces exactly your current tree" before any history was written, which turned the history join
   from an argument into a measurement.
4. **A killed run is still evidence.** The stranded worktree carried the staged merge, so the merged
   tree could be measured stage by stage - which is how `swift test` and the tracer were cleared as
   the stall's cause before spending another full run.
- Blocker: unchanged and still the only one - E3's two GUI clicks on m4mbp (Microphone and System
  Audio Recording for `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app`). G4 did not need them.
- Next: **G5** - republish so the PRD's "one exact SHA everywhere" clause is green again at
  `317df4d`: `git push origin main` (fast-forward `f9285d6..317df4d`, never force, never
  `upstream`), then the server checkout, then the m4mbp checkout, then the four-way check in
  Validation. No service restart: the server tree is byte-identical across the two SHAs. Then
  **G6** - re-run E2b on m4mbp so the installed app carries G1+G2+G3, then the corrected E3 order
  (pair, start, clicks), reading `log show --predicate 'subsystem == "com.alphasight.moss.capture"'`
  if it still fails.

### Off-target discovery: two context.md facts were false, and they change the E3/G6 premise
Probing the installed app also listed `~/Library/Application Support/MOSSCapture` - which context
recorded as **absent** ("nothing has paired yet"). It exists, 0700 dir / 0600
`secrets.json`, mtime 2026-07-28 03:10:14. The server's `live/live-auth.json` - recorded as
**absent**, "D4 is what creates it" - also exists, 0600, `{schema_version: 1, devices: 1}`, mtime
03:10:15. One second apart. The supervisor's attended-diagnosis entry did record the cause in
passing ("one device pairing persisted to the Mac secret store by the unsigned build", "two pairing
sessions were minted"), but neither state block was corrected, so the loop has been carrying two
false facts since. Both are fixed in context.md this iteration.
Independent witness, the live journal since 02:00: 5 `POST /api/live/pairing-codes`, 2 `POST
/api/live/pairings`, 2 `POST /api/live/sessions`, 13 `GET /api/live/descriptor`, and **zero** frame
posts - so a device really is paired, two sessions really exist, and no session has ever received
audio. (It is a uvicorn access log: paths only, no bodies, no token - a small piece of the
secret-hygiene clause, gathered for free.)
Store contents read keys-and-lengths only, never values: `capture-bearer` 43,
`capture-certificate-pin` 64, `capture-device-id` 36, `capture-server-url` 23
(= `https://100.64.0.8:7861`), `capture-session-id` 32, `capture-view-token` 43,
`local-control-secret` 44.
**Consequence for G6, and it is not cosmetic.** The supervisor's finding that
`captureConfiguration(from:)` throws `missingCaptureConfiguration` before `CaptureController.start`
was measured against an *empty* store. m4mbp's store is now complete, so a bare `start` there would
**resolve**, raise both TCC prompts, and publish into the stale 03:10 session instead of failing
closed. Read out of the source to check it could: `LiveServiceRuntime.create`
(`live_service_runtime.py:430`) just inserts into `self._sessions` with no single-session refusal,
and `moss-live-web` has not restarted since 00:50:29, so those in-memory sessions are still there
(until the 12 h view cap ~15:10). Nothing is blocked - but G6 must pair first so the fresh
`pairings` -> `sessions` exchange overwrites the stored session id and bearer.

### Known benign divergence G5 leaves behind
`/api/live/descriptor` still reports `source_revision f9285d69…` - it is baked into the manifest D2
generated and `provider_manifest_hash 61d97ffe…` covers the file. It names an ancestor of the
deployed SHA whose server tree is byte-identical, so it still honestly describes the running code.
Restamping it would rotate a file every paired client hashes for no behavioral gain; recorded rather
than "tidied".

### Validation
- Four-way SHA check (the PRD clause) - four commands, one value: `317df4d728b6765dbe365a3166158ba581299557`.
- `git push origin main` rc=0, `f9285d6..317df4d`; `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` re-read
  after the push confirms it.
- Both host fences printed `fences: ok`; both `git status --porcelain` empty afterwards.
- Services: same MainPIDs / `NRestarts=0` / same `ActiveEnterTimestamp`s before and after.
- Reachability from MacStudio and from m4mbp: 200 / 200 / 200.
- Six-file cross-host `shasum -a 256` parity.
- No tracked product source changed this iteration; the only files this loop wrote are
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.

### Learned
1. **A fact recorded as "absent" decays silently.** "`live-auth.json` is absent, so its absence is
   the marker that no device is paired" was true when written and became false during an
   operator-attended session that was *itself* recorded in progress.txt - just not back-propagated
   into the state blocks. Absence-based markers need re-measuring, not remembering; a positive
   marker (mtime, device count) would not have rotted the same way.
2. **Publishing is a good time to re-measure the hosts.** The G5 mutation needed a pre-state read
   anyway, and that read is what surfaced the stale pairing. Cheap probes on the way to a small
   change beat a dedicated audit iteration.
3. **"Fails closed" is a property of state, not only of code.** The unpaired-`start` guard is real
   and unchanged, but m4mbp is no longer unpaired, so the safety it provided during the diagnosis is
   gone on that host. Re-check the precondition, not the guard.
- Blocker: unchanged and still the only one - E3's two GUI clicks on m4mbp (Microphone and System
  Audio Recording for `/Applications/MOSSCapture.app`). G5 did not need them.
- Next: **G6** - `build-app.sh --configuration release` then `install-app.sh` on m4mbp so the
  installed bundle carries G1+G2+G3. Expect the replacement path (SwiftPM is not byte-reproducible
  there), a `<...>.backup-<utc>` for both bundle and CLI, and **no** DR-change warning; stop if the
  DR moves off `H"e118d874…"`. Prove the fix shipped with the `plutil -extract` before/after and a
  new `CDHash` against `0268367…`. Then the operator's step in the order the diagnosis established:
  D4 mint -> `pair` (Ctrl-D; a stray Return is harmless since G2) -> `start` -> the two clicks ->
  the read-only `sqlite3`/`csreq` verification. Pair first regardless: the stale 03:10 configuration
  means `start` alone would no longer fail closed.

### Rollbacks, recorded before the mutations they cover (prd.md "Reversibility")
1. **Independent pre-copy of the installed artifacts**, taken first so a restore never depends on the
   tool's own backup step succeeding. SwiftPM is not byte-reproducible on this host, so the *only*
   way back to these exact bytes is a copy that already exists:
   `mkdir -p /tmp/moss-g6-prebackup && cp -R /Applications/MOSSCapture.app /tmp/moss-g6-prebackup/ && cp -p /Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture /tmp/moss-g6-prebackup/`
   Restore from it:
   `rm -rf /Applications/MOSSCapture.app && cp -R /tmp/moss-g6-prebackup/MOSSCapture.app /Applications/ && cp -p /tmp/moss-g6-prebackup/mtd-capture /Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture`
   Removing the pre-copy afterwards is `rm -rf /tmp/moss-g6-prebackup`.
2. **`build-app.sh --configuration release`** overwrites `macos/MOSSCapture/.build/product`, which is
   gitignored derived output. Rollback is rollback 1 (the bytes it replaces are the installed ones)
   plus, if the tree ever needs to be clean of build output, `rm -rf macos/MOSSCapture/.build/product`.
   It touches no keychain state: it unlocks the signing keychain from the password file and signs;
   it refuses an install location by design.
3. **`install-app.sh`** replaces the bundle and the CLI. Because the rebuild changes the bytes, this
   takes the **replacement** path, which prints its own rollback line *before* the first mutation and
   moves the previous artifacts aside rather than deleting them:
   `rm -rf '/Applications/MOSSCapture.app' && mv '/Applications/MOSSCapture.app.backup-<utc>' '/Applications/MOSSCapture.app'`
   `rm -f  '/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture' && mv '/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture.backup-<utc>' '/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture'`
   The `<utc>` stamps are recorded in the result section below once the tool prints them.
4. **Stop condition, not a rollback.** If the new bundle's designated requirement is anything other
   than `identifier "com.alphasight.moss.capture" and certificate leaf = H"e118d874…"`, apply
   rollback 3 (or 1) immediately and record a blocker: a changed DR is exactly what would cost the
   operator the TCC grants E3 is about to create.
5. **Nothing else is at risk.** No TCC row exists for this client yet (iteration 23 baseline: zero
   `%moss%` rows in either service), so no grant can be lost by an inode change this time; the
   E1 identity is untouched, so the DR is stable; the server and both services are not involved.

### Result - G6's automatable half is DONE: the installed product now carries G1+G2+G3
The whole point of this iteration in one before/after, measured on the real installed bundle:

| | before | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `plutil -extract NSAppTransportSecurity … /Applications/MOSSCapture.app/Contents/Info.plist` | `No value at that key path` | exactly `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` / `true` |

| bundle inode | 211502957 | 211648186 |

- `build-app.sh --dry-run` printed its seven `plan:` lines and the `rollback:` line and mutated

  `evidence: backup_bundle=/Applications/MOSSCapture.app.backup-20260728T085551Z`,

  `unchanged: /Applications/MOSSCapture.app already holds this exact bundle`,
  `unchanged: … mtd-capture …`, `backup_bundle=none`, `backup_cli=none`, and the inode stayed
  **211648186** - so the shortcut that preserves TCC grants works outside the test fixture too.

### Reachability re-asserted from m4mbp after the reinstall (read-only)
Served leaf hashes to `a35ca9fc4a0f5b32bf7da6dc2e03c1fa5b4ac60992f0ee49b6d5677d22b680ff` = the D2
pin; `/live` 200 and `/api/live/descriptor` 200 over pinned TLS (pin verified *before* trusting the

### Validation
- `build-app.sh --configuration release` rc=0, 7 s, zero warnings; `install-app.sh` rc=0; re-run
  rc=0 with two `unchanged:` lines and an unmoved inode; both dry runs rc=0 with no mutation.
- `codesign --verify --strict` ok on the installed bundle, the installed CLI and the backup bundle.
- `codesign --verify -R=<E1 DR>` passes; wrong-leaf variant refused.
- `plutil -extract NSAppTransportSecurity` on the installed `Info.plist`: errored before, prints the
  G1 shape after.
- Four reachability probes from m4mbp: pin match, 200, 200, 200, 000.
- No tracked file changed this iteration; the loop wrote only
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.

### Learned
1. **`codesign --verify -R=` will not accept what `codesign -d -r-` prints.** The `-d -r-` output
   begins `designated => `; feeding that back verbatim fails `unexpected token: designated` and reads
   exactly like a corrupted signature. Strip the prefix. This iteration's first DR check "failed"
   for that reason alone - a probe bug that, uncaught, would have looked like the stop condition that
   aborts G6 and rolls back a perfectly good install.
2. **Verify the built artifact before installing it, not after.** The ATS key, the DR and the
   entitlement drop were all asserted on `.build/product` while `/Applications` still held the old
   bundle, so a bad build would have been caught with the previous product still in place and no
   backup/restore cycle needed. The install then had nothing left to discover.
3. **The idempotent path is worth exercising deliberately.** `install-app.sh`'s `unchanged:`
   shortcut is what preserves the bundle's inode and therefore the operator's future TCC grants;
   it had only ever been proven against scratch paths in the test suite. Re-running the installer
   once, immediately, proves it on the artifact that will actually hold the grants - for the cost of
   one command.
4. **A rebuild is where a DR would silently drift**, and the loop had a stop condition ready for it.
   It did not drift - E1's identity is stable and the grants will key on it - but the check cost
   nothing and the alternative (discovering it after the human clicks) costs the human's clicks.
- Blocker: **unchanged, and now the only thing between here and Phase F** - E3's human step on
  m4mbp. Everything mechanical is done: the product carries the ATS fix, the pairing-payload trim and
  the control-channel classifier; the live service answers over the pinned tailnet address; the
  checkout, the identity and the installed bundle all verify. What remains cannot be scripted:
  1. On the server, mint one payload (loop-runnable, but only in the same 5-minute window as step 3):
     `ops/live-pair.sh --url https://127.0.0.1:7861 --cert "$HOME/.local/share/moss-transcribe-diarize/live/live.crt"`
     Never redirect the `payload:` line to a file.
  2. In the **GUI session** on m4mbp (not over SSH): `open -a /Applications/MOSSCapture.app`.
     `LSUIElement=true`, so nothing appears on screen - confirm with `pgrep -x MOSSCaptureApp` and
     `ls -l /tmp/moss-capture-501/control.sock`.
  3. `/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture pair --server https://100.64.0.8:7861`, paste the payload,
     **Ctrl-D**. A stray Return before Ctrl-D is harmless since G2. Pair even though a stale 03:10
     configuration exists - that is what overwrites it.
  4. `/Users/ga0/.local/bin/mtd-capture start`. **System Audio Recording prompts first, Microphone
     second** (`NativeDualCaptureSource.swift:191-192`). A hung `start` while a prompt is on screen
     is expected, not a defect - never kill the app.
  5. Verify read-only from anywhere with the two `sqlite3`/`csreq` queries in Validation: two rows
     with `auth_value 2`, and a csreq decoding to the E1 designated requirement.
- Next: the loop cannot advance the product further without step 2-4 above. If the operator is
  unavailable, the highest-value remaining loop-runnable work is F-phase *preparation* that needs no
  TCC: nothing in F1/F2/F3 qualifies (all three need a running capture), so the honest next action is
  to hold at this blocker rather than manufacture an iteration.

## run 20260728-072601 / iteration 8 - F4a rollback rehearsal, rollbacks recorded FIRST

- Target: **F4a** - the PRD acceptance clause "Rollback rehearsed and recorded: live service

- **Why this candidate, off the strict phase order.** Iteration 7 closed G6's automatable half and

  a running capture - but it is **not** true of the rollback clause: it touches only the server, and

  rehearsal (runnable now, closes a clause on its own) and **F4b** is the final close (still gated on
  every other item having evidence). Rehearsing earlier is also strictly better than rehearsing
  later: the value of a rollback is knowing it works *before* you are committed, and after
  certification the certified state is the thing you least want to disturb.
- Authorized action: candidate 32 (F4) explicitly names "rehearse/record rollback, restore service".
  Its "only when every PRD acceptance item has evidence" condition governs *closing the loop*, not
  the rehearsal. Nothing else is touched: no Mac, no TCC, no push, no merge, no tracked source.

### Pre-state, measured read-only this iteration (server, 2026-07-28)
