  `ops/start-web.sh` does.

### The ordering hazard this rehearsal must respect, and why `disable` is the right verb
`ExecStart` points at `ops/start-web.sh` **inside the reverted checkout**. At `163e969` that script
predates `MOSS_WEB_PORT`/`MOSS_RUNS_DIR`, so a live unit started while the checkout is back at
`163e969` would ignore the live profile and try to bring up a **batch** server. Therefore: disable
the live unit **before** the checkout moves back, and restore the checkout **before** the unit is
re-enabled. `disable --now` (not `stop`) is what the recorded D3 rollback says and is also the
safer verb - a disabled unit cannot be pulled in by `default.target` if anything re-runs it.

### Rollbacks, recorded before the mutations they cover (prd.md "Reversibility")
1. **`systemctl --user disable --now moss-live-web.service`** stops the live service and removes the
   `default.target.wants/` symlink. The unit file itself is untouched.
   Rollback: `systemctl --user enable --now moss-live-web.service`.
   Independent of the tool: the unit file is byte-identical to the tracked
   `ops/systemd/moss-live-web.service`, so even a lost unit file is restorable with
   `ops/install-services.sh --with-live` from the restored checkout.
2. **`git -C /mnt/d/Coding/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize checkout 163e969`** moves `HEAD` only - no ref is
   written, no branch moves, `main` stays at `163e969` and the tree is clean going in.
   Rollback: `git -C /mnt/d/Coding/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize checkout 317df4d728b6765dbe365a3166158ba581299557`.
   Both SHAs are already in the object store (`163e969` is an ancestor of `317df4d`), so neither
   direction needs the network.
3. **Nothing else is mutated.** No file outside the checkout is written; the live TLS pair, the
   manifest, `live-auth.json`, `ops/moss-live.env`, `runs/` and `live-runs/` are all outside the
   checkout or untracked, and their inodes+hashes above are the check that this held.
4. **Accepted, deliberate loss:** the two stale 2026-07-28T03:10 in-memory live sessions die with
   the process. That is not a regression - E3's runbook already says `pair` first precisely so the
   canary uses a session the signed app created. Device pairings live in `live-auth.json` on disk;
   proving they survive the cycle is part of this iteration's evidence, not a risk it takes.
5. **Stop condition.** If after the restore the served leaf does not hash to
   `a35ca9fc4a0f5b32bf7da6dc2e03c1fa5b4ac60992f0ee49b6d5677d22b680ff`, stop and record a blocker:
   that pin is what every paired Mac stores, and a rotated pin would break E3 before it starts.
6. **Untouched by design:** the Windows portproxy/firewall/scheduled-task half of the D3 rollback is
   *recorded* (context.md Validation) but deliberately **not** rehearsed - the PRD's rollback clause
   names three things and none of them is the Windows forward, and removing it would risk the
   sign-in task for no acceptance credit. The client-side probe while the service is down covers the
   part that matters behaviorally: a portproxy row with nothing behind it serves nothing.

### Result - F4a DONE: the PRD's "Rollback rehearsed and recorded" clause is GREEN
The clause has three assertions. All three were made for real on the server, in the order the
hazard above requires, and then undone.

**1. Live service disabled.** `systemctl --user disable --now moss-live-web.service` ->
`Removed "…/default.target.wants/moss-live-web.service"`; afterwards `is-enabled`=**disabled**
(rc=1), `is-active`=**inactive** (rc=3), `MainPID=0`, `SubState=dead`, **`Result=success`** (a clean
shutdown, not a kill - the journal shows `Shutting down` / `Waiting for application shutdown` /
`Application shutdown complete` / `Finished server process [334346]`). The 7861 listener vanished;
only `0.0.0.0:7860` pid 301112 remained. The unit **file** was left in place (sha256 `090b7d98…`).
From MacStudio, with the portproxy row still in place: `https://100.64.0.8:7861/live` **000**,
`http://100.64.0.8:7861/live` **000**, `https://192.168.68.38:7861/live` **000**, and
`openssl s_client` to 100.64.0.8:7861 fails `write:errno=54`. A forward with nothing behind it
serves nothing - which is why the Windows half did not need to be torn down to prove the rollback.

**2. Server checkout reverted to `163e969`.** `git -C /mnt/d/Coding/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize checkout
163e969` -> `HEAD` `163e969a054dd7f894e550ff611e9c5a7620ae4d`, tree `4b4de55db114094e413205b8bc0af6a60e33975d`,
`main` still `163e969` (no ref written). Verified the live surface really left the disk:
`ops/systemd/moss-live-web.service`, `ops/live-pair.sh`, `ops/generate-live-tls.sh`,
`moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_auth.py`, `macos/scripts/build-app.sh` and
`ops/moss-live.env.example` all **absent**. Untracked host state survived untouched: `runs/` still
143 dirs, `live-runs/` and `ops/moss-live.env` still present, and the four files under
`~/.local/share/moss-transcribe-diarize/live/` kept **both** inode and sha256
(`live-auth.json` 11374/`9d306766…`, `live.crt` 12436/`1839a186…`, `live.key` 12123/`7bb3173f…`,
`live-provider-manifest.json` 10565/`8bef5c36…`).

**3. `http://192.168.68.38:7860/` still serving**, measured from MacStudio while rolled back:
root **200**, `/api/jobs` **200** returning **141** real jobs, `/api/runtime` **200** with the vLLM
backend still answering (`backend: vllm`, `base_url http://127.0.0.1:8000/v1`). `moss-vllm` MainPID
**322117** and `moss-web` MainPID **301112**, both `active`, `NRestarts=0`, ActiveEnterTimestamps
unchanged at 2026-07-26 22:05:29 / 2026-07-24 21:37:11 - **no batch service was restarted at any
point in this rehearsal.**
*And the next restart would be healthy too, measured at the rolled-back SHA:* the read-only argv
probe (last `exec` line asserted first, then rewritten in a `/tmp` copy, so it can never launch a
server) run against `163e969`'s own `ops/start-web.sh` with the host's real `ops/moss.env` derives
exactly `tests/test_live_service_deployment.py:51-62`'s `BATCH_ARGV` - port 7860, runs dir
`<checkout>/runs`, **no `--live` flag**.

**Then restored, in the reverse order.** `git checkout 317df4d…` -> HEAD `317df4d7…`, tree
`3b37815fd1ba68d83e8f2441f8d4d9a2778446a3`, `git status --porcelain` **empty**, all six probed files
back, and the installed unit still byte-identical to the restored tracked
`ops/systemd/moss-live-web.service`. Then `systemctl --user enable --now moss-live-web.service` ->

ActiveEnterTimestamp 2026-07-28 05:06:05, listening TLS on `0.0.0.0:7861`.
Post-restore from MacStudio: the served leaf is **byte-identical** to the one captured before the
rehearsal and still hashes to `a35ca9fc4a0f5b32bf7da6dc2e03c1fa5b4ac60992f0ee49b6d5677d22b680ff`

`provider_manifest_hash 61d97ffe…`, `source_revision f9285d69…` (the known benign divergence,

**200/200/200** with the same 141 jobs.

### What the rehearsal cost, and what it deliberately did not touch
- **Cost:** the two stale 2026-07-28T03:10 in-memory live sessions, which died with PID 334346.
  Accepted in the pre-record above; E3 pairs first anyway. The on-disk device state is provably
  intact (`live-auth.json` same inode, same sha256), so nothing needs re-pairing that did not
  already need it. This is the persistence split the view-authority contract asserts - devices
  durable, sessions memory-only - observed on the real host for the first time, though only the
  durable half is directly measured; the session loss is inferred from the new MainPID plus the
  memory-only contract.
- **Not touched:** the Windows portproxy/firewall/scheduled task (out of the clause's scope, and the
  000s above show the forward is inert without a listener), the batch units, the TLS pair, the
  manifest, `runs/`, `live-runs/`, `ops/moss-live.env`, and every Mac.

### Validation
- `systemctl --user disable --now` rc=0; state went enabled/active -> disabled/inactive with
  `Result=success`; `enable --now` rc=0; state back to enabled/active with a new MainPID.
- `git checkout 163e969` rc=0 and `git checkout 317df4d…` rc=0; tree hash back to `3b37815f…`,
  `git status --porcelain` empty at the end.
- 12 client-side probes across the three phases (before / rolled back / restored): pinned `/live`
  200 -> 000 -> 200, `/api/live/descriptor` 200 -> 000 -> 200, plaintext 7861 000 -> 000 -> 000,
  batch root 200 -> 200 -> 200, `/api/jobs` 200/141 jobs throughout, `/api/runtime` 200 throughout.
- Leaf `cmp` before vs after: identical bytes; DER sha256 equals the D2 pin both times.
- No tracked file changed this iteration; the loop wrote only
  `scripts/ralph-afk/{context.md,progress.txt}`.

### Learned
1. **The live service needs ~10 s from `enable --now` to a bound listener** (05:06:05 started ->
   05:06:15 `Uvicorn running on https://0.0.0.0:7861`), because the app imports the ONNX/decoder
   stack on startup. The first post-restore probe was taken at 6 s and returned **000** - i.e. a
   correct restore that reads exactly like a failed one. Poll for 200 with a timeout; never sample
   once and conclude. This is the single most likely way a real rollback-and-restore would be
   misread as broken.
2. **`163e969` is "before the MVP", not "before anything named live".**
   `moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_transport.py` **exists** at that SHA while `live_auth.py` does
   not. Anyone verifying the rollback by looking for `live*` files will get a confusing answer;
   check for the files the MVP actually added.
3. **A rolled-back checkout is not a clean checkout, and that is expected.** `163e969`'s
   `.gitignore` predates the `ops/moss-live.env` entry, so while rolled back
   `git status --porcelain` reports `?? ops/moss-live.env`. The checkout does not delete the
   profile - only a `git clean` would, and that is forbidden - but an operator who reflexively
   tidies an "unclean" tree there would destroy the live profile and with it the ability to restore
   in one command.
4. **The "batch unharmed" contract was verified against its own origin for the first time.**
   `BATCH_ARGV` is documented as "recorded from `ops/start-web.sh` before the live service existed";
   until now every check ran it against the post-merge adapter. Running the probe at `163e969` on
   the real host reproduces it byte for byte, so the two-profile claim is now anchored at both ends.
5. **Rehearsing the rollback before certification was the cheap moment.** Nothing was in flight, no
   capture session existed, and the only casualty was two stale sessions the runbook already
   planned to replace. After F1-F3 the same rehearsal would have to disturb a certified state.
- Blocker: **unchanged** - E3's human TCC step on m4mbp (pair -> start -> two GUI clicks) is still
  the only thing between here and Phase F. The exact runbook is in iteration 7's entry and in
  context.md's E3 blocks. This iteration did not touch it and could not.
- Next: with F4a closed, **every remaining PRD acceptance item depends on the operator**: permissions
  granted, the 60 s canary, the 300 s certification, the 16-minute soak, the run-time half of secret
  hygiene, and F4b's final close. There is no further loop-runnable product or evidence work; the
  honest next action is to hold at the E3 blocker.

## run 20260728-072601 / iteration 9 - F0 server-side live pipeline probe (no Mac, no TCC)
================================================================================

- Target: **F0** (new candidate, off-list, justified below) - drive the deployed live service
  through the *entire* pipeline E3's canary depends on, from a real remote pinned-TLS peer, with
  real two-speaker speech, and without a Mac or a TCC grant. Prove or disprove that the server can
  turn accepted v2 frames into a transcript with speaker labels.
- **Why this candidate, off the strict phase order.** Iteration 8 concluded there is no
  loop-runnable acceptance work left. That conclusion is right about the *acceptance clauses* and
  wrong about the *risk*. The host journal shows **zero frame posts ever**: ingress, lane contract,
  mixer, endpointer, decoder, diarizer, publish and snapshot have never run on the real host with
  the real GPU and the deployed manifest. Every one of them sits **downstream** of the operator's
  irreducible TCC clicks, so if any is broken the loop discovers it only by spending the one thing
  it cannot obtain. The same argument iteration 8 used to pull F4a forward applies here and more
  strongly: this is the cheap moment, and unlike F1-F3 it needs no capture device, because the Mac
  client's whole contribution to that path is HTTP requests this loop can make itself.
- Not an acceptance claim. F1/F2/F3 remain the PRD's certification and still require the app: the
  app owns the single-clock latency measurement, the permission transitions and the pasteboard
  handoff. This probe retires *server* risk only, and its latency figure is a plausibility
  preview, not the PRD's Phase F number.
- Authorized action: no tracked product source, no server file edit, no service restart, no merge,
  no push, no Mac. One new evidence tool under `scripts/ralph-afk/` (the only path the post-merge
  freeze still permits) plus live-service API calls.

### Pre-state, measured read-only before any mutation (2026-07-28)
- m4mbp TCC: `select count(*) from access` = **320** (reader sane), rows matching `%moss%` = **0**
  in both services, `pgrep -x MOSSCaptureApp` = 0, no `/tmp/moss-capture-501/control.sock`.
  **E3 is still unstarted; the operator has not acted.**
- Server: `moss-live-web` MainPID **336320** active NRestarts=0 (ActiveEnterTimestamp 2026-07-28
  05:06:05, i.e. still the F4a restore), `moss-web` **301112**, `moss-vllm` **322117**, both
  NRestarts=0.
- `live-auth.json`: inode **11374**, 275 bytes, mode 600, mtime 2026-07-28 03:10:15,
  sha256 `9d306766a001df1ef60155bb70f1ec41348c6eac1b56115704901c7f6e9aa47c`,
  `{schema_version, devices}` with **1** device.
- `/mnt/d/Coding/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize/live-runs`: **empty**, `du -sh` = 0. That is the baseline
  for the PRD's "no raw audio is persisted" clause.
- From MacStudio: batch `http://192.168.68.38:7860/` **200**; pinned `https://100.64.0.8:7861/live`
  **200**; descriptor **200** (`source_revision f9285d69…`, the known benign divergence).

### Rollbacks, recorded before the mutations they cover (prd.md "Reversibility")
1. **Pairing a probe device** (`POST /api/live/pairings` from MacStudio) writes a second device into
   `live-auth.json`.
   Rollback: `curl -sk -X DELETE https://127.0.0.1:7861/api/live/devices/<device_id>` **run on the
   host** (the route is loopback-only, `live_auth.py:305`). It aborts and releases any session the
   device owns, so it is also the rollback for mutation 2.
   *Known, accepted residue:* revocation marks the device `revoked` rather than deleting it, so
   `live-auth.json` ends larger than baseline and that device id can never be re-paired. The entry
   is inert - no token, no authority - and nothing a client hashes lives in that file (clients pin
   the certificate, not the auth state). The deeper rollback that restores the exact baseline bytes
   is `cp` of the pre-copy taken below, and it costs a service restart, so it is deliberately not
   the default.
   Pre-copy taken first, on the host, outside the repo:
   `cp -p live-auth.json live-auth.json.ralph-f0-backup-<utc>` under
   `~/.local/share/moss-transcribe-diarize/live/`.
   Deeper rollback if ever needed: `systemctl --user stop moss-live-web.service` ->
   `mv <backup> live-auth.json` -> `systemctl --user start moss-live-web.service`, then poll ~10 s
   for 200 (F4a learning 1).
2. **Creating a live session** (`POST /api/live/sessions`) is memory-only (`live_service_runtime.py`
   holds `self._sessions`; the persisted state file carries devices only).
   Rollback: `POST /api/live/sessions/<id>/stop`, which the probe does itself; failing that,
   `POST /api/live/sessions/<id>/abort`; failing that, mutation 1's device revoke aborts it; and in
   the limit the session dies with the process, as F4a observed for the two 03:10 sessions.
3. **Posting frames** mutates only that session's in-memory state and whatever the decode path
   writes. `MOSS_RUNS_DIR=<checkout>/live-runs` is the only writable run root the live unit has and
   it is empty at baseline, so the rollback is `rm -rf` of anything that appears **under
   `live-runs/` only** - and whether anything appears there at all is itself evidence for the PRD's
   "no raw audio is persisted" clause.
4. **Nothing else is mutated.** No unit is restarted or reloaded, no checkout moves, no file inside
   the repo on any host is written, the TLS pair and the manifest are not touched, and no Mac is
   contacted for anything but the read-only TCC probe above.
5. **Stop condition.** If the served leaf stops hashing to
   `a35ca9fc4a0f5b32bf7da6dc2e03c1fa5b4ac60992f0ee49b6d5677d22b680ff`, abort and record a blocker:
   that pin is what m4mbp has stored. The probe enforces this itself - it is a full-certificate pin
   client and refuses the connection on mismatch, exactly like the Mac.
6. **Secret hygiene is part of the design, not a post-hoc check.** The pairing payload travels
   host -> MacStudio inside an SSH pipe into a shell variable and then into the probe's **stdin**;
   it is never an argument, never echoed, never redirected to a file. The probe holds the payload,
   device token and view token in locals and reports only their lengths.

### Result - F0 FOUND A CERTIFICATION BLOCKER. The live pipeline dies in ~3 seconds.
Three runs against the deployed service from MacStudio over the real pinned tailnet TLS hop, each
from a freshly minted single-use payload and a fresh device. **Every one of them killed or was
rejected by the session inside the first six 0.5 s ticks.** Nothing about the failures is
client-shaped: every frame the server rejected was preceded by frames it accepted with 200 from the
same code path, and the two failures are raised by server code, not by transport.

**What works, and is now proven on the real host for the first time:**
- Full-certificate pinning end to end. The probe is a leaf-pin client with `CERT_NONE` and no
  hostname check, exactly like `PinnedCertificateURLSessionDelegate`; the served leaf hashed to
  `a35ca9fc…` on every connection. **1 TLS handshake for a whole run** - connection reuse holds.
- `POST /api/live/pairings` from a **remote** non-loopback TLS peer -> 200, `scope=capture`,
  43-char device token. `POST /api/live/sessions` -> 200 with a 43-char view token and a descriptor
  reporting `sample_rate 16000`, `frame_samples 8000`,
  `provider_manifest_hash 61d97ffe…` - **the C2 manifest is admitted by the running service**, not
  only by the runtime readers iteration 18 used.
- v2 lane ingress with the canonical wire format: 8000-sample 16 kHz frames on both lanes with real
  nanosecond `capture_timestamp_ns`, per-lane sequences from zero, honest `silent` flags. Accepted
  with 200, `accepted_samples` advancing 8000 per frame per lane, `retained_samples` tracking it.
- The endpointer freezes spans and the coordinator writes them out as WAVs for decode
  (`/tmp/mtd-live-<rand>/span-0000.wav`).
- View authority behaves as C1 says: 200 while the session is viewable, **401 the moment it is
  not**, and 401 after a stop. `revoke_view`'s sibling `DELETE /api/live/devices/{id}` works on the
  real host - it returned the owned `session_ids` and released them.
- **No raw audio is persisted.** After three runs `live-runs/` still has **0 entries** and
  `/tmp/mtd-live-*` is **gone** - the span WAVs are temporary and cleaned up. That is real evidence
  for a PRD clause, taken on the live path rather than argued from source.
- Timings are comfortable: frame publish p95 **209-279 ms**, snapshot p95 **73-105 ms**, events p95
  **22-175 ms** over the tailnet. A render bound of `1000 + 105 + 175 = 1280 ms` leaves ~2.7 s of
  the PRD's 4 s canary budget for committed latency. **Not a Phase F number** - nothing ever
  committed, so committed latency could not be measured at all.

**Blocker 1 - a span with no speech makes the whole session terminal.**
Run B (20 s plan, 1.0 s of leading silence, fail-fast): five frames accepted, then tick 4
microphone -> **409** with

    detail:  vLLM transcription returned zero parsed segments for /tmp/mtd-live-3lmp3f6_/span-0000.wav.
    failure: {kind: integrity, code: RuntimeError, retryable: false}

and the snapshot carrying that same record as **`terminal_failure`** while `session.status` still
read `active` - precisely the divergence the C1 view-authority contract predicted, observed for the
first time on a real failure rather than a test. Every later frame got 409, every snapshot/events
poll got 401, and `POST /stop` answered 409.
*The span had no speech in it, and that is measured rather than inferred:* the snapshot reports
`frozen_until_sample: 14400` (0.9 s) while the run's first utterance starts at sample 16000 (1.0 s).
So span-0000 covered 0 -> 0.9 s of pure silence.
*The code path:* `vllm_runner.py:245` `_validate_transcription_response` raises a **bare
`RuntimeError`** when `parse_transcript(text)` is empty. It escapes before
`live_adapters._validated_segments` (`live_adapters.py:360-364`), which has a *typed*
`LiveProviderError` for the identical condition, can classify it. The live session therefore sees an
unclassified `RuntimeError`, records `kind=integrity, retryable=false`, and goes terminal.
*Why this is a certification blocker and not a probe artifact:* every real meeting begins with the
operator pressing start and then a second or two of nobody speaking, and contains silence between
turns. On this build the first such span ends the meeting. F1 would have failed within ~3 seconds of
the operator's TCC clicks, with the browser's `/live` view going 401 at the same moment - which is
the *least* diagnosable way for it to fail.

**Blocker 2 - the endpointer can ask to freeze a span that does not advance.**
Run C, identical except `--lead-seconds 0` so the first span contains speech: the session survived
one tick longer (eleven frames accepted, `accepted_samples` 32000, snapshot version 4), then tick 5
microphone -> **400 `frozen span end must advance.`**
That string is `live_session.py:237`, `freeze_until` refusing `end_sample <= self._frozen_until_sample`.
Both call sites are the coordinator (`live_coordinator.py:128` on the accept path and `:231` in
`_freeze_and_queue`, which `flush_endpoint` uses), so the endpoint policy handed the session a span
whose end had already been frozen.
*Honest caveat for whoever fixes this:* the probe's two lanes carry **identical**
`capture_timestamp_ns` and start at the same instant, which the Mac's two lanes will not. That is a
legal input - the contract has no distinctness requirement, and the server 200'd eleven such frames -
but a fix iteration must rule it in or out before concluding, because it is the one input property
that is more degenerate than a real capture. Blocker 1 has no such caveat.

**Why the suite is green while both of these are live.** No test in the repo puts the *real* VAD
endpointer and a *real* decoder in the same process: `tests/test_live_vad.py` drives the endpointer
with a stub provider (it even asserts the typed `LiveProviderError` for "zero parsed segments" that
the real path never reaches), and every decoder node is stubbed because MacStudio has no GPU. The
seam where the two meet has therefore never been exercised anywhere but the deployed host, and that
seam is where both blockers live. That is the durable lesson, not the individual bugs.

### The probe's own defect, found and fixed inside this iteration
Run A published for **seven minutes** after the session was already terminal, because the first
version had no fail-fast: it recorded each non-200 and kept going. A terminal failure is sticky, so
this buried the one response that explained it and cost a foreground timeout. `--fail-fast` is now
the default (`--no-fail-fast` is the deliberate opt-out) and every request prints
`tick/lane/status/ms` to stderr, so the *next* hang is diagnosable while it is happening. Related
measurement: healthy requests are 4-280 ms, but the post-terminal 409s took **6-8 s each** in run A.
That is unexplained and bounded (it only happens to a session that is already dead), but it means an
Outbox on the Mac will back up hard behind a dead session.

### Validation
- Probe runs: A (60 s plan, aborted by the harness at 7 min), B (20 s, `--lead-seconds 1.0`,
  rc=3, aborted tick 4), C (20 s, `--lead-seconds 0`, rc=3, aborted tick 5). Reports at
  `/tmp/moss-f0-diag.json` and `/tmp/moss-f0-leadzero.json` (volatile; the durable evidence is here).
- `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` on the probe: ok. `--help` renders.
- Audio generation checked before any network call: 60 s plan -> 120 frames/lane, 50 voiced mic /
  53 voiced system, **2** overlapping voiced frames (clean turns); 48 s -> 14 overlapping, which is
  why 60 s is the default shape.
- **Post-state, all mutations undone or accounted:**
  - All three probe devices **revoked** via the recorded loopback rollback; each revoke returned the
    session id it released. `live-auth.json` device map is now
    `{AB600574-…: revoked=false, ralph-f0-probe-…091938Z: true, …092856Z: true, …093052Z: true}` -
    **the m4mbp device is untouched**, which is the only one that matters.
  - `moss-live-web` MainPID **336320**, NRestarts=0, ActiveEnterTimestamp still 2026-07-28 05:06:05;
    `moss-web` **301112** and `moss-vllm` **322117**, both NRestarts=0. **Three terminal sessions did
    not restart or destabilise anything** - the failure is per-session, which is itself worth knowing.
  - `live-runs/` **0 entries**, `/tmp/mtd-live-*` **0 directories**.
  - Server checkout `317df4d728b6765dbe365a3166158ba581299557`, `git status --porcelain` **empty**.
  - From MacStudio: batch `/` **200**, batch `/api/jobs` **200**, live `/live` **200**, live
    `/api/live/descriptor` **200**, served leaf still `a35ca9fc…`.
  - Repo: only `scripts/ralph-afk/{live-pipeline-probe.py,context.md,progress.txt}` changed. No
    tracked product source, no `ops/`, no server file, no Mac.
- Secret hygiene held by construction: the payload went host -> SSH pipe -> shell variable ->
  probe **stdin**; it was never an argument, never echoed, never written to a file. The reports
  contain `device_token_len: 43` / `view_token_len: 43` and no token. `--dry-run`-style evidence
  lines from `live-pair.sh` were printed (`payload_matches_certificate=true`,
  `expires_in_seconds=300`) with the `payload:` line filtered out on the host side.

### Learned
1. **The one thing nobody had tested was the one thing that was broken.** Every gate this loop ran
   was green, and the live pipeline still cannot survive three seconds of ordinary audio. The gates
   were honest; they just could not reach the decoder seam. When a suite is green and a path has
   never run in production, the greenness is evidence about the suite, not about the path.
2. **Pulling the probe ahead of the human step was worth more than the whole rest of the iteration.**
   Had F1 run first, the operator would have granted TCC, watched the session die in three seconds
   with a 409 and a 401, and the loop would still have had to build this probe to find out why -
   except now with a burned canary and a paired-but-broken app in the way.
3. **A sticky terminal failure needs fail-fast instrumentation, not more retries.** The seven-minute
   run produced strictly less information than the two-second one.
4. **`session.status == "active"` with a non-null `terminal_failure` is not a hypothetical.** C1's
   contract was written against exactly this and is now confirmed against a real runtime failure.
5. **Revoking a device is a complete, no-restart rollback for a pairing** - it returns and releases
   the owned sessions - but it *marks* rather than deletes, so the auth file only grows. Three inert
   revoked entries is the price paid here, recorded rather than hidden.
- **Blocker (new, and it needs the operator's decision, not their hands):** both fixes are in tracked
  **server** source (`vllm_runner.py` / `live_adapters.py` classification, and the endpoint policy),
  which the post-merge freeze forbids on this branch and which the 2026-07-28 amendment - already
  spent on the Mac client - does not cover. This is the same shape as the ATS blocker that produced
  that amendment: a defect no gate could see, found after the merge. **prd.md needs a second
  authorized fix cycle** (or an explicit decision to certify against a rebuilt branch) before F1-F3
  can pass. The loop must not start server product work until that authorization exists.
- **Blocker (unchanged):** E3's physical TCC clicks on m4mbp. Still zero `%moss%` rows in TCC.db,
  app not running. But E3 is now *pointless until the server is fixed* - the canary it exists to
  enable cannot pass on this build. The correct order is now: authorize the server fix -> fix and
  gate it -> deploy -> then spend the operator's TCC clicks.
- Next: hold for the authorization. If it is granted, iteration 1 of that cycle should (a) reproduce
  Blocker 1 deterministically as a test that puts the real `vllm_runner` validation seam under the
  live coordinator with an empty transcript, (b) decide the policy question - a span the decoder
  cannot parse should be *dropped or committed empty*, never terminal - and (c) only then look at
  Blocker 2, first ruling the identical-lane-timestamp caveat in or out by re-running this probe
  with a per-lane timestamp offset.

================================================================================
## Iteration 10 (run 20260728-072601) - rollbacks recorded BEFORE the host probe run
================================================================================
About to run `scripts/ralph-afk/live-pipeline-probe.py` once against the deployed live
service with per-lane `capture_timestamp_ns` offsets, to rule the F0 blocker-2 caveat in or
out on the real host. Mutations it makes and their rollbacks, recorded before applying any of
them (prd.md "Reversibility"):

1. `ops/live-pair.sh` mints one pairing code on the host (loopback-only route).
   Rollback: none needed - `live_auth.PAIRING_TTL_SECONDS = 300` expires it unused, and the
   probe consumes it. Nothing durable is written until a device pairs.
2. The probe pairs one device `ralph-h2-probe-<utc>` and creates one session.
   Rollback (mandatory after the run, on the host, loopback-only, no restart):
     curl -sk -X DELETE https://127.0.0.1:7861/api/live/devices/ralph-h2-probe-<utc>
   It marks revoked rather than deleting, so `live-auth.json` only grows; the m4mbp device
   AB600574-FD93-4321-967E-652AB064A70B must stay `revoked: false`.
3. Deeper rollback, only if `live-auth.json` is damaged (costs a service restart, so it is
   NOT the default): restore the iteration-9 baseline copy
   `live-auth.json.ralph-f0-backup-20260728T091927Z` (sha256 9d306766...).
No checkout, unit, manifest, certificate or Mac state is touched by any of this.

================================================================================
## Iteration 10 (run 20260728-072601) - F0's caveat was real, and it was hiding a
## third blocker that kills the session in ~1.1 s
================================================================================
Target: the one open question iteration 9 recorded - do the probe's identical lane
`capture_timestamp_ns` values explain blocker 2, or not? Answering it was the only remaining
work that needed neither the operator's hands nor an authorization the loop does not have.

Files touched (Ralph evidence only; `git diff --name-only main HEAD -- ':!scripts/ralph-afk'`
is EMPTY, so the post-merge freeze is intact):
  scripts/ralph-afk/live-hardcap-repro.py   (new - offline reproduction)
  scripts/ralph-afk/live-pipeline-probe.py  (+ `--lane-offset-ms LANE=MS`)
  scripts/ralph-afk/context.md, progress.txt

### Answer: the caveat was NOT a false alarm, and it was not what it looked like either
Ruling it out structurally came first, and the repro prints the reason instead of asserting it:
`live_session.AudioFrame` - the frame the mixer hands the coordinator - has fields
`sequence, pcm, sample_count, sample_rate` and **no timestamp field at all**. Lane capture
timestamps stop at the mixer. So they cannot be what makes `freeze_until` refuse a span.
Then the offset run on the real host showed the caveat still mattered, for the opposite reason:
the degenerate input was **masking a worse defect** that fires before blocker 2 is reachable.

### Blocker 2, named and reproduced offline (deterministic, 0.4 s, no server/GPU/network)
`LiveSession.accept_frame` freezes its own hard-cap span (`live_session.py:198` ->
`:353-359`, `while accepted - frozen_until >= hard_cap_samples`). `LiveCoordinator.accept_frame`
calls that first (`live_coordinator.py:122`), *then* runs the endpoint policy, which emits its own
`hard_cap` span at the identical sample; `freeze_until` refuses it because the session already moved
`_frozen_until_sample` there. Two independent freezers, same boundary, session wins, coordinator
dies. Deployed config (both caps 40000), measured this iteration:
  --frames 8                       -> ValueError: frozen span end must advance. @ accepted 40000
  --frames 8 --speech-pattern 0    -> same error, same sample (pure silence: nobody has spoken yet)
  --frames 45 --frame-samples 1000 -> same error, same sample  (NOT a frame-size artifact)
  --frames 24 --speech-pattern 1100-> SURVIVES 192000 samples  (endpoints every <2.5 s keep the
                                      two cursors moving together)
  --frames 8 --session-hard-cap none -> SURVIVES; this is the shape every test uses
So the failure condition is exact: **any stretch of `hard_cap_samples` (40000 = 2.5 s) of accepted
audio containing no endpoint span** - 2.5 s of continuous speech, or 2.5 s of opening silence.
*Second defect in the same output:* the span the session freezes by itself is never queued for
decode. `FrameAck.frozen_span_ids` is dropped by the coordinator and `live_service_runtime.py:477`
reports only `result.frozen_spans`. Even without the collision that audio would never be transcribed.
*Why the suite is green, exactly:* every harness gives the policy a cap and the session none -
`tests/test_live_coordinator.py:101` (`LiveSession(max_retained_samples=8000)`, policy 4000),
`tests/test_live_service_runtime.py:155,165` (endpoint 4000, `bounds.hard_cap_samples=None`),
`tests/test_live_api.py:209` (same shape, bounds default `None`); `tests/test_live_session.py` tests
the session's cap with no coordinator and no policy. The two have never coexisted outside production.
And C2 (iteration 12) *requires* `endpoint_config.hard_cap_samples == bounds_config.hard_cap_samples`
in a deployed manifest - so the work that made a divergence impossible is what guarantees the
collision on the real host. That is worth stating plainly: C2 was right about spans and blind to this.

### Blocker 3 (NEW, and the one that fires first): an unaligned lane makes an illegal VAD frame
One probe run, deployed service, real pinned tailnet TLS, fresh single-use payload, fresh device,
`--lane-offset-ms system=137 --lead-seconds 0`:
  tick 0 system 200 / microphone 200, snapshot 200
  tick 1 system 200
  tick 1 microphone **500 Internal Server Error** (468.8 ms), wall time 1.13 s
  terminal_failure {kind: integrity, code: Error, message: "Error while processing frame",
                    retryable: false}; accepted_samples **5808**; stop -> 409; snapshot -> 401
5808 samples = 363 ms = 500 ms - 137 ms, i.e. exactly the offset eaten out of the first mixed frame.
Host traceback (`journalctl --user -u moss-live-web.service`):
  live_transport.py:221 -> live_mixer.py:112 -> live_service_runtime.py:465 ->
  live_coordinator.py:122 -> live_provider_bundle.py:424 -> :643 ->
  webrtcvad.Error: Error while processing frame
*Mechanism.* `LiveMixer._stage` takes `origin_ns = max(first capture_timestamp_ns over active lanes)`
and non-final `safe_end_ns = min(last sealed interval end)`, then
`sample_count = floor((safe_end - cursor) * 16000 / 1e9)` - an arbitrary integer as soon as the lanes
are not aligned on the frame grid. `WebRtcSpeechProvider.observe` (`live_provider_bundle.py:417-434`)
chops it with `piece_samples = min(frame_samples, end - cursor)`, so the trailing piece is short. The
deployed `speech_provider` is `{kind: webrtc, frame_samples: 160, mode: 1}` (read off the host
manifest) and webrtcvad accepts only 10/20/30 ms = 160/320/480 samples at 16 kHz.
5808 = 36x160 + **48** -> raise. Bare third-party exception, so like blocker 1 it escapes typed
classification, lands as `kind=integrity, retryable=false`, and the session is terminal.
Reproduced offline with the real `WebRtcSpeechProvider` and a stand-in VAD that enforces only
webrtcvad's documented length contract (MacStudio has no native webrtcvad wheel - itself part of why
no test could catch this):
  --speech-provider webrtc --frame-samples 5808 -> refused at frame 0, "got 48"
  --speech-provider webrtc --frame-samples 8000 -> SURVIVES (8000 = 50x160)  [control]
**This is the normal case.** Two real capture devices never start on the same instant, so the Mac
will produce unaligned lanes on essentially every run. F0 could not see it because one shared origin
makes every mixed frame exactly 8000 samples.

### Severity order for whoever is authorized to fix this
  blocker 3  ~1.1 s  any unaligned lanes            (H3 - fix first)
  blocker 1  ~3 s    any span the decoder can't parse (H1)
  blocker 2  2.5 s   any endpoint-free stretch        (H2)
All three are `kind=integrity, retryable=false`; the session cannot recover from any of them, and
each one alone is enough to end a real meeting in the first few seconds.

### Validation
- `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` on both tools: ok. Both `--help` render.
  `--lane-offset-ms bogus=5` is refused with rc=2 **before** any network call.
- Seven offline repro cases run, listed above; rc=3 (reproduced) or rc=0 (survived) each time.
- `git diff --name-only 317df4d HEAD -- moss_transcribe_diarize` is **empty**, so the offline repro
  runs the *deployed* server source, not a drifted local copy.
- One host probe run; report at /tmp/moss-h2-offset.json (volatile - the durable evidence is here).
- **Post-state, all mutations undone or accounted:**
  - Device `ralph-h2-probe-20260728T094218Z` **revoked** via the recorded loopback rollback; the
    call returned the one session id it released. `live-auth.json` now holds 5 devices with exactly
    one unrevoked: `AB600574-FD93-4321-967E-652AB064A70B` (m4mbp) - untouched.
  - `moss-live-web` MainPID **336320** / ActiveEnterTimestamp 2026-07-28 05:06:05, `moss-web`
    **301112** / 2026-07-24 21:37:11, `moss-vllm` **322117** / 2026-07-26 22:05:29, all
    `NRestarts=0`, all `active`. Nothing restarted; the 500 did not destabilise the service.
  - Server checkout `317df4d728b6765dbe365a3166158ba581299557`, `git status --porcelain` empty.
  - `live-runs/` **0 entries**, `/tmp/mtd-live-*` **0 directories** - no raw audio persisted.
  - From MacStudio: batch `http://192.168.68.38:7860/` **200**, live `/live` **200**,
    `/api/live/descriptor` **200**.
  - Repo: only `scripts/ralph-afk/*`. No tracked product source, no `ops/`, no Mac, no Windows.
- Secret hygiene: payload went host -> SSH pipe -> shell variable -> probe **stdin**; never argv,
  never a file, never echoed. The report carries `device_token_len: 43` / `view_token_len: 43`.

### Learned
1. **A recorded caveat is worth spending an iteration on even when you expect it to be nothing.**
   The structural check said lane timestamps cannot reach the coordinator - true, and it would have
   been a perfectly defensible reason to skip the run. The run found a defect that fires four times
   sooner than the two already known.
2. **Degenerate test input hides defects in the code that consumes its shape.** Identical lane
   origins made every mixed frame exactly 8000 samples, which is the one size that happens to be a
   whole number of VAD frames. The probe was measuring the pipeline through a keyhole.
3. **Two mechanisms enforcing one policy is the bug.** Blocker 2 is not a wrong number anywhere;
   both freezers are individually correct and individually tested. The defect is that both exist.
4. **The suite's blind spot has a precise shape and it is now written down:** every live harness
   configures the session's hard cap to `None`. That is not an oversight to shrug at - it is the
   single line that would have turned this into a red test at iteration 12.
5. Offline reproduction beats host reproduction once the mechanism is known: 0.4 s and no pairing,
   versus a minted payload, a device that can only be revoked rather than deleted, and a live
   session. Iteration 9 needed the host to *find* the seam; iteration 10 needed it only once more.
- **Blocker (unchanged, and now three items deep):** H1/H2/H3 all change tracked **server** source,
  which the post-merge freeze forbids on this branch and which the spent 2026-07-28 amendment does

## Iteration 1 (run 20260728-112922) - H3: webrtcvad never gets an illegal frame
================================================================================
Target: candidate **H3**, the first blocker the deployed build hits (~1.1 s, on essentially
every real capture). Chosen because context.md fixes the order H3 -> H1 -> H2 and because
the amendment's own gate - a `live-pipeline-probe.py` run that survives its full plan -
is unreachable while H3 stands.

Scope justification, recorded because prd.md's second amendment names only blockers 1 and 2:
blocker 3 was produced by that amendment's *own* Blocker-2 instruction ("rule the identical
per-lane capture_timestamp_ns in or out by re-running the probe with a per-lane offset");
it is the same classification seam the amendment opens the cycle over (a bare third-party
exception -> kind=integrity, retryable=false); and it fires before blockers 1 and 2 are
approached, so the amendment's gate cannot pass without it. Nothing outside it was touched.

Files touched (2 tracked, both inside `moss_transcribe_diarize/` + `tests/`, which the second
amendment authorizes):
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_provider_bundle.py   (WebRtcSpeechProvider)
  tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py                     (new, 5 nodes)
  scripts/ralph-afk/context.md, progress.txt
`git diff --stat main HEAD` for tracked source is exactly those two files.

### The change, in one sentence
`WebRtcSpeechProvider` tiles the **accepted-sample stream** instead of each accepted range, so
webrtcvad is called only with exactly `frame_samples` of real contiguous audio - never with the
short trailing piece `min(frame_samples, end - cursor)` used to build whenever the mixed frame
was not a multiple of the VAD frame.
- A tail that cannot fill a VAD frame is **carried** into the next `observe`: not zero-padded
  (that fabricates input to a detector) and not dropped (that leaves audio unexamined).
- The coordinator still requires gap-free coverage *now* (`_observe_endpoint` refuses anything
  else), so the carried tail is reported with the last decided frame's answer,
  `confidence=None` and `provider_reason=webrtc_observation_carried` - it says it is not a
  decision rather than pretending to be one. Silence is the answer before anything is decided.
- Carry state (PCM + one bit) is per provider instance, and `speech_provider_factory()` is
  called once per session at `live_service_runtime.py:442`, so it cannot leak across sessions.
- `frame_samples` outside {160, 320, 480} (10/20/30 ms at 16 kHz) is now refused at
  construction, so a manifest that would raise on *every* frame is refused at admission rather
  than at the first frame of a meeting. The deployed manifest's 160 is unaffected.
Deliberately NOT fixed here: the classification seam that turns a bare third-party exception
into `kind=integrity, retryable=false`. It is shared with H1 (bare `RuntimeError` from
`vllm_runner.py:245`), and fixing it once, there, is better than twice. Recorded in the H1
candidate so it cannot be lost.

### Validation
- `pytest tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py -q` -> **5 passed** (3.4 s).
- **Red proven without touching git history**: `cp` the file aside, `git show HEAD:<file> >`
  it, re-run, `cp` back, and verify the restore by sha256 (`restored_identical=true`).
  Pre-fix: **4 failed / 1 passed**, failing with the shipped signature -
  `webrtcvad accepts only [160, 320, 480] samples, got 48` (48 is the host's own remainder:
  5808 = 36*160 + 48), plus `got 40` and `got 1`. The one node that passes both before and
  after is the aligned 8000-sample control, which is what makes it a control rather than a
  second copy of the red case.
- Offline repro, deployed config: `--speech-provider webrtc --frame-samples 5808 --frames 3`
  went **rc=3 (reproduced) -> rc=0 (survived 17424 accepted samples)**; the 8000-sample control
  still rc=0. Blocker 2 unchanged in the same sweep - `--frames 8` and `--frames 8
  --speech-pattern 0` still rc=3 at accepted_samples=40000, `--frames 24 --speech-pattern 1100`
  still rc=0 - so H3 did not mask H2.
- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **542 passed / 2 skipped /
  368 subtests** in 60.3 s (was 537/2/368; +5 is exactly the new file). No node changed status.
- Swift untouched this iteration; the full Swift gate belongs to H4.
- Nothing was run against the server, the Mac, or any remote host. No service, checkout,
  manifest, certificate, pairing or TCC state was touched, so this iteration has no rollback
  to record beyond `git revert`.

### Learned
1. **The coverage contract decided the fix.** The obvious repair - buffer the remainder and emit
   it next time - is impossible: `live_coordinator._observe_endpoint` requires observations to
   start at the accepted range's start, be gap-free, and end exactly at its end. So the freedom
   is not *when* to answer but *what each observation covers and what produced its answer*. That
   constraint is what makes "carry the audio, report the last decision, label it as carried" the
   only shape that neither fabricates nor drops.
2. **A control node must pass before the fix too.** The aligned 8000-sample node is the evidence
   that the shipped path is byte-identical (8000 = 50*160, carry always empty); a node that only
   passes after the fix could not have shown that.
3. **The suite's blind spot had a second layer.** Iteration 10 recorded that every harness gives
   the session no hard cap; this iteration adds that every harness also feeds the provider a
   sample count that happens to be a whole number of VAD frames. Both are *shapes of the fixture*,
   not missing assertions - which is why review never found either.
4. `tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py` now exists as the home the amendment's third bullet asked
   for: both sides of a seam are the product class, and only the genuinely off-host part is a
   stand-in. H1's `vllm_runner` node and H2's shared-hard-cap node belong in it.

- **Blocker (unchanged):** E3's physical TCC clicks on m4mbp. Still correct to defer - H1 and H2
  are open and the deployed service is still the unfixed `317df4d`.
- Next: **H1**. Decide and record the policy (drop the span, or commit it empty) before the
  patch, put the real `vllm_runner` validation seam under the live coordinator with an empty
  transcript in `tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py`, and fix the shared classification seam
  there so no bare provider exception can make a session terminal.

## Iteration 2 (run 20260728-112922) - H1: a span the decoder cannot parse is committed empty
================================================================================
Target: candidate **H1**, the second blocker the deployed build hits (~3 s, on the first span
of every meeting). Chosen because context.md fixes the order H3 -> H1 -> H2, H3 landed in
iteration 1, and the amendment's own gate (a probe run that survives its full plan) cannot
pass while a leading-silence span kills the session.

### The policy decision the amendment required, and why
**Commit the span empty. Never drop it, never terminal.** Dropping was ruled out by the
product's own accounting, not by preference: `LiveSession.stop` waits for
`committed_samples == accepted_samples`, `live_service_runtime.stop` fails a stop whose
accepted and accounted totals differ, and `accounted_samples` *is* `committed_samples`. So a
dropped span strands the session exactly as surely as a terminal failure does - it just does it
at the end of the meeting instead of the start, and it would break the PRD's "both lanes
accepted and accounted with zero loss" clause. Committing empty keeps the committed prefix
contiguous, advances the prefix hash, prunes retained PCM, and publishes nothing false.
There is a node that *is* this argument: `test_a_meeting_of_pure_silence_stops_with_exact_accounting`.

Files touched (7 tracked; `moss_transcribe_diarize/` + `tests/`, which the second amendment
authorizes):
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/transcription_outcome.py   (new leaf: EmptyTranscriptionError)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/vllm_runner.py             (types the three empty conditions)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_adapters.py           (decode seam: translate + classify)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_coordinator.py        (empty-span path, no identity work)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_session.py            (submit_empty_canonical)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_service_runtime.py    (empty_reason on canonical_processed)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_portal.py             (do not render a blank gap)
  tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py                      (+5 nodes, 5 -> 10)

### The change
1. `vllm_runner._validate_transcription_response` raises the typed `EmptyTranscriptionError`
   for all three of its "the model produced nothing usable" cases. It subclasses `RuntimeError`
   and the messages are byte-identical, so **every batch caller is unchanged** - a batch job
   that returns no transcript for a submitted file must still fail closed. The type lives in a
   new leaf module because `vllm_runner` drags torch/transformers in through `inference_utils`
   and no live module may import that.
2. `RunnerBoundedWavInference.transcribe_pcm` translates that one type into
   `InferenceTranscript(transcript="", generated_tokens=0)` with the measured wall time - an
   empty span still carries an RTF figure instead of vanishing from the gate - and wraps every
   *other* exception from `runner.transcribe` in `LiveProviderError` with the original as
   `__cause__`. That second half is the classification seam H3 deferred: nothing crosses the
   decode seam unclassified.
3. The rule is stated **on the transcript, not on an exception type**:
   `live_coordinator._empty_transcript_reason` (replacing `_transcript_text`, which raised)
   returns `decoder_returned_no_transcript` / `decoder_returned_unparseable_transcript` / None,
   so a decoder that *returns* garbage without raising is handled identically and the fix is not
   welded to vLLM. An empty span skips identity work entirely and commits through the new
   `LiveSession.submit_empty_canonical`, which leaves the identity snapshot version untouched.
   `empty_reason` rides the `canonical_processed` event, so an empty span is visible, not silent.
4. A decoder that *failed* is still terminal, now named. Committing its spans as silence - what a
   blanket catch would do - would render a dead GPU as a blank meeting.

### Validation (all offline on MacStudio; no server, Mac, or remote host was touched)
- `pytest tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py -q` -> **10 passed** (3.6 s), was 5.
- **Red proven without touching git history**: the five product files restored from `HEAD`,
  re-run, restored, restore verified by sha256 (`restored_identical=true`). Pre-fix:
  **5 failed / 5 passed** - the five new nodes red, H3's five still green. The failure is the
  deployed signature exactly:
  `LiveServiceFailureRecord(kind=integrity, code='RuntimeError', message='vLLM transcription
  returned zero parsed segments for .../span-0000.wav.', retryable=False, detail=None)`.
- The leading-silence span the node builds is `(0, 14400, "leading_silence")` - F0's measured
  `frozen_until_sample 14400` reproduced from the deployed endpoint config
  (16000 - pre_speech_padding_samples 1600), not asserted.
- H2 unchanged by this, checked in the same sweep: `live-hardcap-repro.py --frames 8` and
  `--frames 8 --speech-pattern 0` still **rc=3** at accepted_samples=40000; `--frames 24
  --speech-pattern 1100` still rc=0; both webrtc cases still rc=0. So H1 masked nothing.
- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **547 passed / 2 skipped /
  368 subtests** in 59.7 s (was 542/2/368; +5 is exactly the new nodes). No node changed status.
  `tests/test_vllm_runner.py`'s fail-closed node still passes unmodified, which is the batch
  contract's evidence.
- Swift untouched this iteration; the full Swift gate belongs to H4.

### Learned
1. **The accounting decided the policy.** "Drop or commit empty" reads like a taste question and
   is not one: `accounted_samples` is a synonym for `committed_samples`, so the session has no
   representation for audio that was accepted and then discarded. Only one of the two options
   was implementable at all.
2. **F0's root-path sentence was half wrong, and it mattered.** It said the bare `RuntimeError`
   escaped before `live_adapters._validated_segments` could type it. That function is on
   `LiveProvider.decode_canonical`, which **the runtime never builds** - the live decoder is
   `RunnerBoundedWavInference` under `LiveCoordinator`. Fixing where the note pointed would have
   changed nothing on the live path. Corrected in context.md's F0 block.
3. **The endpoint policy *manufactures* the silent span.** `_observe_speech` emits
   `leading_silence` over everything before the padded speech start, so the first span of every
   meeting is silence by construction, not by luck of the input. That is why this fired on span
   0000 on the host, and it is why the node can build it deterministically.
4. **A classification fix has a wrong version that looks right.** Catching everything at the
   decode seam and committing empty would make the suite green and turn a dead GPU into a
   silently blank meeting. The distinction that had to be preserved is "produced nothing" vs
   "failed", which is why the runner-side type exists at all.

- **Blocker (unchanged):** E3's physical TCC clicks on m4mbp. Still correct to defer - H2 is
  open and the deployed service is still the unfixed `317df4d`.
- **New candidate recorded (36, not started, out of scope):** a transient decoder failure still
  ends the meeting. Honest classification, but a real reliability gap for the 300 s
  certification; the fix is bounded per-span retry plus a typed degraded state, and it needs its
  own authorization.
- Next: **H2** - the two hard-cap freezers. `live-hardcap-repro.py --frames 8` is the red case in
  0.4 s, and the regression node must give one `LiveSession` and one `EndpointPolicy` the *same*
  hard cap (no harness in the repo does). Decide which freezer is the single authority, and
  answer the orphan the second reading creates: spans the session freezes by itself are never
  queued for decode.

================================================================================
## Iteration 3 (run 20260728-112922) - H2: one authority decides where a span ends
================================================================================
Target: candidate **H2**, the last of F0's three server blockers. Chosen because context.md
fixes the order H3 -> H1 -> H2, both predecessors landed, and the amendment's own gate (a
probe run that survives its full plan) cannot pass while 2.5 s of endpoint-free audio ends
the meeting.

### The design decision, and why the other reading was rejected
The diagnosis offered two: (A) the session stops freezing on its own and the endpoint policy
stays the single authority, or (B) the coordinator honours `FrameAck.frozen_span_ids` and the
policy's cap goes away. **A.** B inverts the design - a boundary decided by the session would
have to be pushed back into the policy to keep `_open_start` in step, and even then the
policy's own later span can land *behind* the session's cursor and be refused by the same
`ValueError` (session freezes 40000; policy then emits end_silence at 38000). B also has to
solve the orphan separately. A closes the collision and the orphan with one removal.

Files touched (3 tracked product + 6 tests + 1 Ralph tool; `moss_transcribe_diarize/` +
`tests/`, which the second amendment authorizes):
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_session.py          (hard_cap_samples + freezer removed)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/app/live_service_runtime.py  (_require_one_span_cap)
  moss_transcribe_diarize/live_replay.py               (retired knob refused, not ignored)
  tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py                    (+3 nodes, 10 -> 13)
  tests/test_live_session.py                           (+1 node; explicit freeze_until)
  tests/test_live_identity.py                          (explicit freeze_until, 7 sites)
  tests/test_live_service_runtime.py, tests/test_live_api.py,
  tests/test_live_provider_bundle.py                   (declare both caps: deployed shape)
  scripts/ralph-afk/live-hardcap-repro.py              (now a green check, not a repro)

### The change
1. `LiveSession` loses `hard_cap_samples` entirely - parameter, attribute and
   `_freeze_hard_cap_spans`. `accept_frame` freezes nothing and returns `frozen_span_ids=()`.
   The **client's** ack is unaffected: `live_service_runtime.accept_frame` has always rebuilt
   `FrameAck` from `result.frozen_spans` (the coordinator's). That rebuild is precisely why
   session-frozen spans were orphaned - frozen, never queued, never decoded.
2. `LiveServiceRuntime._require_one_span_cap` refuses to open a session unless the endpoint
   policy carries exactly `bounds_config.hard_cap_samples`. This is load-bearing, not tidiness:
   without it, removing the session's freezer turns a `bounds`-capped/`endpoint`-uncapped
   manifest into a session with **no** cap, where one span grows until retention backpressures.
   It immediately caught a real fixture - `tests/test_live_provider_bundle.py`'s manifest
   declared the cap only in `endpoint_config`.
3. `live_replay.py` raises on `session_hard_cap_samples` rather than ignoring it: such a
   manifest was authored against a session that closed its own spans and would replay
   differently now.
4. The only boundary the session still draws is `stop`'s tail flush. It cannot collide - it
   fires only when `accepted > frozen_until`, i.e. only when nothing else closed the tail, and
   on the runtime path `stop_endpoint()` has already closed it.

### Validation (all offline on MacStudio; no server, Mac, or remote host was touched)
- `pytest tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py -q` -> **13 passed** (3.5 s), was 10.
- **Red proven without touching git history**: `live_session.py` and `live_service_runtime.py`
  restored from `HEAD`, the seam harness temporarily handed the cap the pre-fix session took,
  re-run, then all three restored and verified by sha256 (identical). Pre-fix: **3 failed /
  10 passed** - the three new nodes red with the deployed signature exactly,
  `ValueError: frozen span end must advance.` at `live_session.py:237`, for continuous speech
  *and* for opening silence; the ten H3/H1 nodes stayed green.
- `live-hardcap-repro.py --frames 8` flipped **rc=3 -> rc=0**: was `REFUSED ValueError: frozen
  span end must advance.` at accepted_samples=40000, now `survived 8 frames (64000 accepted
  samples)` with `session_frozen_span_ids=[0]` == `coordinator_queued_span_ids=[0]` - the
  orphan closed in the same line as the collision. `--speech-pattern 0` (silence),
  `--frame-samples 1000` (not a frame-size artifact), `--speech-pattern 1100` (endpointing,
  11 spans, all queued) and both webrtc cases are rc=0.
- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider -rs` -> **551 passed /
  2 skipped / 368 subtests** in 59.4 s (was 547/2/368; +4 = 3 seam nodes + 1 session node).
  The two skips printed with `-rs` are `tests/test_large_upload.py:155,175` "Python 3.10
  compatibility contract", the same pre-existing pair as every prior gate.
- Swift untouched this iteration; the full Swift gate belongs to H4.

### Learned
1. **The blind spot was a *shape*, for the third time.** Iteration 1 found "every harness feeds
   the provider a whole number of VAD frames"; iteration 10 found "every harness gives the
   session no hard cap"; this iteration found that the second one is enforced by C2 in
   production and by nothing in the suite. A fixture that omits a production value is invisible
   to review because there is nothing wrong on the line you are reading.
2. **Deleting a mechanism can silently delete a guarantee.** Removing the session's freezer is
   correct, but on its own it would have left a `bounds`-only manifest running uncapped. The
   equality check is the part of this change that is not a deletion, and it is the part that
   keeps the safety property.
3. **The declared-vs-enforced distinction is worth naming.** `bounds_config.hard_cap_samples`
   now declares what the endpoint policy must do; it does not do anything itself. A config
   value that is both a declaration and an independent mechanism is the collision, generalised.
4. **The repro tool had to change with the fix.** `--session-hard-cap` cannot survive the
   removal of the parameter it set; leaving it accepted-and-ignored would have made the tool
   lie about what it was testing. It is now retired with an error that explains the fix, and the
   script is a green check whose red form is only reachable by restoring the pre-fix file.

- **Blocker (unchanged):** E3's physical TCC clicks on m4mbp. Still correct to defer - the
  deployed service is still `317df4d` and carries none of H1/H2/H3.
- Next: **H4** - gate, merge, redeploy. Re-run `live-pipeline-probe.py` against the deployed
  service *after* the redeploy (before it, it still dies at the first unaligned frame); full
  Swift + Python gate; the single merge the second amendment authorizes via `merge-keeper.sh`
  with `expected_main` advanced in-script from `317df4d` (never by CLI override); then push and
  redeploy so all four checkouts return to one exact SHA. Run `merge-keeper.sh` in the
  BACKGROUND. Note for the redeploy: the new `_require_one_span_cap` reads the host manifest,
  which D2's finalizer wrote with `hard_cap_samples 40000` in **both** sections, so it passes -
  but verify that before restarting the service, because a mismatch now refuses every session.

### The merge payload, reviewed against the second amendment's scope

### The pre-redeploy check iteration 3 asked for - done now, not at restart time
Read-only against the host's deployed `live-provider-manifest.json`:
  endpoint_config.hard_cap_samples = 40000, bounds_config.hard_cap_samples = 40000, equal
  speech_provider = {kind: webrtc, frame_samples: 160}
  "session_hard_cap_samples" present anywhere in the document = False
So H2's new `LiveServiceRuntime._require_one_span_cap` admits the deployed manifest instead of
refusing every session, and H3's construction refusal admits 160 (10 ms at 16 kHz is one of the
three legal webrtcvad lengths). Both new checks fail the service **closed**, so this belongs
before a restart, not after one. Recorded as a standing command in context.md's Validation.

### Learned
1. **Both Phase-H fixes added a refusal that the deployed configuration has to satisfy, and
   neither is exercised by anything on the host until a session opens.** A manifest that was
   fine yesterday is now either admitted or fatal, so "does the deployed config still pass the
   new gate" is a distinct pre-deploy step from "do the tests pass". It cost one read-only ssh
   here; discovering it from a dead service after the restart would have cost the rollback.
2. **The gate is unchanged in shape but the numbers moved only on the Python side** (537+2 ->
   551+2, +14 = the 13 seam nodes plus the session node), which is itself the evidence that the
   cycle stayed server-side: a Swift count that moved would mean the amendment's scope leaked.

- **Blocker (unchanged):** E3's physical TCC clicks on m4mbp. Still correct to defer - the
  deployed service is still `317df4d` and carries none of H1/H2/H3.
- Next: **H4 step (b)** - the merge. Join `main` into the feature branch first and prove it
  content-free with `git merge-tree --write-tree` before running it; advance `expected_main`
  from `317df4d...` **in-script** with a comment citing the second amendment (never by CLI
  override); run `merge-keeper.sh` in the **BACKGROUND** (a foreground kill strands a worktree
  holding `main` and blocks the retry; recover with `git -C <wt> merge --abort && git worktree
  remove --force <wt>`). Then (c) publish + redeploy + four-way SHA, and only then (d) the
  probe, which is the amendment's actual gate.

### Learned
1. **A wrong probe signature can read as a passing refusal.** The first H3 check called
   `WebRtcSpeechProvider(sample_rate=16000, frame_samples=5808, aggressiveness=2)` and printed
   "refused ... TypeError" - but the constructor is keyword-only with a REQUIRED `vad=`, so the
   TypeError was the call being wrong, not the manifest being rejected. Re-measured correctly it is
   `LiveProviderBundleAdmissionError` with the frame-length message. Assert the exception TYPE;
   "it raised" is not evidence that the guard fired. Recorded in Validation for the next reader.
2. **`/api/live/descriptor`'s `source_revision` does not move on redeploy.** It still reads
   f9285d69…, because it is a field C2's finalizer stamped into the manifest at D2 time, not the
   running code's revision. It is a plausible-looking wrong answer to "what SHA is deployed" - use
   the four-way `git rev-parse` plus the venv introspection above instead.
3. **The restart needs a poll, and 6 s is not enough.** `/live` came up at 11 s here, consistent
   with F4a's note that a single probe at 6 s returns 000. Model load is in that window.

- **Blocker (partial, this iteration):** m4mbp is offline, so the PRD's "one exact SHA everywhere"
  clause is 3/4. Not fatal and not on the critical path: `macos/` is byte-identical between
  317df4d and b817871, so the app installed on m4mbp in G6 is already current at the new SHA, and
  the remaining work is a `git fetch && git checkout` with **no rebuild and no reinstall**.
- **Blocker (unchanged):** E3's physical TCC clicks on m4mbp - and it is now doubly blocked, since
  the Mac is not even reachable. But E3 is finally *worth* spending once step (d) passes.
- Next: **H4 step (d)** - the amendment's actual gate. Re-run
  `python3 scripts/ralph-afk/live-pipeline-probe.py` against the redeployed service and require a
  run that survives its full plan with committed samples advancing (the pre-fix runs died ~1.1 s in
  at the first unaligned frame). Then retry the m4mbp checkout to close the four-way SHA check.

### Blocker 4 - the failure, then the mechanism, then the control
Failure (from the probe, tick 8, accepted_samples 53808):
  409 {kind: identity_commit, code: canonical_not_submitted, retryable: false,
       detail: {span_id: 1, identity_status: "failed"}}
  every later frame and `stop` got the same; the host journal has **no traceback** - a typed
  refusal, not an escaping exception, which is why nothing in the journal explains it.

The reason string is unobservable from outside: it is written into a *proposed* identity snapshot
that is never committed, and only `identity_status` reaches the event and the failure detail. So
this iteration built `scripts/ralph-afk/live-identity-seam-probe.py`, which rebuilds the same two
seams the runtime builds from the same manifest (bounded decoder over the vLLM runner; bounded
causal identity preparer over the manifest's wespeaker evidence provider) and reports `reason`.
Run on the host with the service's own venv, mutating nothing:

  input = span 1's audio rebuilt sample-for-sample on MacStudio from the pipeline probe's own
          build_schedule/build_lane_track, system lane shifted 137 ms, mixed with live_mixer's own
          _HEADROOM_GAIN/_LIMITER_* constants, sliced [12208:52208] = 40000 samples = 2.5 s
  decode -> "[0.11][S01] Good morning everyone. This is the microphone.[2.51]"   (25 tokens, RTF 0.478)
  prepare(with real evidence)  -> status=failed reason=**timestamp_outside_span**  would_publish=false
  prepare(without evidence)    -> status=failed reason=timestamp_outside_span      would_publish=false
  rc=4, verdict "pre-evidence check"           report: /tmp/moss-h4d-identity-span1.json

  CONTROL, same audio + 0.5 s of trailing silence (3.0 s span):
  decode -> "[0.00][S01]Good morning everyone. This is the microphone.[2.54]"
  -> **prepared**, would_publish=true, rc=0                report: /tmp/moss-h4d-identity-tail.json

  Baseline, the manifest's own golden.wav (2.1 s): generated_tokens 0, transcript "" ->
  coordinator_empty_reason `decoder_returned_no_transcript`, i.e. H1's path.  /tmp/moss-h4d-identity.json

Mechanism, with line anchors:
  1. the model's closing marker lands at ~= the duration of the audio it is handed. For a 2.50 s
     span whose speech runs to the end it emits **2.51** - one 0.01 s quantisation tick past.
  2. `BoundedCausalIdentityPreparer.prepare` live_identity.py:101-102 rejects `segment.end >
     duration` with **no tolerance** -> status="failed", reason="timestamp_outside_span".
  3. `LiveSession._identity_preparation_is_current` live_session.py:449 admits **only**
     status == "prepared", so `submit_prepared_canonical` returns False.
  4. `live_service_runtime.py:745-751` turns that False into a non-retryable terminal failure of
     the whole session.
A hard-cap span is 2.5 s of continuous speech with no endpoint **by construction**, so this fires
on any speaker who does not pause - the ordinary case, not an edge case.

Two more defects visible in the same code, recorded so one amendment can settle them together:
  - `LiveSession._canonical_validation_error` live_session.py:436-442 carries the SAME strict bound
    check, so relaxing only the identity side moves the failure instead of fixing it.
  - an `abstain` preparation - the DESIGNED outcome for ambiguous identity or speaker capacity
    (live_identity.py:106,121,127) - is terminal by the same line 449. Design intent ("don't
    relabel") and implementation ("kill the meeting") disagree for a second input class.
Positive side-evidence: the wespeaker ONNX evidence provider **works** on the host - the control
run reached `prepared` through it, the first time that has been observed.

### Rollback: applied and verified
  DELETE https://127.0.0.1:7861/api/live/devices/ralph-h4d-probe-20260728T123400Z -> 200 (repeat 200)
  live-auth.json: that device revoked=true; m4mbp AB600574-...-652AB064A70B still revoked=false
  moss-live-web MainPID 338545 / moss-web MainPID 301112, both NRestarts=0, both active - untouched
  host /tmp WAVs and .b64 blobs deleted (no audio left on the server); JSON reports kept
  from MacStudio afterwards: /live 200, /api/live/descriptor 200, batch / 200, /api/jobs 200

### Learned
1. **A gate that fails is still the gate working.** Three blockers were fixed and deployed, and the
   run that proved it is the same run that found the fourth. The probe's reach (tick 1 -> tick 8,
   0 -> 1 committed spans) is the measurement that says the cycle was worth it.
2. **A typed refusal with no traceback is harder to diagnose than a crash.** H1/H3 left tracebacks
   in the journal; this one left nothing, because the process classified it correctly and then
   threw away the one word that named it. `reason` reaching the failure detail is not a nicety -
   it is the difference between reading a log line and building a host-side probe.
3. **Reproduce the exact input, not a similar one.** golden.wav (the manifest's own fixture) decodes
   to empty and proves nothing about this defect. Rebuilding span 1 from the probe's own schedule -
   same voices, same offset, same mixer arithmetic, same sample window - reproduced it first try,
   and the +0.5 s-silence control then isolated the boundary condition in one more run.
4. **`say`-driven probe audio is reusable evidence.** Both runs cost one vLLM request each (~1-2.5 s)
   and no service disturbance, so this seam can now be re-measured after any fix without a Mac.

  post-merge freeze resumed at 9e0780a, `merge-keeper.sh` refuses a fourth merge, and no test in the
  repo covers this seam (nothing puts the real decoder's timestamps under the real identity
  preparer). The loop must not touch product source for it without that authorization.
  What the amendment has to decide is written out in candidate 37: (a) the timestamp tolerance,
  answered ONCE and applied in both live_identity.prepare and
  LiveSession._canonical_validation_error; (b) whether a non-`prepared` preparation may be terminal

### The measurement
  hard-cap (2.5 s) spans failing identity preparation ... 2 of 33 = 6.1 %
    span-s92208 -> "[0.06][S01]Receiving two separate audio, let us check whether[2.42]
                    [0.11][S02]My voice is arriving on different lanes.[2.51]"   (end 2.51 > 2.50)
    span-s268208 -> single segment ending 2.52 in a 2.50 s span
  overshoot values ......... +0.01, +0.01, +0.01, +0.02   (max = TWO quantisation ticks)
  the other 31 spans ....... end 0.00 to -0.68 s BEFORE the span end
  end == duration exactly .. 1 span, admitted (the check is strict `>`)
  start < 0 ................ never observed in 44 decodes; min start 0.00
  determinism .............. 3 decodes of identical bytes -> identical transcript and verdict;
                             4 further spans identical across the two independent rounds
  derived .................. P(60 s canary survives) ~ 0.22, P(300 s survives) ~ 0.0006,
                             expected ~16 spans ~ 41 s of continuous speech to the first kill

### Why this makes the fix a clamp
1. A one-tick tolerance is already refuted by the data: the larger overshoot is two ticks. Any
   fixed epsilon is a guess about a tail sampled 44 times; a clamp cannot be exceeded.
2. `live_provider_bundle.py:727-728` (`_speaker_intervals_by_label`, inside the wespeaker evidence
   provider) ALREADY does `max(0.0, start)` / `min(duration, end)` on these very segments -- and
   `prepare` refuses at `live_identity.py:101-102` before it can be called. The clamp exists and is
   unreachable; the fix moves it one call earlier, it does not invent a policy.
3. A retry cannot rescue a refusal: same bytes, same transcript, every time.
4. The bound is written three times (`live_identity.py:101`, `live_session.py:438`,
   `live_adapters.py:387`) and tested zero times - `timestamp_outside_span` / "timestamps outside
   frozen span" appear nowhere under `tests/`. The third copy is dead on the live path (nothing
   constructs a `LiveProvider`; the coordinator calls `decoder.transcribe_pcm` directly), so it is
   a fourth divergence waiting to happen. Answer the rule once, in a shared helper.
5. Observability (37c) is structural, not cosmetic: `submit_prepared_canonical`
   (`live_session.py:267-283`) returns bare `False` for six distinct conditions and
   `live_service_runtime.py:745` reports all six as `canonical_not_submitted`.

### Rollback: applied and verified
  rm -rf /tmp/moss-span-sweep /tmp/live-identity-seam-probe.py   -> directory ABSENT afterwards
  the 44 span wavs were deleted inside the same ssh invocation that decoded them (no audio left)
  moss-live-web MainPID 338545 / moss-web MainPID 301112, both NRestarts=0, both active - untouched
  live-runs/ 0 entries; no session opened, no pairing minted, no auth state written
  NOT deleted (not this iteration's artifacts): /tmp/mtd_span_{2.5,5,7.5,rt}.wav dated 2026-07-27
  16:23-16:32 on the host, left over from a Phase-D decode measurement. They are probe output, not
  service output, but they should go before the secret-hygiene clause is signed off.

### Learned
1. **One observation names a defect; it cannot size one.** Iteration 7's single span said "2.51 in
   a 2.50 s span", which reads like a rounding tick and invites a one-tick tolerance. Thirty-three
   spans said the overshoot reaches two ticks and fires on 6 % of them - the same defect, but a
   different fix and a different urgency.
2. **Reuse the probe, vary the input.** The expensive part (the host seam probe) already existed;
   this iteration only had to generate inputs and loop. Building the cuts out of the pipeline
   probe's own schedule/mixer means a cut is what the service would have mixed by construction,
   which is why round 1 reproduced a failure on the first attempt.
3. **A deterministic decoder is worse news than a flaky one**, and worth knowing before designing:
   it removes retry from the option list entirely.
4. `sha256sum ... | head -3` under `set -o pipefail` aborts a remote script with rc=141 (SIGPIPE) -
   it cost one full 35-span round. Never pipe into `head` in a `pipefail` script.
5. zsh does not word-split an unquoted `$CUTS` string; build an array under `bash -c`.

- **BLOCKER (unchanged, still the critical path):** blocker 4 needs a **THIRD prd.md amendment**.
  What it must authorize is now three lines: (a) clamp decoder segment timestamps into the span,
  once, in a shared helper that the three copies call; (b) rule that a non-`prepared` preparation
  (`abstain` above all) commits the span un-relabeled instead of ending the meeting; (c) carry the
  preparation's `reason` into the failure detail and the `canonical_processed` event. Candidate 36
  (a transient decoder failure is terminal) is the same shape on the same path and should be
  settled with it. The regression test goes in tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py, and the
  after-fix gate is free: re-run the sweep, and spans 12208+10*8000 and 12208+32*8000 must publish.
- **Blocker (unchanged):** m4mbp offline, so "one exact SHA everywhere" stays 3/4 at b817871.
- **Blocker (unchanged):** E3's TCC clicks - now with a number attached: at 6.1 % per span a 60 s
  canary has a ~22 % chance of even finishing, so spending the human step today buys an aborted run.
- Next: nothing on the critical path is authorized. If the operator lands the amendment, the fix is
  scoped and the gate is written. Otherwise the only open mechanical step is the m4mbp checkout.
