### The decision, stated once
A decoder timestamp outside its span is CLAMPED INTO the span, never refused. No tolerance
constant exists anywhere in the tree after this change. Four reasons, all from iteration 8's sweep:
the measured overshoot reaches TWO quantisation ticks so a one-tick tolerance still loses a span;
any fixed epsilon is a guess about a tail sampled 44 times, while a clamp cannot be exceeded;
live_provider_bundle._speaker_intervals_by_label already clamps these same segments one call later,
so this moves an existing policy to where the value is first read; and the decoder is deterministic
for identical bytes, so no retry rescues a refusal.
The rule lives in ONE function - `span_segments(transcript, *, sample_count)` in the new leaf
`app/live_span_bounds.py` - and all three former copies call it. Fixing fewer than three would have
relocated the failure: live_identity.py:101 (the one H4d hit), live_session.py:438 (the same
submission path, one call later) and live_adapters.py:387 (dead on the live path today, a fourth
divergence tomorrow). LIVE_SAMPLE_RATE moved into the leaf and is re-exported by live_session so no
import site changed.

                                                               (was 551/2/368 - the delta is 11)

### What the 11 nodes assert
1  the H4d transcript ("[0.11][S01]Good morning everyone...[2.51]" in a 2.50 s hard-cap span)
   through the real runtime: no terminal failure, span 0..40000 committed, "[2.5]" present and
   "2.51" absent, identity_status "prepared", speaker-0001 born, meeting continues past it
2  the same overshoot through LiveSession.submit_canonical alone (the second copy)
3  the same overshoot through LiveProvider.decode_canonical (the third copy)
4-9 the clamp table, driven by the sweep's measured values: +0.01 and +0.02 -> 2.5; end == duration
   unchanged; wholly inside unchanged; wholly after -> a zero-length segment at the span end; a
   two-segment transcript where only the offending segment moves
10 a negative start yields no segments at all (see below)
11 an unparseable transcript is still () - the helper does not decide that question

### Learned
1. **One half of the bound was unreachable.** `parse_transcript("[-0.4][S01]x[1.0]")` returns
   nothing: the parser does not accept a negative timestamp token, so `segment.start < 0` could
   never fire and a negative start would have been classified "unparseable" instead. That explains
   the sweep's min start of 0.00 across 44 decodes structurally rather than statistically. The
   clamp still covers it, cheaply, for a future parser or a non-vLLM decoder.
2. **Put the shared rule in a module the pre-fix files do not need.** Because
   `live_span_bounds.py` does not exist at HEAD, restoring all three consumers still leaves the
   test file importable, so the red proof shows the defect rather than an ImportError. Had the
   helper gone into live_session.py, the same restore would have produced a collection error and
   proven nothing.
3. **Clamping in the preparer changes what identity sees, and that is the point.** The evidence
   provider and the relabeled transcript now receive in-span values, so a published span cannot
   claim audio it does not hold - the clamp keeps the committed prefix honest, which a widened
   bound would not have.

- Next: **J2** (candidate 39) - a non-`prepared` preparation, `abstain` above all, must commit the
  span un-relabeled instead of ending the meeting. It is now the only thing on this path between
  the branch and a hard-cap span that publishes. Then J3 (transient decoder failure), J4 (`reason`
  must reach the failure detail and the canonical_processed event), then J5's gate/merge/redeploy.
- **Not done here, deliberately:** J1's real-audio confirmation on the host (the sweep's two
  known-failing spans, 12208+10*8000 and 12208+32*8000, must publish). The fix is not deployed and
  the freeze forbids deploying it before Phase J's merge, so that is J5's gate. It costs no human
  step when it comes.
- Blocker (unchanged): m4mbp offline, so "one exact SHA everywhere" stays 3/4 at b817871.
- Blocker (unchanged): E3's TCC clicks stay unspent until Phase J is deployed and both probes are
  green - the same rule that has now held four times.

                                                    Validation gained J2's red/green recipe

### The ruling, stated once
NO PREPARATION THE PREPARER RETURNS MAY END THE MEETING. `BoundedCausalIdentityPreparer` is a pure
proposer - it mutates nothing and it already catches its own evidence provider's exceptions - so
every outcome it can name is a statement about *who spoke*, not about whether the session can
continue. A span whose identity did not resolve publishes its WORDS WITH NO SPEAKER ATTRIBUTED.
Only the claim is dropped.
This is deliberately broader than "abstain must not be terminal". Ruling only on `abstain` would
have left `failed:evidence_provider_failed:*` - the wespeaker ONNX provider on the host - as the
fifth blocker, which is exactly the per-blocker-per-cycle pattern the third amendment exists to
stop. The genuine precondition violation is already terminal one call earlier and did not need a
carve-out: `RunnerBoundedWavInference.transcribe_pcm` calls `_validate_pcm_length` and raises
before the preparer ever sees the span, so the preparer's own `pcm_span_mismatch` is unreachable
in production.
Three pieces, each in the module that owns its question:
  live_session.UNATTRIBUTED_SPEAKER = "S00" - the wire grammar admits only S+digits and canonical
    display labels are S{index+1:02d}, so S00 is the one such token a canonical mapping can never
    produce. A wire-format constant of the same kind as S01.
  live_identity.unattributed_transcript(transcript, *, sample_count) - the words with that marker,
    through J1's same span_segments clamp, so an unattributed span is no less honest about the
    audio it holds. "" for a transcript that parses to nothing.
  live_session.submit_unlabeled_canonical(...) - publishes with the identity snapshot left
    BYTE-IDENTICAL (no speaker born, no version advanced, no capacity burned) and refuses a
    transcript naming anything but the marker.
The coordinator is the single decision point, and it renders from the DECODER's transcript rather
than from `preparation.relabeled_transcript` - so a preparer that leaves local labels in a field it
never relabeled cannot publish `S01` as canonical. If the rendering has no words at all the span
commits empty (H1's path) and `empty_reason` names it, which makes the rule total rather than
dependent on an invariant two functions away.
Why publish rather than drop: `stop` waits for `committed_samples == accepted_samples` - the same
accounting constraint that decided H1. A withheld span strands the session until the drain deadline.

### Commands and results
python3 -m pytest tests/test_live_pipeline_seams.py -q  -> 32 passed (24 + 8)

### The three tests that had to be re-pointed (the honest cost, not a weakened gate)
Three existing tests used an abstention to MANUFACTURE a terminal failure, i.e. they asserted the
defect. Each now uses a genuinely non-submittable preparation instead, and each asserts more than
it did before:
1. test_live_service_runtime.py::test_runtime_terminal_failure_is_session_local and
2. test_live_service_replay.py::test_identity_commit_failure_writes_typed_terminal_artifacts
   -> PreparingIdentity(stale_base_version=True): a preparation built against identity state the
   session has already moved past. That is the ONLY remaining way a canonical submission refuses,
   and it is a statement about the session (a stale proposal would overwrite newer identity state)
   rather than about who spoke, so it stays terminal by the amendment's own rule.
3. test_live_replay.py: the `identity` row (exit code 4) left the hard-failure table and became its
   own node - the same manifest now exits 0, publishes the span unattributed, keeps accounting
   exact and invents no speaker. EXIT CODE 4 IS NO LONGER MANUFACTURABLE FROM A REPLAY MANIFEST;
   `run_replay`'s `not result.submitted` guard stays for a coordinator contract violation. A lab
   harness stricter than the service would rebuild the divergence Phase J exists to close.

### Learned
1. **A red proof needs the shared name to live in a file it does not restore.** J1 learned this by
   putting the clamp in a new leaf. Here the same rule chose which file to restore rather than
   which file to write: UNATTRIBUTED_SPEAKER and unattributed_transcript are in live_session.py and
   live_identity.py, both tracked at HEAD, so restoring all three product files would have produced
   an ImportError and proven nothing. Restoring only live_coordinator.py - the decision point -
   reds exactly the behaviour and leaves the two contract nodes green, which is the correct
   partition: they test the other two files.
2. **Rebuild the published text from the decoder, not from the preparation.** The obvious
   implementation reads `preparation.relabeled_transcript`, which for a non-prepared preparation is
   the raw decoder transcript with its LOCAL `S01` labels. Publishing that would have asserted the
   very link the abstention declined to make - `S01` in span 3 is not `S01` in span 1 - and no test
   in the tree would have caught it, because the fakes all return an already-labeled string.
3. **Tests that manufacture a failure through a designed behaviour become load-bearing.** Three
   suites reached for `status="abstain"` because it was the cheapest way to get `submitted=False`.
   That is worth noticing before writing such a test: a defect used as a fixture is a defect the
   suite will defend.

- Next: **J3** (candidate 40) - a transient decoder failure must not be terminal, while a permanent
  one stays terminal and named. Starting facts, verified this iteration:
  `live_service_runtime._process_in_flight_item` turns EVERY exception from `prepare_work_item`
  into `_failure_from_exception`, and `RunnerBoundedWavInference.transcribe_pcm`
  (live_adapters.py:236-254) wraps the runner's `ConnectionResetError` - and every other exception -
  into one untyped `LiveProviderError`. So a socket reset mid-meeting and a permanently
  misconfigured endpoint are indistinguishable today; that classification is the work. Then J4
  (`reason` must reach the failure detail and the canonical_processed event), then J5's
  gate/merge/redeploy.

### The ruling, stated once
A FAILURE A LATER ATTEMPT COULD ANSWER DIFFERENTLY IS TRANSIENT AND DEGRADES; A FAILURE ANY LATER
ATTEMPT WOULD ANSWER IDENTICALLY IS PERMANENT AND STAYS TERMINAL. AN OUTAGE THAT DOES NOT END IS NOT
TRANSIENT HOWEVER IT STARTED.
The classification is made where the evidence is - `vllm_runner._post_multipart`, the one place that
still holds the HTTP status. Before this it flattened HTTP 4xx, HTTP 5xx and every URLError into a
single bare RuntimeError whose only distinguishing feature was English prose, so no downstream code
could classify without parsing a message. Transient: 408/425/429, every 5xx, any URLError (refused,
reset, unresolved, timed out) and a bare TimeoutError from a slow read. Permanent: every other
status - a verdict on the request itself, which would be returned identically forever.
`LiveProviderTransientError` subclasses `LiveProviderError`, so every existing caller that treats a
decode failure as a failure is unchanged; only the coordinator, which owns the span, acts on it.
`transcribe_pcm` also maps bare ConnectionError/TimeoutError so the rule holds for ANY runner, not
just the one that types its own transport failures.
The policy is in the coordinator, the same single decision point J2 used:
  DECODE_ATTEMPTS_PER_SPAN = 2. The bytes are identical and nothing is committed until an attempt
    answers, so a retry can only add an answer - it can never publish a span twice. No delay: what
    this recovers is a dropped connection, and a decoder that is gone refuses instantly.
  MAX_CONSECUTIVE_UNANSWERED_SPANS = 3, RESET BY ANY SPAN THAT DECODES. Below the line a span is
    committed empty with empty_reason="decoder_did_not_answer" (H1's accounting path, so stop can
    still drain); at it, a LiveProviderError naming the consecutive count ends the session.
Why a count of spans and not a timer: no clock in the coordinator, the unit is what the policy is
about, and it separates "a blip every few minutes" from "the GPU is gone" without a wall-time
threshold. At the 2.5 s span cap the worst case is ~7.5 s of blank meeting before the outage is named.
H1's line survives: a dead decoder still may not render as a blank meeting - it renders as at most
two empty spans and then a named terminal failure.

### The twelve nodes
- an eight-row status table through the REAL runner (URLError, 503, 429, 500, TimeoutError transient;
  400, 401, 404 permanent), asserting the type rather than the message.
- one dropped connection mid-meeting: two spans, both published with their words, THREE transport
  attempts for them - the retry is visible in the count, not inferred.
- a 503 outage: two spans degrade (empty, named, accounted), the third ends the session, the message
  carries both "consecutive spans" and "503", and the attempt count is exactly 3 x 2.
- a meeting that blinks five times but never three in a row: alive, four named-empty spans, two with
  words, committed == accounted.
- a 400: terminal at the first span after EXACTLY ONE attempt - the discriminator that proves this is
  not a retry-everything.
One existing node was re-pointed: test_a_decoder_that_failed_is_not_a_span_with_nothing_to_say used a
ConnectionResetError as its example of a failed decoder. That input is transient by this ruling, so
it now uses ValueError("model weights are not loaded") and additionally asserts the error is NOT
transient. Same claim, an example that still means it.

### Learned
1. **THE STUB WAS SITTING ON THE CODE UNDER TEST.** The first version of the transient nodes passed
   nothing: StubbedTransportVllmRunner overrides `_post_multipart`, which is exactly where the status
   code becomes a typed outcome, so the tests exercised the stub's own re-raise. Moving the stub down
   one layer - `urlopen`, with a CannedHttpResponse or a factory that raises a fresh exception per
   attempt - made the product's request build, response unpack and transport classification all real,
   and every earlier node in the file gained that coverage for free. The rule this file already
   states ("stub only what is genuinely off-host") had drifted one layer too high, and only a test
   that needed the lower layer could notice.
2. **A retry is safe exactly when nothing has been committed and the input is fixed.** The span's PCM
   is already frozen and retained, and no session state moves until submit, so a second attempt has
   no way to double-publish. That is why the retry could go in without a dedup key or an idempotency
   token - the ordering the session already enforces is doing that work.
3. **Per-span rulings cannot express "it never came back".** Committing an unanswered span empty is
   right for one span and wrong for a hundred. The consecutive counter is the smallest thing that
   makes both true, and it has to be reset by success or a long meeting accumulates its way to a
   terminal failure with no outage anywhere in it - which is the node that proves it.

  code=exc.__class__.__name__, so J3's own outage arrives as code='LiveProviderError' with
  detail=None and only its message carries the facts;
  (ii) submit_prepared_canonical still returns bare False for six conditions all reported as

  gate, the single authorized merge (expected_main 317df4d..., advanced in-script), push, redeploy.

### The ruling, stated once
A REFUSAL CARRIES THE WORD THAT NAMES IT OUT OF THE PROCESS, AND A REFUSAL THAT NAMES NOTHING CANNOT
BE CONSTRUCTED.
`CanonicalSubmission.__post_init__` is the second half of that sentence: `submitted=False` with no
refusal raises. The shape that cost H4d a host-side probe is now unwritable rather than discouraged.
Eighteen refusal words replace the bare `False` on the three live submit paths - four shared span
words, nine `identity_preparation_*`, three `canonical_*` validation words, two `unattributed_*` -
and iteration 8's finding ("six distinct conditions, one code") is closed at the source rather than
papered over at the report.
The other two inputs answered in the same pass:
  - the preparer's `reason` now rides the canonical_processed event. After J2 it is the ONLY thing
    that distinguishes an abstention (identity working correctly on undecidable audio) from an
    evidence-provider outage (an operator's problem): both publish unattributed, both keep the
    meeting alive, and both used to look identical from outside.
  - `_failure_from_exception` gives every arm an `error_type` detail and gives a decode failure the
    stable code `canonical_decode_failed` instead of `exc.__class__.__name__`. A class name used as a
    code means adding a subclass silently renames a failure the operator reads - which J3 had just
    done by adding LiveProviderTransientError. The seam's facts travel as fields on
    LiveProviderError.detail: {span_id, cause} at the decode seam, plus consecutive_unanswered_spans
    when the coordinator ends a meeting for an outage.

### The six nodes
- an abstention and an evidence-provider outage, parametrized: same published span, same terminal
  state (none), told apart ONLY by identity_reason (speaker_capacity_exceeded vs
  evidence_provider_failed:RuntimeError).
- the stale preparation - the only refusal still reachable after J2 - naming itself in the event AND
  in the terminal detail. Its identity_status is "prepared", which is the point: the field the old
  detail carried says nothing at all about why the session refused it.

  detail=None.

### Learned
1. **THE FAILURE PATH COULD FAIL ON THE FAILURE, AND IT WAS REACHABLE.** LiveServiceFailureRecord
   refuses an empty message, so `raise ValueError()` anywhere the coordinator can reach made
   _failure_from_exception raise INSIDE the except block: the session was left with no terminal
   record at all and the caller got a complaint about failure messages in place of the fault. Found
   by writing the test for the guard rather than by reading the code - I had assumed it defensive.
   `_failure_message` falls back to the exception's type; a poor name beats none.
2. **A `bool` return is a lossy encoding of a decision, and the loss compounds downstream.** Six
   conditions became one `False`, one `False` became one `canonical_not_submitted`, and by the time
   it reached the wire the only surviving fact was `identity_status`, which after J2 describes a
   DIFFERENT question. Returning the reason at the point of refusal fixed all three layers at once;
   nothing downstream needed a lookup table.
3. **The invariant is worth more than the words.** Eighteen refusal strings will drift. A dataclass
   that cannot be constructed as a silent refusal will not: the next `return False` on this path is
   a TypeError at the call site, not a mystery in a journal three weeks later.

RunnerBoundedWavInference over the real VllmRunner, and the real BoundedCausalIdentityPreparer; the
`prepared_by` hook WRAPS the real preparer rather than replacing it. Exactly two things are stubbed,
both genuinely off-host: the native webrtcvad wheel (by a class enforcing only its documented
10/20/30 ms length contract) and urllib.request.urlopen.

### The governing rule, checked by reading rather than by counting tests
After J1-J4 nothing the DECODER PRODUCES can end a meeting: a timestamp outside the span is clamped,
an empty or unparseable transcript commits empty, a transient failure retries then commits empty, and
every preparation the preparer returns publishes - `BoundedCausalIdentityPreparer.prepare` has no
`raise` path at all, its one LiveIdentityError is caught and turned into an abstention. What remains
terminal is session state or a genuine outage: stale_epoch / unknown_span / span_out_of_order /
span_sample_mismatch, a stale identity base version, a permanent decoder refusal, three consecutive
unanswered spans, unqueued_frozen_span, and the stop-drain deadline. Each is a session that cannot
continue, which is exactly what the third amendment asked for.

### Learned
1. UNLIKE H4a, THERE IS NO PRE-REDEPLOY CONFIGURATION RISK THIS TIME, and that is worth stating
   rather than assuming. H1/H2/H3 each added a refusal the deployed manifest had to satisfy, so a
   redeploy could have failed the service closed. Grepping the product diff for new admission
   refusals finds none - Phase J adds no configuration constraint, only policy inside the session.
   The read-only manifest check was run anyway (it costs one ssh and it proves J5c's path is live),
   but the risk it guards is absent here.
2. THE COVERAGE REVIEW WAS WORTH DOING AS ARITHMETIC, NOT AS PROSE. Selecting each amendment clause
   with -k and summing gave 51 against a 50-node file, and `sort | uniq -d` over the collections
   named the single overlap in one command. That is a much stronger statement than "the nodes look
   comprehensive": every node in the file belongs to a clause the amendment names, and no clause is
   unrepresented.
3. THE SWIFT COUNT NOT MOVING IS EVIDENCE, NOT A NON-EVENT. 139 unchanged across H4a and J5a is how
   a server-only cycle proves it stayed server-only; a moved Swift count would mean the amendment's
   scope leaked into macos/.

- Next: J5 step (b) - the merge. Join main into the feature branch first and prove it content-free
  with `git merge-tree --write-tree` BEFORE running it; advance expected_main from 317df4d... to
  b817871... IN-SCRIPT with a comment citing the third amendment (never by CLI override) and COMMIT
  that before running - the script refuses a dirty tree and the commit becomes the feature tip the
  merge captures; run merge-keeper.sh in the BACKGROUND (a foreground kill skips its EXIT trap and
  strands a worktree holding main, which then blocks the retry; recover with
  `git -C <wt> merge --abort && git worktree remove --force <wt>`). Then (c) publish + redeploy, and
  only then (d) both probes, which are the amendment's actual gate.
- Blocker (unchanged): m4mbp offline, so "one exact SHA everywhere" stays 3/4 at b817871.
- Blocker (unchanged): E3's TCC clicks stay unspent until Phase J is deployed and both probes are
  green - the same rule that has now held seven times.

run 20260728-112922 | iteration 14 | J5 step (b) - Phase J's one authorized merge

Target: candidate 42, step (b) - the third prd.md amendment's single authorized no-ff merge to
main. Chosen because J5a recorded the gate green at 517306b and (b) is the only thing between a
reviewed branch and a redeployable SHA. The only tracked file this iteration changed is
scripts/ralph-afk/merge-keeper.sh (the guard's expected_main), which the amendment names explicitly
("advance expected_main in-script, never by CLI override"). No product source changed.

### The two fences, in the order that matters
1. HISTORY JOIN, PROVEN CONTENT-FREE BEFORE IT WAS MADE
   git merge-base --is-ancestor main HEAD    -> NO      (b817871 lives only on main)
   git merge-tree --write-tree main HEAD     -> 7141ec53b334845a8849835c2abdcb19c4dd1a03
                                                = HEAD's own tree, so the join adds no content
   git merge --no-ff main                    -> 3ba0f742ad35d56190965d49962b58ed5f3189bd
   git diff 9d68d2d 3ba0f74 -- .             -> EMPTY   (tree still 7141ec53)
   git diff --name-only 517306b 3ba0f74 -- ':!scripts/ralph-afk'  -> EMPTY
       i.e. the product tree J5a measured is byte-identical to the one being merged
   git merge-base --is-ancestor main HEAD    -> YES     (honestly, not by loosening the check)
2. GUARD ADVANCED IN-SCRIPT AND COMMITTED FIRST
   merge-keeper.sh expected_main 317df4d... -> b817871..., comment citing the THIRD amendment
   (J1 clamp / J2 unattributed publish / J3 transient retry / J4 named refusal) and stating that a
   FIFTH merge still fails here.
   dry run BEFORE the edit -> rc=1 "ERROR: main moved from expected pre-merge SHA 317df4d..."
   dry run AFTER  the edit -> rc=0 "PASS feature=3ba0f74 main_before=b817871"
   committed as d35be63 -> which is the feature tip the merge then captured (the real run refuses a
   dirty tree, so this order is not optional).

### The merge
Run in the BACKGROUND per the H4 lesson (a foreground kill skips the EXIT trap and strands a
worktree holding main, which blocks the retry). ~2 min, no stall.
  Keeper merge: PASS feature=d35be63b3751a913cae4b2b287cdcff73c7e23d6
                     main=6a540fe086cf819ba0e07a948da9fec0766202c3
  main^1 = b817871414fcc8f609c6f5eb2898ec2957c7768c   (the third merge)
  main^2 = d35be63b3751a913cae4b2b287cdcff73c7e23d6   (the feature tip)
  merge tree = feature tree = b1f4ab91a2bd2f65166540c52d3296c8dbbc4ffb
  git diff d35be63 6a540fe -> EMPTY, so the merge commit carries exactly the feature tree
  git worktree list -> ONE entry (the primary). The EXIT trap ran; nothing holds main.
  git status --porcelain -> clean

So a FIFTH merge is refused until a fourth amendment advances the line in-script. The post-merge

### Learned
1. THE JOIN'S is-ancestor CHECK PRINTED "NO" BEFORE AND "YES" AFTER, AND BOTH HALVES ARE EVIDENCE.
   Recording only the "YES" would make the fence look vacuous; recording the "NO" first is what
   shows the join was necessary and that the check was satisfied by adding history, not by
   loosening the test. Third merge in a row where this is the whole discipline.
2. THE PAYLOAD PREDICTION FROM (a) HELD TO THE BYTE (15 files, +1475/-134). That is the point of
   splitting gate from merge: (a)'s review becomes a falsifiable claim that (b) checks against the
   merge commit itself, rather than a description of a branch nobody re-measures.
3. macOS HAS NO `timeout`. A foreground blocking wait built on `timeout 900 ...` fails with
   "command not found" - use `grep -m1 ... < <(tail -f log)` (process substitution, so bash does
   not wait on tail) and let the tool's own timeout bound it. Cost one wasted wait this iteration.
4. THE SWIFT COUNT NOT MOVING IS STILL THE SERVER-ONLY PROOF, and it now holds across three
   measurements (H4a, J5a, and this merge worktree): 139 every time.

- Next: J5 step (c) - publish + redeploy. `git push origin main` (fast-forward b817871..6a540fe),
  fetch and detach the host checkout at /mnt/d/Coding/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize to 6a540fe, verify the
  tree hash matches `git rev-parse 6a540fe^{tree}` = b1f4ab91 and take an independent content hash
  (sha256 of live_session.py on both hosts), then `systemctl --user restart moss-live-web.service`
  and POLL /live - a single probe before ~10 s returns 000. Re-verify the served leaf still hashes
  to the D2 pin a35ca9fc... (every paired Mac depends on it) and that moss-web.service's MainPID did
  NOT move. Confirm J1-J4 are deployed by VENV INTROSPECTION in the deployed checkout - never by the
  SHA alone and never by /api/live/descriptor's source_revision, which is a stamped manifest field
  that does not move on redeploy. Suggested probes: `live_span_bounds` importable and
  `span_segments` present (J1); `live_session.UNATTRIBUTED_SPEAKER == "S00"` and
  `submit_unlabeled_canonical` present (J2); `transcription_outcome.TransientTranscriptionError` and
  `live_coordinator.DECODE_ATTEMPTS_PER_SPAN == 2` (J3); `live_session.CanonicalSubmission` refusing
  to build a silent refusal (J4). Then (d), the amendment's actual gate: both probes.
- Blocker (unchanged): m4mbp offline, so "one exact SHA everywhere" stays 3/4 even after J5c; its
  `git fetch && git checkout 6a540fe` is the one mechanical step left and needs no rebuild.
- Blocker (unchanged): E3's TCC clicks stay unspent until J5c/J5d are green - the same rule that has
